8930 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from a military expedition, pilgrimage, or ʿumrah, he would begin by proclaiming Allah’s greatness three times and then say:
“There is no deity except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all sovereignty and praise, and He has power over all things. We return repentant, worshipping, prostrating, and praising our Lord. Allah fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the Confederates by Himself.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4116) and Muslim (1344/428).

8931 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory by the east wind, while ʿĀd were destroyed by the west wind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4105) and Muslim (900/17).
Al-ṣabā is the east wind, while al-dabūr is the west wind.

8932 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory by the east wind, while ʿĀd were destroyed by the west wind.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Awsaṭ (7837) and Al-Ṣaghīr (1069).

8933 — Sulaymān ibn Ṣurad reported: I heard the Prophet ﷺ say when the Confederates had been driven away from him:
“Now we shall campaign against them, and they will not campaign against us. We shall march toward them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4110).
“When the Confederates had been driven away” means that they withdrew from him.
This was one of the signs of his prophethood. In the following year, he ﷺ set out to perform ʿumrah, but Quraysh prevented him from reaching the Sacred House. A truce was then concluded between them. When Quraysh later violated it, that became the cause of the conquest of Makkah. Events thus unfolded exactly as he ﷺ had foretold. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/405).

8934 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: On the day of the Confederates, after they had assembled vast forces against him, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“They will never campaign against you after this; rather, we shall campaign against them.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1810).

8935 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Saʿd was wounded on the day of the Trench. A man of Quraysh named Ḥibbān ibn al-ʿAriqah shot him in the medial vein of his arm. The Prophet ﷺ had a tent erected for him inside the mosque so that he could visit him nearby. His wound continued discharging blood, and he eventually died from it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4122) and Muslim (1769).
Mūsā ibn ʿUqbah related from Ibn Shihāb that among the Anṣār martyred on the day of the Trench was Anas ibn Muʿādh ibn Aws ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr. It was narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Kabīr (1/238) and is a mursal report.
The authors of the biographies and campaign histories also listed the following among those martyred at the Trench: ʿAbdullāh ibn Sahl al-Ashhalī; Thaʿlabah ibn Ghanamah ibn ʿAdī al-Anṣārī al-Khazrajī; Ṭufayl ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Anṣārī; Kaʿb ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī al-Najjārī; Sulayṭ ibn ʿAwf al-Aslamī; Sufyān ibn ʿAwf al-Aslamī; and Sinān ibn Ṣayfī al-Khazrajī. There is disagreement concerning some of these names.

8936 — ʿĀʾishah reported: When the Prophet ﷺ returned from the Trench, laid down his weapons, and bathed, Gabriel came to him and said: “Have you laid down your weapons? By Allah, we have not laid ours down! March against them.” The Prophet ﷺ asked: “Where?” Gabriel replied, “Over there,” pointing toward Banū Qurayẓah. The Prophet ﷺ therefore set out against them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4117) and Muslim in al-Jihād (1769), both through Ibn Numayr, from Hishām, from his father, from ʿĀʾishah; the wording is al-Bukhārī’s. It will appear later in fuller form.

8937 — Anas reported: It is as though I can still see the dust rising through the street of Banū Ghanm from Gabriel’s procession when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched against Banū Qurayẓah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4118), from Mūsā, from Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim, from Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, from Anas.

8938 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from pursuing the Confederates, laid down his armor, and perfumed himself with incense—as Duḥaym added in his narration—the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Gabriel descended and said: ‘What excuse can there be for laying aside battle? I see that you have removed your armor, while we have not yet laid ours down.’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sprang up in alarm and firmly instructed the people not to perform the afternoon prayer until they reached Banū Qurayẓah. They put on their weapons and departed, but did not reach Banū Qurayẓah until after sunset.
The people differed regarding the afternoon prayer. Some said: “Perform the prayer, for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not intend that you should omit it.” Others said: “He firmly instructed us not to pray until we reached Banū Qurayẓah. We are carrying out the Messenger of Allah’s explicit command, so there is no sin upon us.”
One group performed the afternoon prayer on time, acting in faith and seeking Allah’s reward. The other group did not pray until they reached Banū Qurayẓah after sunset, likewise acting in faith and seeking Allah’s reward. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not reprimand either group.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (19/79–80) through Ibrāhīm ibn Duḥaym al-Dimashqī and al-Ḥasan ibn Isḥāq, from al-Walīd ibn Muslim, from Marzūq ibn Abī al-Hudhayl, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Kaʿb, from his uncle ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Kaʿb, from Kaʿb ibn Mālik.

8939 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: On the day of the Confederates, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“None of you should perform the afternoon prayer until he reaches Banū Qurayẓah.”
The time of the afternoon prayer reached some of them while they were still traveling. Some said, “We shall not pray until we reach Banū Qurayẓah.” Others said, “We shall pray now; the Prophet ﷺ did not intend that we delay the prayer itself.” The matter was later mentioned to the Prophet ﷺ, and he did not reprimand either group.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4119) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1770/69), both from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Asmāʾ al-Ḍubaʿī, from Juwayriyah ibn Asmāʾ, from Nāfiʿ, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar.
Their number was three thousand men accompanied by thirty-six horses, as mentioned by Ibn Saʿd (3/74).
Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ besieged them for twenty-five nights until the siege exhausted them and Allah cast terror into their hearts. The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/235).

8940 — Al-Barāʾ reported: The Prophet ﷺ said to Ḥassān:
“Satirize them—or engage them in poetic combat—for Gabriel is with you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4123) and Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2486/153), both through Shuʿbah, from ʿAdī ibn Thābit, from al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib.
Al-Bukhārī’s narration states that the Prophet ﷺ said this on the day of Banū Qurayẓah.

8941 — Abū Saʿīd reported: Banū Qurayẓah agreed to submit to the judgment of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh. The Prophet ﷺ sent for Saʿd, who arrived riding a donkey. When he drew near the mosque, the Prophet ﷺ said to the Anṣār: “Rise to receive your leader—or the best among you.” He then said to Saʿd: “These people have agreed to submit to your judgment.” Saʿd said: “Their fighting men are to be killed and their women and children taken captive.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “You have judged according to Allah’s judgment.” On another occasion, the narrator said: “According to the judgment of the Sovereign.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4121) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1768/64), both from Muḥammad ibn Bashshār, from Ghundar (Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar), from Shuʿbah, from Saʿd ibn Ibrāhīm, from Abū Umāmah, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī.

8942 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, reported: Saʿd was wounded on the day of the Trench. A man of Quraysh named Ḥibbān ibn al-ʿAriqah shot him in the medial vein of his arm. The Prophet ﷺ had a tent erected for him inside the mosque so that he could visit him nearby.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from the Trench, he laid down his weapons and bathed. Gabriel came to him, shaking dust from his head, and said: “Have you laid down your weapons? By Allah, I have not laid mine down! March against them.” The Prophet ﷺ asked: “Where?” Gabriel pointed toward Banū Qurayẓah.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to them, and they agreed to submit to his judgment. He referred the judgment to Saʿd, who said: “My judgment is that their fighting men be killed, their women and children taken captive, and their property divided.”
Hishām said: My father related to me from ʿĀʾishah that Saʿd prayed: “O Allah, You know that there are no people whom I would rather strive against for Your sake than those who rejected Your Messenger ﷺ and drove him out. O Allah, I believe that You have brought the warfare between us and them to an end. If any fighting against Quraysh remains, preserve me so that I may strive against them for Your sake. But if You have ended the war, cause my wound to burst and make my death result from it.”
The wound burst open near his upper chest. The people in a tent belonging to Banū Ghifār inside the mosque were suddenly startled by blood flowing toward them. They asked, “People of the tent, what is this coming toward us from your direction?” They discovered that Saʿd’s wound was pouring blood, and he died from it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4122) and Muslim (1769/65).
Muslim added the statement of ʿUrwah: I was informed that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “You have judged them according to the judgment of Allah Almighty.”
Muslim also added (1769/68), through another route from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah with this chain: His wound burst open that night and continued bleeding until he died.

8943 — ʿĀʾishah reported: On the day of the Trench, I went out following the people’s tracks. I heard the heavy sound of the ground behind me—that is, the sound of someone walking. I turned and saw Saʿd ibn Muʿādh accompanied by his nephew al-Ḥārith ibn Aws, who was carrying Saʿd’s shield.
I sat down upon the ground. Saʿd passed wearing an iron coat of armor from which his limbs protruded, and I feared that his exposed limbs might be struck. Saʿd was among the largest and tallest of men. As he passed, he chanted:
Wait but a little—Ḥamal will reach the raging battle;
 how beautiful death is when the appointed time has come.
I rose and entered an enclosed garden. A group of Muslims was inside, including ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and a man wearing a helmet that covered his face. ʿUmar asked: “What brought you here? By my life, you are certainly bold! What makes you feel safe from a calamity or a forced retreat?”
He continued reproaching me until I wished that the ground would split open at that moment and swallow me. The man lifted the helmet from his face, and it was Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbaydullāh. He said: “ʿUmar, you have gone too far today! Where can anyone seek refuge or flee except to Allah Almighty?”
A polytheist of Quraysh known as Ibn al-ʿAriqah shot an arrow at Saʿd, saying, “Take that! I am Ibn al-ʿAriqah.” It struck and severed the medial vein in Saʿd’s arm.
Saʿd prayed: “O Allah, do not cause me to die until You grant me satisfaction concerning Banū Qurayẓah.” They had been his allies and confederates during the pre-Islamic period.
His wound stopped bleeding. Allah Almighty sent the wind against the polytheists and spared the believers from fighting; Allah is All-Strong, Almighty. Abū Sufyān and those with him withdrew toward Tihāmah, while ʿUyaynah ibn Badr and his forces returned to Najd. Banū Qurayẓah returned and fortified themselves inside their strongholds.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned to Madinah and laid down his weapons. He ordered that a leather tent be erected for Saʿd inside the mosque.
Gabriel came with dust visible upon his front teeth and said: “Have you laid down your weapons? By Allah, the angels have not yet laid down theirs! March against Banū Qurayẓah and fight them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ put on his armor and announced to the people that they were to depart. He set out and passed by Banū Ghanm, who lived around the mosque. He asked: “Who passed by you?” They replied, “Diḥyah al-Kalbī passed us.” Gabriel resembled Diḥyah al-Kalbī in his beard, teeth, and facial appearance.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached Banū Qurayẓah and besieged them for twenty-five nights. When the siege and hardship became severe, they were told: “Submit to the judgment of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” They consulted Abū Lubābah ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir, who gestured toward his throat to indicate slaughter.
They said, “We shall submit to the judgment of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ told them: “Then submit to the judgment of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh,” and they did so.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for Saʿd ibn Muʿādh. He was brought riding a donkey fitted with a saddle made of palm fiber, having been helped onto it. His people surrounded him and said: “Abū ʿAmr, consider your allies and confederates—people who have fought alongside you and whose position you know.”
He gave them no answer and did not turn toward them. When he approached the dwellings of Banū Qurayẓah, he turned to his people and said: “The time has come for me not to care, for Allah’s sake, about the criticism of any critic.”
Abū Saʿīd said: When Saʿd came into view, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Rise to receive your leader and help him dismount.” ʿUmar said, “Our Master is Allah Almighty.” The Prophet ﷺ repeated: “Help him dismount,” so they did.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Pass judgment concerning them.” Saʿd replied: “My judgment is that their fighting men be killed, their women and children taken captive, and their property divided.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “You have judged according to the judgment of Allah Almighty and the judgment of His Messenger.”
Saʿd then prayed: “O Allah, if any warfare against Quraysh remains for Your Prophet ﷺ, preserve me for it. But if You have ended the war between him and them, then take me to Yourself.”
His wound burst open after having healed until only a ring-like scar could be seen. He was returned to the tent that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had erected for him.
ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Abū Bakr, and ʿUmar were present with him. By Him in whose hand is Muhammad’s soul, I could distinguish ʿUmar’s weeping from Abū Bakr’s while I was inside my apartment. They were as Allah Almighty described them:
“Compassionate among themselves.”
 [Al-Fatḥ 48:29]
ʿAlqamah said: I asked, “Mother, what would the Messenger of Allah ﷺ do?” She replied: “His eyes would not shed tears over anyone, but when grief affected him, he would grasp his beard.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (25097), Ibn Saʿd (3/421), and Ibn Ḥibbān (7028).

8944 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: Saʿd ibn Muʿādh was shot in the medial vein of his arm. The Prophet ﷺ cauterized it with his own hand using a broad-headed arrowhead. When the arm swelled, he cauterized it a second time.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2208/75).

8945 — Jābir reported: On the day of the Confederates, Saʿd ibn Muʿādh was shot and the medial vein of his arm—or another major vein—was severed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ cauterized it with fire. His arm swelled, so he left it untreated for a time, but when the bleeding resumed, he cauterized it again, and the arm swelled once more.
When Saʿd saw this, he prayed: “O Allah, do not take my soul until You grant me satisfaction concerning Banū Qurayẓah.” His vein then closed and did not release another drop until they submitted to the judgment of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh.
The Prophet ﷺ sent for him. Saʿd judged that their men should be killed and their women kept alive so that the Muslims could benefit from their service. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “You have reached Allah’s judgment concerning them.”
They numbered four hundred. When their execution was completed, Saʿd’s vein opened again and he died.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1582), Aḥmad (14773), and Ibn Ḥibbān (4784).

8946 — ʿAṭiyyah al-Quraẓī reported: I was among the captives of Banū Qurayẓah. They examined the captives: anyone whose pubic hair had grown was killed, while anyone whose hair had not grown was not killed. I was among those whose hair had not grown.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4404, 4405), al-Tirmidhī (1584), and al-Nasāʾī (3430, 4981).

8947 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Only one of their women—meaning the women of Banū Qurayẓah—was killed. She was sitting with me, speaking and laughing heartily, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was having their men put to the sword. Then someone called out her name: “Where is so-and-so?” She replied, “Here I am.”
I asked, “What is the matter with you?” She replied, “It is because of something I did.” She was taken away and beheaded. I shall never forget my astonishment at her: she was laughing heartily despite knowing that she was going to be killed.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2671) and Aḥmad (26364); authenticated by al-Ḥākim (3/35–36).

8948 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ fought Banū al-Naḍīr and Banū Qurayẓah. He expelled Banū al-Naḍīr but allowed Qurayẓah to remain and showed them favor. Later, when Qurayẓah fought, he had their men killed and divided their women, children, and property among the Muslims. Some of them, however, joined the Prophet ﷺ, who granted them safety, and they embraced Islam.
He expelled all the Jews of Madinah: Banū Qaynuqāʿ—the clan of ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām—the Jews of Banū Ḥārithah, and all the other Jews of Madinah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4028) and Muslim (1766).

8949 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: A man would assign some palm trees from his land to the Prophet ﷺ. When the lands of Qurayẓah and al-Naḍīr were conquered for him, the Prophet ﷺ began returning to each person what he had given.
Anas said: My family instructed me to go to the Prophet ﷺ and ask him to return what they had given him, or part of it. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ had given it to Umm Ayman. I went to the Prophet ﷺ, and he gave the palm trees back to me.
Umm Ayman came, placed a cloth around my neck, and said: “No, by Allah! We shall not give them to you after he has given them to me.” The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “Umm Ayman, let him go, and you shall receive such-and-such.” She replied: “No, by Him besides whom there is no deity!”
He continued increasing his offer until he gave her ten times their value, or nearly ten times their value.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4120) and Muslim (1771/71).

8950 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent a cavalry detachment toward Najd. They returned with a man from Banū Ḥanīfah named Thumāmah ibn Uthāl and tied him to one of the pillars of the mosque.
The Prophet ﷺ came to him and asked: “What do you have to say, Thumāmah?” He replied: “I have good to say, Muhammad. If you kill me, you will kill a man whose blood will be avenged. If you show me favor, you will show favor to one who is grateful. If you desire wealth, ask for whatever amount you wish.”
The Prophet ﷺ left him until the following day, then asked: “What do you have to say, Thumāmah?” He replied: “What I told you: if you show me favor, you will show favor to one who is grateful.” He left him until the next day and again asked: “What do you have to say, Thumāmah?” He replied: “I have what I already told you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Release Thumāmah.” Thumāmah went to a grove near the mosque and bathed. He then entered the mosque and declared: “I testify that there is no deity except Allah, and I testify that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.”
He continued: “Muhammad, by Allah, there was no face upon the earth more hateful to me than yours, but now your face has become the most beloved of all faces to me. By Allah, no religion was more hateful to me than your religion, but now your religion has become the most beloved religion to me. By Allah, no land was more hateful to me than your land, but now your land has become the most beloved land to me.”
“Your cavalry captured me while I was intending to perform ʿumrah. What do you advise?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave him glad tidings and instructed him to perform his ʿumrah.
When he reached Makkah, someone asked him, “Have you abandoned your religion?” He replied: “No, but I have embraced Islam with Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By Allah, not a single grain of wheat will come to you from al-Yamāmah until the Prophet ﷺ permits it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4372) and Muslim in al-Jihād (1764/59), both through al-Layth, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Saʿīd, who heard Abū Hurayrah.

8951 — Abū ʿAyyāsh al-Zuraqī reported: We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at ʿUsfān when the polytheists confronted us under the command of Khālid ibn al-Walīd. They were positioned between us and the direction of prayer. The Prophet ﷺ led us in the noon prayer.
They said: “They were in a position in which we could have taken them by surprise.” They then said: “A prayer is now approaching that is dearer to them than their children and their own lives.” Gabriel descended between the noon and afternoon prayers with the verses beginning:
“When you are among them and lead them in prayer…”
 [Al-Nisāʾ 4:102]
When the afternoon prayer arrived, the Prophet ﷺ instructed them to take up their weapons. We formed two rows behind him. He bowed and we all bowed; he rose and we all rose.
The Prophet ﷺ prostrated together with the row immediately behind him, while the other row remained standing on guard. When the first row had prostrated and stood, the other row prostrated in its place. The two rows then exchanged positions.
The Prophet ﷺ then bowed and they all bowed; he rose and they all rose. He prostrated with the row directly behind him while the others remained standing on guard. When he sat, the others sat and prostrated. He then pronounced the salutation to all of them and completed the prayer.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the fear prayer in this manner twice: once at ʿUsfān and once in the territory of Banū Sulaym.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1236) and Aḥmad (16580), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2876) and al-Ḥākim (1/337–338), all through Manṣūr ibn al-Muʿtamir, from Mujāhid, from Abū ʿAyyāsh al-Zuraqī.

8952 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ encamped between Ḍajnān and ʿUsfān. The polytheists said: “These people have a prayer that is dearer to them than their fathers and children—the afternoon prayer. Unite upon your plan and attack them in one concerted assault.”
Gabriel came to the Prophet ﷺ and instructed him to divide his Companions into two groups. He was to pray with one group while the other stood behind them on guard, taking their precautions and carrying their weapons. The second group would then come and pray with him while the first took its precautions and weapons. In this way, each group would perform one unit with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while he would perform two units.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3035), al-Nasāʾī (1544), and Aḥmad (10765), through ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAbd al-Wārith, from Saʿīd ibn ʿUbayd al-Hannāʾī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shaqīq, from Abū Hurayrah.

8953 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent an expedition toward the coast, appointing Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ as its commander. They numbered three hundred men, and I was among them.
We set out, but while we were partway through the journey, our provisions ran out. Abū ʿUbaydah ordered that all the army’s remaining provisions be gathered together. They amounted to two provision bags of dates. He rationed them to us little by little each day until they were exhausted, and eventually each man received only one date.
I asked, “What benefit could a single date provide?” Jābir replied: “We certainly felt its loss when even that ran out.”
We eventually reached the sea and found an enormous fish resembling a hill. The army ate from it for eighteen nights. Abū ʿUbaydah ordered that two of its ribs be erected, then had a camel saddled and led beneath them without touching them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (23), al-Bukhārī (4360), and Muslim (1935/21).

8954 — Jābir reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us to intercept a caravan of Quraysh and appointed Abū ʿUbaydah as our commander. He supplied us with one leather bag of dates, having nothing else to give us. Abū ʿUbaydah distributed them one date at a time.
I asked, “What did you do with a single date?” He replied: “We sucked it as a child sucks something, then drank water afterward. It sustained us from morning until night. We would also beat the leaves from the trees with our staffs, soak them in water, and eat them.”
We continued along the seacoast until something resembling an enormous sandhill appeared before us on the shore. When we approached it, we found that it was a sea creature called al-ʿAnbar.
Abū ʿUbaydah initially said: “It is carrion.” He then said: “No; we are the messengers of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, traveling in Allah’s cause, and necessity has overtaken you, so eat.”
We remained there for a month, although we numbered three hundred, until we regained our weight. I saw us scooping fat from its eye socket with large pitchers and cutting pieces of its flesh as large as an ox.
Abū ʿUbaydah seated thirteen of our men inside its eye socket. He erected one of its ribs, then had the largest camel with us saddled and led underneath it.
We took dried strips of its flesh as provisions. When we returned to Madinah, we went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and told him what had happened. He said: “It was provision that Allah brought forth for you. Do you have any of its flesh with you so that you may feed us?” We sent some of it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he ate it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1935/17) and al-Bukhārī (4362).

8955 — ʿAmr ibn Dīnār reported: I heard Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh say: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent three hundred mounted men under the command of Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ to watch for a caravan of Quraysh.
We remained on the coast for half a month and suffered such severe hunger that we ate leaves beaten from the trees. The expedition therefore became known as the Army of al-Khabaṭ.
The sea cast out for us a creature called al-ʿAnbar. We ate from it for half a month and rubbed its fat upon our bodies until our physical strength and fullness returned.
Abū ʿUbaydah took one of its ribs and erected it. He then selected the tallest man with him—Sufyān once narrated: “He erected a bone from its body”—and had the man ride a camel beneath it.
Jābir said: One of the men slaughtered three camels for the army, then another three, and then another three. Abū ʿUbaydah subsequently prohibited him from slaughtering more.
ʿAmr would also relate: Abū Ṣāliḥ informed us that Qays ibn Saʿd told his father: “I was with the army when they became hungry.” His father said, “Slaughter a camel.” Qays said, “I did.” He said, “Then they became hungry again.” His father said, “Slaughter another.” He replied, “I did.” He said, “Then they became hungry again.” His father said, “Slaughter another.” He replied, “I did.” He said, “Then they became hungry once more.” His father said, “Slaughter another.” Qays replied, “I was prohibited from doing so.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4361), whose wording this is, and Muslim (1935/18).

8956 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported in a lengthy hadith that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf to prepare for an expedition over which he had appointed him commander. He came in the morning wearing a turban made of black cotton cloth.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ drew him close, unwrapped the turban, then wrapped it around his head himself, allowing approximately four finger-widths of it to hang down behind him. He said: “This is how you should wear your turban, Ibn ʿAwf, for it is more distinctive and more becoming.”
He then instructed Bilāl to give him the banner. Bilāl handed it to him, and the Prophet ﷺ praised Allah and invoked blessings upon His Prophet. He then said: “Take it, Ibn ʿAwf. Campaign together in Allah’s cause and fight those who disbelieve in Allah. Do not steal from the spoils, betray anyone, mutilate the dead, or kill a child. This is Allah’s covenant and the way of your Prophet among you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (6175), al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Awsaṭ (4668), and al-Ḥākim (4/540).

8957 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: Some men from ʿUkl and ʿUraynah came to the Prophet ﷺ in Madinah and professed Islam. They said: “Prophet of Allah, we are people accustomed to livestock, not agricultural settlements.” They found the climate of Madinah unhealthy.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ provided them with a herd of camels and a shepherd, instructing them to go out with the herd and drink from its milk and urine.
They departed, but when they reached the region of al-Ḥarrah, they renounced Islam, killed the Prophet’s shepherd, and drove away the camels.
When the Prophet ﷺ learned of this, he sent men in pursuit. After they were captured, he ordered that their eyes be seared and their hands cut off. They were left in the region of al-Ḥarrah until they died in that state.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4192), whose wording this is, and Muslim (1671/14).

8958 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed four ʿumrahs, all of them in Dhū al-Qaʿdah except the one performed together with his pilgrimage: the ʿumrah of al-Ḥudaybiyyah in Dhū al-Qaʿdah; the ʿumrah of the following year in Dhū al-Qaʿdah; the ʿumrah from al-Jiʿrānah, where he distributed the spoils of Ḥunayn, in Dhū al-Qaʿdah; and the ʿumrah performed with his pilgrimage.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4148) and Muslim in al-Ḥajj (1253/217), both from Hudbah—or Hudāb—ibn Khālid, from Hammām, from Qatādah, from Anas.

8959 — Marwān and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah reported: In the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, the Prophet ﷺ set out with between thirteen and fifteen hundred of his Companions. When he reached Dhū al-Ḥulayfah, he placed identifying garlands upon the sacrificial animals, marked them, and entered the state of iḥrām from there.
The narrator said: I cannot count how many times I heard it from Sufyān until I heard him say: “I do not reliably remember the words concerning marking and garlanding the sacrificial animals from al-Zuhrī.” The narrator was unsure whether Sufyān meant only the passage concerning marking and garlanding or the entire hadith.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4157, 4158).

8960 — Nāfiʿ reported: During the period of civil strife, ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar set out intending to perform ʿumrah. He said: “If I am prevented from reaching the Sacred House, we shall do as we did with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” He therefore entered iḥrām for ʿumrah because the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had entered iḥrām for ʿumrah in the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (103), al-Bukhārī (4183), and Muslim (1230/180).

8961 — Nāfiʿ reported that ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh and Sālim ibn ʿAbd Allāh told him that they spoke to ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar and said, “If only you would remain here this year, for we fear that you may not reach the Sacred House.” He replied, “We set out with the Prophet ﷺ, but the disbelievers of Quraysh prevented him from reaching the House. The Prophet ﷺ therefore slaughtered his sacrificial animals and shaved his head, while his Companions shortened their hair.”
He then said, “I call you to witness that I have entered into ʿumrah. If I am allowed to reach the House, I shall circumambulate it; but if I am prevented from reaching it, I shall do as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did.” After travelling for a while, he said, “I consider the matter of the two rites to be the same. I call you to witness that I have entered into ḥajj together with my ʿumrah.” He performed one circumambulation and one saʿy, and did not leave the state of consecration until he had completed both rites.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4185) and Muslim (1230:181).

8962 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Whoever ascends the mountain pass—the Pass of al-Marār—will have his sins removed as they were removed from the Children of Israel.” He said: “The first to ascend it were our horsemen, the horsemen of Banū al-Khazraj, and then the people followed one after another.”
Authentic: Related by Muslim (2880:12).
Ibn Shihāb said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed the people, saying: “Take the road to the right, through the middle of al-Ḥumush, along a route that will bring you out at the Pass of al-Marār, descending toward al-Ḥudaybiyyah from below Makkah.” Ibn Isḥāq mentioned it, as recorded in Ibn Hishām’s Sīrah (2/310). This route was taken to avoid the polytheists’ cavalry.

8963 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Whoever climbs the Pass of al-Marār—or al-Marār…” He then related a report similar to the narration of Muʿādh, except that he said: “There was a Bedouin who had come looking for a lost animal.”
Authentic: Related by Muslim (2880:13).

8964 — Abū Saʿīd reported: We set out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When we reached ʿUsfān, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us: “The scouts of Quraysh are now at Ḍajnān. Which of you knows the road of Dhāt al-Ḥanẓal?”
When evening came, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Is there a man who will dismount and go ahead of the mounted party?” A man said, “I will, Messenger of Allah.” He dismounted, but the stones kept striking him and the branches caught his clothing, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Mount again.” Another man then dismounted, but the stones struck him and the branches caught his clothing, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told him: “Mount again.”
We eventually found the road and travelled through a pass called al-Ḥanẓal. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The likeness of this pass is that of the gate through which the Children of Israel entered. They were told: ‘Enter the gate prostrating and say, “Relieve us of our burdens,” and We shall forgive you your sins’ [al-Baqarah 2:58]. No one will cross this pass tonight without being forgiven.”
The people began hurrying across it. The last to cross was Qatādah ibn al-Nuʿmān, who was at the rear of the company. The people crowded upon one another until we had all caught up. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then dismounted, and we dismounted.
Good: Related by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1812).

8965 — Zayd ibn Khālid reported: We accompanied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, and rain fell upon us one night. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in the dawn prayer and then turned toward us and said: “Do you know what your Lord has said?” We replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said: “Allah said: ‘This morning some of My servants became believers in Me and others became disbelievers in Me. Whoever said, “We were given rain through Allah’s mercy, provision and grace,” is a believer in Me and a disbeliever in the stars. But whoever said, “We were given rain because of such-and-such a star,” is a believer in the stars and a disbeliever in Me.’”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4147) and Muslim (71:125).

8966 — Al-Barāʾ said: You consider the conquest to be the conquest of Makkah. The conquest of Makkah was indeed a conquest, but we considered the Pledge of al-Riḍwān on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah to be the conquest.
We were fourteen hundred men with the Prophet ﷺ. Al-Ḥudaybiyyah had a well, and we drew out all its water until not a single drop remained. When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he came to the well and sat at its edge. He called for a vessel of water, performed ablution, rinsed his mouth, supplicated, and then poured the water into the well. We left it for a short while, after which it supplied all the water that we and our riding animals required.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4150).

8967 — Mirdās al-Aslamī reported that the Prophet ﷺ said: “The righteous will depart, one after another, and there will remain only dregs like the husks of barley or the refuse of dates. Allah will give them no consideration.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (6434). A narration in al-Bukhārī (4156) states that Mirdās was one of the Companions of the Tree.

8968 — Zayd ibn Aslam reported from his father: I went with ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, may Allah be pleased with him, to the marketplace. A young woman caught up with him and said, “Commander of the Faithful, my husband has died and left behind small children. By Allah, they cannot even cook a sheep’s trotter, and they possess neither crops nor milk-producing animals. I fear that a hyena may devour them. I am the daughter of Khafāf ibn Īmāʾ al-Ghifārī, and my father witnessed al-Ḥudaybiyyah with the Prophet ﷺ.”
ʿUmar stopped and went no farther. He said, “Welcome to one of close lineage.” He then went to a strong pack camel that was tethered in the house, loaded it with two sacks filled with food, and placed money and clothing between them. He handed her its halter and said, “Lead it away. This provision will not be exhausted before Allah brings you relief.”
A man said, “Commander of the Faithful, you have given her a great deal.” ʿUmar replied, “May your mother lose you! By Allah, I can still picture this woman’s father and brother besieging a fortress for a long time until they conquered it, and afterward we benefited from the shares they had earned in it.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4160, 4161).

8969 — Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: I set out to perform the pilgrimage and passed by some people who were praying. I asked, “What is this place of prayer?” They replied, “This is the tree beneath which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ received the Pledge of al-Riḍwān.”
I went to Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib and told him about it. Saʿīd said: “My father told me that he was among those who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ beneath the tree. When we returned the following year, however, we had forgotten its location and could not identify it.” Saʿīd then remarked: “The Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ did not know where it was, but you know it—you must therefore know better!”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4163) and Muslim (1859:77).

8970 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā, who was one of the Companions of the Tree, reported: Whenever people brought their charitable contributions to the Prophet ﷺ, he would say: “O Allah, bestow Your blessings upon them.” When my father brought him his charitable contribution, he said: “O Allah, bestow Your blessings upon the family of Abū Awfā.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4166) and Muslim (1078:176).

8971 — Al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Musayyib reported from his father: I met al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib, may Allah be pleased with him, and said, “Blessed are you! You accompanied the Prophet ﷺ and pledged allegiance to him beneath the tree.” He replied, “My nephew, you do not know what we introduced after him.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4170).

8972 — Abū Qilābah reported that Thābit ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk told him that he had pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ beneath the tree. The remainder of the report follows.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4171) and Muslim (110:176).

8973 — Zāhir al-Aslamī, who was among those who witnessed the Pledge beneath the tree, said: I was tending the fire beneath a cooking pot containing donkey meat when the herald of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ announced: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ forbids you to eat donkey meat.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4173). His words, “I was tending the fire beneath a cooking pot containing donkey meat,” refer to the day of Khaybar.

8974 — Suwayd ibn al-Nuʿmān, who was one of the Companions of the Tree, reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions were brought sawīq—a preparation of roasted grain—and they chewed and ate it.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4175).

8975 — Abū Jamrah said: I asked ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿAmr, may Allah be pleased with him, who was one of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ and one of the Companions of the Tree, “Should the witr prayer be repeated?” He replied, “If you have performed witr at the beginning of the night, do not perform another witr at its end.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4176).

8976 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported from al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam, each adding details to the other’s account, that they said:
The Prophet ﷺ set out during the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah with more than fourteen hundred of his Companions. When he reached Dhū al-Ḥulayfah, he garlanded and marked the sacrificial animals and entered into the state of consecration for ʿumrah. He also sent a scout from the tribe of Khuzāʿah.
The Prophet ﷺ continued until he reached the pond of al-Ashṭāṭ, where his scout came to him and said, “Quraysh have assembled their forces against you and gathered the allied tribes. They intend to fight you, turn you away from the Sacred House and prevent you from reaching it.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Advise me, people. Do you think that I should turn against the families and children of those who intend to prevent us from reaching the House? If they come to confront us, Allah Almighty will have cut off a force from among the polytheists; and if they do not come, we shall leave them stripped and ruined.”
Abū Bakr said, “Messenger of Allah, you set out intending to reach this House. You do not desire to kill or fight anyone. Proceed toward it, and we shall fight whoever prevents us from reaching it.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “Proceed in the name of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4178, 4179), from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad, from Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah, who heard al-Zuhrī relate the report; Maʿmar confirmed what he had retained, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, from al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam.
The words “a scout from Khuzāʿah” refer to an intelligence-gatherer. His name was Bishr ibn Sufyān al-Khuzāʿī.
“The pond of al-Ashṭāṭ” was a place near ʿUsfān, two stages from Makkah on the road to Madinah.

8977 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported that on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah the Prophet ﷺ said: “Do not light a fire at night.” Later, he said: “Light your fires and prepare what you need, for no people who come after you will equal even your ṣāʿ or your mudd.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (11208) and Abū Yaʿlā (984), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (3/36), all through Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd al-Qaṭṭān, from Muḥammad ibn Abī Yaḥyā, from his father, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī.

8978 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We arrived at al-Ḥudaybiyyah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and we numbered fourteen hundred. Its well contained so little water that it could not provide enough for fifty sheep. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat at the edge of the well and either supplicated or spat into it. The water then surged forth, so we drank and drew as much as we needed.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Jihād (1807:132) through several routes from ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Iyās ibn Salamah, from his father, in a longer hadith.
“Jabā al-rakiyyah” means the area surrounding the well, and rakiyy means a well.

8979 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān reported: The people complained to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ of thirst. He took an arrow from his quiver and instructed them to place it in the well. By Allah, it continued surging with abundant water for them until they had all finished and departed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Shurūṭ (2732, 2731), from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad, from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, from al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān, each confirming the other’s report, in the longer narration.

8980 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported that they were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, numbering fourteen hundred or more. They stopped beside a well and drew out all its water. They went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who came to the well, sat at its edge and said: “Bring me a bucket of its water.”
It was brought to him, and he spat into it and supplicated. He then said: “Leave it for a while.” Thereafter, they fully quenched themselves and their riding animals before departing.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4151), from Faḍl ibn Yaʿqūb, from al-Ḥasan ibn Muḥammad ibn Aʿyan Abū ʿAlī al-Ḥarrānī, from Zuhayr, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib.

8981 — Al-Barāʾ reported: We were fourteen hundred men with the Prophet ﷺ. Al-Ḥudaybiyyah had a well, and we drew out all its water until we left not a single drop in it. When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he came to the well and sat at its edge. He called for a vessel of water, performed ablution, rinsed his mouth and supplicated, and then poured the water into it. We left it for a short while, after which it supplied all the water that we and our riding animals required.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4150), from ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Mūsā, from Isrāʾīl, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ.

8982 — Jābir reported: The people became thirsty on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a small leather vessel of water before him, from which he performed ablution. The people then came toward him, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “What is the matter with you?”
They replied, “Messenger of Allah, we have no water with which to perform ablution or drink except what is in your vessel.” The Prophet ﷺ placed his hand in the vessel, and water began gushing from between his fingers like springs. We drank and performed ablution.
I asked Jābir, “How many were you that day?” He replied, “Had we been one hundred thousand, it would have sufficed us. We were fifteen hundred.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4152) and Muslim in al-Imārah (1856:73), both through Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Jābir.

8983 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: The people’s provisions became scarce and they fell into poverty, so they came to the Prophet ﷺ seeking permission to slaughter their camels, and he permitted them to do so. ʿUmar met them and they informed him, whereupon he said, “How will you survive after your camels are gone?”
He went to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “Messenger of Allah, how will they survive after their camels are gone?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Call upon the people to bring whatever remains of their provisions.”
A leather sheet was spread out, and the provisions were placed upon it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood, supplicated and invoked blessings over the food. He then called upon the people to bring their containers, and they took handfuls until they had all finished. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said: “I testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and that I am the Messenger of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (2484) and Muslim (1729).
The word naṭʿ means a dining sheet made of leather, or a spread.

8984 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah: “You are the best people on earth.” We numbered fourteen hundred. Had I still possessed my sight today, I would have shown you the location of the tree.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4154) and Muslim in al-Imārah (1856:71), both through Sufyān, from ʿAmr, who heard Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh.

8985 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh reported: Umm Mubashshir informed me that she heard the Prophet ﷺ say in the presence of Ḥafṣah: “Allah willing, none of the Companions of the Tree—those who pledged allegiance beneath it—will enter the Fire.”
Ḥafṣah said, “Indeed they will, Messenger of Allah.” He rebuked her, so she quoted: “There is none of you who will not pass over it” [Maryam 19:71]. The Prophet ﷺ replied: “Allah Almighty also said: ‘Then We shall save those who were mindful of Allah and leave the wrongdoers there upon their knees’ [Maryam 19:72].”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2496), from Hārūn ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Ḥajjāj ibn Muḥammad, from Ibn Jurayj, from Abū al-Zubayr, who heard Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh.

8986 — Maʿqil ibn Yasār reported: I remember myself on the day of the Tree, while the Prophet ﷺ was receiving the people’s pledges and I was holding one of its branches above his head. We numbered fourteen hundred. He said: “We did not pledge to die; rather, we pledged that we would not flee.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Imārah (1858), from Yaḥyā ibn Yaḥyā, from Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ, from Khālid, from al-Ḥakam ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Aʿraj, from Maʿqil ibn Yasār.

8987 — Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd reported: I asked Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ, “For what did you pledge allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah?” He replied, “To face death.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4169) and Muslim in al-Imārah (1860:80), both from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from Ḥātim ibn Ismāʿīl, from Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd.
Regarding the words “to face death,” some narrations state: “They pledged to remain steadfast and not flee.” Whoever described it as a pledge to face death stated its necessary consequence, whereas whoever described it as a pledge to remain steadfast expressed its literal terms.

8988 — ʿAbbād ibn Tamīm reported: On the day of al-Ḥarrah, when the people were pledging allegiance to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓalah, Ibn Zayd asked, “For what is Ibn Ḥanẓalah receiving the people’s pledge?” He was told, “To face death.” He replied, “I shall never give such a pledge to anyone after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” He had witnessed al-Ḥudaybiyyah with him.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4167) and Muslim (1861:81).
The words “on the day of al-Ḥarrah” refer to the time when the people of Madinah renounced their pledge of allegiance to Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah and pledged allegiance to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓalah al-Anṣārī.

8989 — Nāfiʿ reported: People say that Ibn ʿUmar embraced Islam before ʿUmar, but that is not so. On the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, ʿUmar sent ʿAbd Allāh to retrieve a horse of his that was being kept by a man of the Anṣār, so that he could ride it in battle. Meanwhile, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was receiving the people’s pledges beneath the tree, though ʿUmar was unaware of it.
ʿAbd Allāh pledged allegiance to him and then went to retrieve the horse. He brought it to ʿUmar, who was putting on his armour for battle, and informed him that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was receiving pledges beneath the tree. ʿUmar immediately set out with him and pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. This is the incident that people misconstrued when they said that Ibn ʿUmar embraced Islam before ʿUmar.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4186, 4187).
The expression “putting on his armour for battle” means that he was donning his laʾmah, namely his weapons and armour.

8990 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the people were with the Prophet ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah and had dispersed beneath the shade of the trees. They then gathered closely around the Prophet ﷺ.
ʿUmar said, “ʿAbd Allāh, go and see what has happened to the people, for they have gathered around the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” He found them pledging allegiance, so he gave his pledge and then returned to ʿUmar. ʿUmar subsequently went out and pledged allegiance as well.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4187).

8991 — ʿUthmān ibn Mawhab reported: A man came to perform pilgrimage at the Sacred House and saw a group of people sitting together. He asked, “Who are these people?” They replied, “They are Quraysh.” He asked, “Who is the elder among them?” They replied, “Ibn ʿUmar.”
He approached him and said, “I wish to ask you about something. Will you answer me?” He continued, “I adjure you by the sanctity of this House: do you know that ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān fled on the day of Uḥud?” Ibn ʿUmar replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Do you know that he was absent from Badr and did not witness it?” He replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Do you know that he was absent from the Pledge of al-Riḍwān and did not witness it?” He replied, “Yes.” The man exclaimed, “Allah is the Greatest!”
Ibn ʿUmar said: “Come here, and I shall explain the matters about which you asked me. As for his flight on the day of Uḥud, I testify that Allah pardoned him. As for his absence from Badr, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was his wife, and she was ill. The Prophet ﷺ therefore told him: ‘You shall receive the reward and the share of a man who witnessed Badr.’
“As for his absence from the Pledge of al-Riḍwān, had there been anyone in Makkah more respected and influential than ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, the Prophet ﷺ would have sent that person in his place. Instead, he sent ʿUthmān. The Pledge of al-Riḍwān took place after ʿUthmān had departed for Makkah. The Prophet ﷺ indicated his right hand and said: ‘This is ʿUthmān’s hand.’ He then struck it against his other hand and said: ‘This pledge is for ʿUthmān.’ Now take this explanation with you.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (3699, 4066).

8992 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We arrived at al-Ḥudaybiyyah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, numbering fourteen hundred. Its well contained so little water that it could not provide enough for fifty sheep. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat at the edge of the well and either supplicated or spat into it. The water then surged forth, so we drank and drew what we needed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then summoned us to pledge allegiance beneath the tree. I was the first person to pledge to him. He continued receiving people’s pledges until he reached the middle of the company, when he said: “Pledge to me, Salamah!” I said, “I pledged to you, Messenger of Allah, among the first people.” He said: “And again.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw that I was unarmed, so he gave me a leather shield. He continued receiving pledges until he reached the last of the people, when he said: “Will you not pledge to me, Salamah?” I replied, “I pledged to you, Messenger of Allah, among the first people and again in the middle.” He said: “And again.” I therefore pledged to him a third time.
He then asked me: “Salamah, where is the shield I gave you?” I replied, “Messenger of Allah, my uncle ʿĀmir met me while he was unarmed, so I gave it to him.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and said: “You are like the man of old who said: ‘O Allah, grant me a beloved companion who is dearer to me than my own self.’”
Authentic: Related by Muslim (1807).

8993 — Abū al-Zubayr reported that he heard Jābir being asked, “How many were you on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah?” He replied: “We were fourteen hundred. We pledged allegiance to him beneath the tree while ʿUmar held his hand. It was an acacia tree. All of us pledged except al-Jadd ibn Qays al-Anṣārī, who hid beneath his camel.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Imārah (1856:69), from Muḥammad ibn Ḥātim, from Ḥajjāj, from Ibn Jurayj, from Abū al-Zubayr.
Al-Jadd was the son of Qays ibn Ṣakhr ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd ibn ʿAdī ibn Tamīm ibn Ghanm ibn Kaʿb ibn Salimah al-Anṣārī al-Salamī. His honorific was Abū ʿAbd Allāh, and he was among those suspected of hypocrisy. Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr said at the end of his biographical entry (p. 363): “It has also been said that he repented and that his repentance was sincere.”

8994 — Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: I set out to perform pilgrimage and passed by people who were praying. I asked, “What is this place of prayer?” They replied, “This is the tree beneath which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ received the Pledge of al-Riḍwān.”
I went to Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib and informed him. Saʿīd said: “My father told me that he was among those who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ beneath the tree. When we returned the following year, however, we had forgotten its location and could not identify it.” Saʿīd then remarked: “The Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ did not know where it was, but you know it—you must therefore know better!”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4163) and Muslim (1859:77).

8995 — Ṭāriq reported: The tree was mentioned in the presence of Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, and he laughed. He then said: “My father, who had witnessed the pledge beneath it, informed me…”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4165) and Muslim (1859:78).

8996 — Nāfiʿ reported: People used to visit the tree they called the Tree of al-Riḍwān and pray beside it. When this reached ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, he threatened them with punishment and ordered that it be cut down, so it was cut down.
Authentic: Related by Ibn Saʿd in al-Ṭabaqāt (2/100).
Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib’s statement, “The Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ did not know where it was, but you know it,” expresses disapproval and sarcasm. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb had ordered the tree to be cut down, so where could it be now? Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh had known its location, but after becoming blind he was unable to point it out.

8997 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported that they were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, numbering fourteen hundred or more. They stopped beside a well and drew out all its water. They went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who came to the well, sat at its edge and said: “Bring me a bucket of its water.”
It was brought to him, and he spat into it and supplicated. He then said: “Leave it for a while.” Thereafter, they fully quenched themselves and their riding animals before departing.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4151), from Faḍl ibn Yaʿqūb, from al-Ḥasan ibn Muḥammad ibn Aʿyan Abū ʿAlī al-Ḥarrānī, from Zuhayr, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib.

8998 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We arrived at al-Ḥudaybiyyah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, numbering fourteen hundred. Its well contained so little water that it could not provide enough for fifty sheep. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat at the edge of the well and either supplicated or spat into it. The water then surged forth, so we drank and drew what we needed.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Jihād (1807:132) through several routes from ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Iyās ibn Salamah, from his father, in a longer hadith.

8999 — Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd reported from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh: I remember being with the Prophet ﷺ when the time for the afternoon prayer arrived. We had only a small amount of water, which was placed in a vessel and brought to the Prophet ﷺ. He placed his hand in it, spread his fingers and said: “Come, people of ablution! The blessing is from Allah.”
I saw water springing from between his fingers. The people performed ablution and drank. I spared no effort in filling my stomach with it, for I knew that it was blessed. I asked Jābir, “How many were you that day?” He replied, “Fourteen hundred.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (5639).

9000 — Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh reported: The people became thirsty on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a small leather vessel of water before him, from which he performed ablution. The people then came toward him, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “What is the matter with you?”
They replied, “Messenger of Allah, we have no water with which to perform ablution or drink except what is in your vessel.” The Prophet ﷺ placed his hand in the vessel, and water began gushing from between his fingers like springs. We drank and performed ablution.
I asked Jābir, “How many were you that day?” He replied, “Had we been one hundred thousand, it would have sufficed us. We were fifteen hundred.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4152) and Muslim in al-Imārah (1856:73), both through Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Jābir.
Regarding the statement, “We were fifteen hundred,” another narration transmitted by ʿAmr ibn Dīnār from Jābir says: “We were fourteen hundred.” These reports may be reconciled by understanding that they numbered more than fourteen hundred. Whoever said fifteen hundred rounded the number upward, while whoever said fourteen hundred disregarded the additional fraction.
As for ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā’s statement that they numbered thirteen hundred, this may refer to those whom he personally observed, while others knew of the additional people. Alternatively, his figure may describe those who initially departed from Madinah, with others joining them later; or it may represent the fighting men alone, with the additional number consisting of servants, women and children who had not reached puberty.

9001 — Qatādah reported: I said to Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, “I have been informed that Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh used to say that they numbered fourteen hundred.” Saʿīd replied, “Jābir told me that those who pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ numbered fifteen hundred.”
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (4153).

9002 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā reported: The Companions of the Tree numbered thirteen hundred, and the tribe of Aslam constituted one-eighth of the migrating force.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4155) and Muslim (1857:75).

9003 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out in the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah intending to visit the Sacred House, not to fight. He brought seventy sacrificial camels, while the people numbered seven hundred, so each camel was offered on behalf of ten men.
Good: Related by Aḥmad (18910).
The stronger view concerning their number is Jābir’s report that they were fifteen hundred, since the larger figure takes precedence. Reports giving a smaller number may reflect the narrator’s own observation and calculation, as an error in counting could easily occur because the people were dispersed in different places.

9004 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib reported: Two enslaved men came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah before the treaty was concluded. Their masters wrote to him, saying, “Muḥammad, by Allah, they did not come to you out of a desire for your religion; they came only to escape enslavement.”
Some people said, “They are telling the truth, Messenger of Allah. Return them to their masters.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became angry and said: “I do not think you will desist, people of Quraysh, until Allah sends against you someone who will strike your necks over this matter.” He refused to return them and said: “They are those whom Allah Almighty has set free.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2700), Ibn al-Jārūd (1093), al-Ḥākim (2/125), and through him al-Bayhaqī (9/229), by way of Muḥammad ibn Salamah, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Abān ibn Ṣāliḥ, from Manṣūr ibn al-Muʿtamir, from Ribʿī ibn Ḥirāsh, from ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

9005 — Anas reported that eighty armed men from Makkah descended from Mount al-Tanʿīm toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, seeking to take the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions by surprise. They were captured without fighting, but he spared their lives. Allah Almighty then revealed: “He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after giving you victory over them” [al-Fatḥ 48:24].
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Jihād (1808), from ʿAmr ibn Muḥammad al-Nāqid, from Yazīd ibn Hārūn, from Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from Thābit, from Anas. Aḥmad also narrated it (12227), adding at its beginning: “On the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah.”

9006 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī reported: We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at al-Ḥudaybiyyah beneath the tree mentioned by Allah in the Qur’an. Some of its branches were hanging upon the back of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Suhayl ibn ʿAmr sat before him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿAlī: “Write: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’” Suhayl ibn ʿAmr restrained his hand and said, “We do not acknowledge ‘the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’ Write in our agreement what we recognise.” He said: “Write: ‘In Your name, O Allah.’”
ʿAlī wrote: “This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, with the people of Makkah.” Suhayl again restrained his hand and said, “We would have wronged you if you were truly His Messenger. Write in our agreement what we recognise.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “Write: ‘This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib’—and I am indeed the Messenger of Allah.” So he wrote it.
While we were in that state, thirty armed young men came out against us and rushed toward us. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated against them, and Allah Almighty deprived them of their sight. We went forward and captured them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Have you come under anyone’s covenant, or has anyone granted you protection?” They replied, “No.” He nevertheless released them.
Allah Almighty then revealed: “He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after giving you victory over them. Allah sees all that you do” [al-Fatḥ 48:24].
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (16800), from Zayd ibn al-Ḥubāb, from Ḥusayn ibn Wāqid, from Thābit al-Bunānī, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī.

9007 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concluded the treaty with the people of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib wrote the agreement between them and wrote: “Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.”
The polytheists said, “Do not write ‘Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.’ Had you been a messenger, we would not have fought you.” The Prophet ﷺ told ʿAlī: “Erase it.” ʿAlī replied, “I am not the one who will erase it.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ therefore erased it with his own hand.
He concluded the treaty on the condition that he and his Companions could enter Makkah for three days and would enter carrying no weapons except those kept in their sheaths. They asked what this meant, and he replied: “The scabbard together with what it contains.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (2698) and Muslim (1783:90).

9008 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out to perform ʿumrah, but the disbelievers of Quraysh prevented him from reaching the Sacred House. He slaughtered his sacrificial animals and shaved his head at al-Ḥudaybiyyah. He concluded a treaty with them stipulating that he would perform ʿumrah the following year, carry no weapons against them other than sheathed swords, and remain in Makkah only for the period they allowed.
He performed ʿumrah the following year and entered Makkah in accordance with the treaty. After he had remained there for three days, they instructed him to leave, so he departed.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2701).

9009 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported that he heard Marwān and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah relate from the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
Among the conditions imposed by Suhayl ibn ʿAmr when he concluded the treaty that day was: “No man from among us may come to you—even if he follows your religion—without your returning him to us and allowing us to take custody of him.”
The believers disliked this condition and were deeply distressed by it, but Suhayl refused to accept anything else, so the Prophet ﷺ concluded the treaty on that basis. That day he returned Abū Jandal to his father, Suhayl ibn ʿAmr. During the treaty period, no man came to him from Quraysh without being returned, even if he was a Muslim.
Believing women, however, came as emigrants. Umm Kulthūm bint ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ was among those who came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at that time, and she was a young unmarried woman. Her family came to the Prophet ﷺ asking him to return her, but he did not do so, because Allah revealed concerning the women: “Believers, when believing women come to you as emigrants, test their faith—Allah knows their faith best…” through His words: “…nor are the disbelievers lawful husbands for them” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:10].
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2711, 2712).
The Prophet ﷺ did not return the emigrating women because the treaty concluded between him and Suhayl ibn ʿAmr did not include women. Its wording specifically stated: “No man from among us may come to you during that period without your returning him, even if he is a Muslim.”

9010 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān, each confirming the other’s account, reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out at the time of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. When they had travelled part of the way, the Prophet ﷺ said: “Khālid ibn al-Walīd is at al-Ghamīm with a cavalry unit serving as the vanguard of Quraysh, so take the road to the right.”
By Allah, Khālid did not become aware of them until he saw the dust raised by the army. He galloped away to warn Quraysh. The Prophet ﷺ continued until he reached the pass from which he would descend toward them, whereupon his she-camel knelt down. The people called, “Ḥal! Ḥal!” but she remained obstinate. They said, “Al-Qaṣwāʾ has become stubborn.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Al-Qaṣwāʾ has not become stubborn, nor is stubbornness part of her nature. Rather, she has been restrained by the One who restrained the elephant.” He then said: “By the One in whose hand is my soul, they will not propose any plan by which they seek to honour the sacred ordinances of Allah without my accepting it.” He urged the camel onward, and she rose.
He turned away from Quraysh and camped at the far end of al-Ḥudaybiyyah beside a shallow pool containing little water. The people drew from it sparingly, but they soon exhausted it. They complained to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ of thirst. He removed an arrow from his quiver and instructed them to place it in the water. By Allah, it continued surging with abundant water until they had all finished and departed from it.
While they were in this state, Budayl ibn Warqāʾ al-Khuzāʿī arrived with several men of Khuzāʿah. Among the people of Tihāmah, they were the trusted confidants and sincere advisers of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Budayl said, “I left Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy and ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy encamped beside the abundant waters of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. They have brought nursing she-camels with their young, and they intend to fight you and prevent you from reaching the Sacred House.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied: “We have not come to fight anyone; we have come only to perform ʿumrah. War has exhausted and harmed Quraysh. If they wish, I shall conclude a truce with them for a fixed period, provided they allow me to deal freely with the other people. If I prevail, they may enter the faith that the others have entered, should they wish; otherwise, they will at least have regained their strength. But if they refuse, then by the One in whose hand is my soul, I shall fight them for this cause until my neck is severed, and Allah will surely accomplish His command.”
Budayl said, “I shall convey your words to them.”
He went to Quraysh and said, “We have come to you from this man and heard him make a proposal. If you wish, we shall present it to you.” Their foolish men said, “We have no need for you to tell us anything about him.” Those of sound judgment among them said, “Tell us what you heard him say.” Budayl then related the Prophet’s ﷺ proposal to them.
ʿUrwah ibn Masʿūd stood and said, “My people, are you not like parents to me?” They replied, “Yes.” He asked, “And am I not like a son to you?” They replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Do you have any reason to mistrust me?” They replied, “No.” He said, “Do you not know that I called upon the people of ʿUkāẓ to support you, and when they refused me, I came to you with my family, children and all who obeyed me?” They replied, “Yes.”
He said, “This man has offered you a sound proposal. Accept it, and allow me to go to him.” They said, “Go to him.”
He came to the Prophet ﷺ and began speaking with him. The Prophet ﷺ gave him essentially the same reply he had given Budayl.
ʿUrwah then said, “Muḥammad, suppose you completely destroy your own people: have you ever heard of an Arab before you who exterminated his own family? If the opposite occurs, by Allah, I see no prominent men around you; I see only a mixed collection of people who are likely to flee and abandon you.”
Abū Bakr said to him, “Go suck al-Lāt’s clitoris! Would we flee from him and abandon him?”
ʿUrwah asked, “Who said that?” They replied, “Abū Bakr.” He said, “By the One in whose hand is my soul, were it not for a favour you once did me which I have not yet repaid, I would answer you.”
ʿUrwah continued speaking with the Prophet ﷺ, and whenever he spoke he reached for the Prophet’s beard. Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah was standing over the Prophet ﷺ, carrying a sword and wearing a helmet. Whenever ʿUrwah reached for the Prophet’s beard, al-Mughīrah struck his hand with the lower part of the sword’s sheath and said, “Keep your hand away from the beard of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
ʿUrwah raised his head and asked, “Who is this?” They replied, “Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah.” He said, “You treacherous man! Am I not still working to settle the consequences of your treachery?”
Before Islam, al-Mughīrah had accompanied a group of men, killed them and taken their property. He then came and embraced Islam. The Prophet ﷺ told him: “As for your Islam, I accept it; but as for the property, I will have nothing to do with it.”
ʿUrwah began carefully observing the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ. By Allah, whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spat, it fell into the hand of one of them, who rubbed it over his face and skin. Whenever he commanded them, they hastened to carry out his command. Whenever he performed ablution, they almost fought over the remaining water. When he spoke, they lowered their voices in his presence, and out of reverence they did not stare directly at him.
ʿUrwah returned to his people and said, “My people, by Allah, I have visited kings. I have visited Caesar, Chosroes and the Negus. By Allah, I have never seen any king whose followers revere him as the Companions of Muḥammad revere Muḥammad ﷺ.
“By Allah, whenever he spits, it falls into the hand of one of them, who rubs it over his face and skin. Whenever he commands them, they hasten to carry out his command. Whenever he performs ablution, they almost fight over the remaining water. When he speaks, they lower their voices in his presence, and out of reverence they do not stare directly at him. He has offered you a sound proposal, so accept it.”
A man from Banū Kinānah said, “Allow me to go to him.” They said, “Go.” When he appeared before the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “This man belongs to a people who venerate sacrificial animals, so send them out for him to see.”
The sacrificial animals were brought before him, while the people received him reciting the pilgrimage response. When he saw this, he exclaimed, “Glory be to Allah! These people should not be prevented from reaching the Sacred House.”
When he returned to his companions, he said, “I saw the sacrificial animals garlanded and marked. I do not believe they should be prevented from reaching the House.”
A man named Mikraz ibn Ḥafṣ then stood and said, “Allow me to go to him.” They said, “Go.” When he appeared before them, the Prophet ﷺ said: “This is Mikraz, and he is a wicked man.” Mikraz began speaking with the Prophet ﷺ.
While he was speaking, Suhayl ibn ʿAmr arrived. Maʿmar said that Ayyūb reported to him from ʿIkrimah that when Suhayl ibn ʿAmr arrived, the Prophet ﷺ said: “Your affair has now been made easy for you.”
Maʿmar reported that al-Zuhrī’s narration continued:
Suhayl ibn ʿAmr said, “Come, write an agreement between us and you.” The Prophet ﷺ summoned the scribe and dictated: “In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.”
Suhayl said, “By Allah, I do not know who ‘the Most Compassionate’ is. Write instead: ‘In Your name, O Allah,’ as you used to write.”
The Muslims said, “By Allah, we shall write only: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’” The Prophet ﷺ said: “Write: ‘In Your name, O Allah.’”
He then dictated: “This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.”
Suhayl said, “By Allah, had we known that you were the Messenger of Allah, we would neither have prevented you from reaching the House nor fought you. Write instead: ‘Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “By Allah, I am truly the Messenger of Allah even though you deny me. Write: ‘Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh.’”
Al-Zuhrī said that he accepted this because of his earlier statement: “They will not propose any plan by which they seek to honour the sacred ordinances of Allah without my accepting it.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “On condition that you allow us access to the Sacred House so that we may circumambulate it.”
Suhayl replied, “By Allah, we will not allow the Arabs to say that we yielded under pressure. You may do so next year.” This was written into the agreement.
Suhayl then said, “And on condition that no man from among us may come to you—even if he follows your religion—without your returning him to us.”
The Muslims exclaimed, “Glory be to Allah! How can a man be returned to the polytheists after coming as a Muslim?”
While they were in that state, Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr appeared, dragging himself in his chains. He had escaped from the lower part of Makkah and threw himself among the Muslims.
Suhayl said, “Muḥammad, this is the first person concerning whom I demand that you fulfil our agreement: return him to me.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “We have not yet completed the written agreement.”
Suhayl replied, “Then, by Allah, I shall never conclude any treaty with you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Grant him to me as an exception.”
He replied, “I will not grant him to you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Please, do so.”
He replied, “I will not.”
Mikraz said, “We have granted him to you,” but Suhayl did not agree.
Abū Jandal cried, “Muslims! Am I to be returned to the polytheists after coming to you as a Muslim? Do you not see what I have suffered?” He had been severely tortured for the sake of Allah.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: I went to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and asked, “Are you not truly Allah’s Prophet?”
He replied: “Indeed I am.”
I asked, “Are we not upon the truth and our enemy upon falsehood?”
He replied: “Indeed.”
I asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
He said: “I am the Messenger of Allah. I do not disobey Him, and He will grant me victory.”
I asked, “Did you not tell us that we would reach the Sacred House and circumambulate it?”
He replied: “Indeed. But did I tell you that we would reach it this year?”
I said, “No.”
He said: “You will certainly reach it and circumambulate it.”
I then went to Abū Bakr and asked, “Abū Bakr, is he not truly Allah’s Prophet?”
He replied, “Indeed he is.”
I asked, “Are we not upon the truth and our enemy upon falsehood?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
I asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
He said, “Man, he is truly the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He does not disobey his Lord, and Allah will grant him victory. Hold firmly to his stirrup, for by Allah, he is upon the truth.”
I said, “Did he not tell us that we would reach the House and circumambulate it?”
He replied, “Indeed. But did he tell you that you would reach it this year?”
I said, “No.”
He said, “You will certainly reach it and circumambulate it.”
Al-Zuhrī reported that ʿUmar said: “Because of what I had said that day, I later performed many righteous deeds in expiation.”
When the treaty had been completed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told his Companions: “Rise, slaughter your sacrificial animals and then shave your heads.” By Allah, not one of them rose, even after he repeated the instruction three times.
When none of them responded, he entered the tent of Umm Salamah and told her how the people had reacted. Umm Salamah said, “Prophet of Allah, would you like them to comply? Go out and do not speak a word to anyone until you slaughter your own sacrificial animal and summon your barber to shave your head.”
