He went out and spoke to no one until he had done so. He slaughtered his sacrificial animal and summoned his barber, who shaved his head. When the Companions saw this, they rose, slaughtered their animals and began shaving one another’s heads. In their grief, they nearly killed one another.
Believing women then came to him, so Allah revealed: “Believers, when believing women come to you as emigrants, test their faith—Allah knows their faith best. If you determine that they are believers, do not return them to the disbelievers. These women are not lawful for the disbelievers, nor are the disbelievers lawful for them. Return to their former husbands whatever bridal gifts they spent. There is no blame upon you if you marry these women, provided that you give them their bridal gifts. Do not retain marital bonds with disbelieving women” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:10].
That day, ʿUmar divorced two wives who had remained upon polytheism. Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān married one of them, and Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah married the other.
The Prophet ﷺ then returned to Madinah. Abū Baṣīr, a Muslim man from Quraysh, came to him. Quraysh sent two men to retrieve him, saying, “Remember the covenant you made with us.” The Prophet ﷺ handed him over to them.
They departed with him until they reached Dhū al-Ḥulayfah, where they stopped to eat some dates. Abū Baṣīr said to one of the men, “By Allah, I see that this sword of yours is excellent.”
The other man drew it and said, “Yes, by Allah, it is excellent. I have tested it time and again.”
Abū Baṣīr said, “Let me look at it.” The man handed it to him, whereupon Abū Baṣīr struck him with it until he died.
The other man fled until he reached Madinah and ran into the mosque. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said: “This man has certainly witnessed something terrifying.”
When the man reached the Prophet ﷺ, he cried, “By Allah, my companion has been killed, and I shall be killed as well!”
Abū Baṣīr then arrived and said, “Prophet of Allah, by Allah, Allah has fully discharged your obligation. You returned me to them, and then Allah rescued me from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “May his mother lose him! What an inciter of war he would be, if only he had others with him.”
When Abū Baṣīr heard this, he understood that the Prophet ﷺ would return him to Quraysh, so he left and travelled to the seacoast.
Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl also escaped from Quraysh and joined Abū Baṣīr. Thereafter, every man who embraced Islam and escaped from Quraysh joined Abū Baṣīr, until they formed a sizeable band. By Allah, whenever they heard of a Quraysh caravan travelling toward Syria, they intercepted it, killed its guards and seized its property.
Quraysh therefore sent word to the Prophet ﷺ, imploring him by Allah and by the bonds of kinship to summon these men, declaring that anyone who thereafter came to him from Makkah would be safe and would not be returned. The Prophet ﷺ then sent for them.
Allah revealed: “He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after giving you victory over them…” through His words: “…the zealotry—the zealotry of the Age of Ignorance” [al-Fatḥ 48:24–26].
Their ignorant zealotry consisted of refusing to acknowledge that he was Allah’s Prophet, refusing the words “In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,” and preventing the Muslims from reaching the Sacred House.
ʿUqayl reported from al-Zuhrī that ʿUrwah said: ʿĀʾishah informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to test the faith of the emigrating women.
We have also been informed that when Allah commanded the Muslims to return to the polytheists what they had spent as bridal gifts upon wives who emigrated, and ordered the Muslims not to retain marital bonds with disbelieving women, ʿUmar divorced two wives: Qarībah bint Abī Umayyah and the daughter of Jarwal al-Khuzāʿī. Muʿāwiyah married Qarībah, while Abū Jahm married the other.
When the disbelievers refused to reimburse the Muslims for what they had spent upon wives who defected to them, Allah revealed: “If any of your wives go over to the disbelievers and you subsequently obtain compensation…” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:11].
The compensation referred to what the Muslims paid to a disbeliever whose wife had emigrated. The Muslim whose wife had gone over to the disbelievers was therefore to be reimbursed for the bridal gift he had paid, using the bridal gifts left behind by the disbelieving husbands of women who had emigrated. We do not know of any emigrating woman who renounced her faith after believing.
We have also been informed that Abū Baṣīr ibn Usayd al-Thaqafī came to the Prophet ﷺ as a believing emigrant during the treaty period. Al-Akhnas ibn Sharīq wrote to the Prophet ﷺ requesting that Abū Baṣīr be returned, and the remainder of the account follows.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2731, 2732, 2733).

9011 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out during the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah intending to visit the Sacred House, not to fight. He brought seventy sacrificial camels, while the people numbered seven hundred, so each camel was offered on behalf of ten men.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached ʿUsfān, Bishr ibn Sufyān al-Kaʿbī met him and said, “Messenger of Allah, Quraysh have heard of your approach. They have come out with nursing she-camels and their young, dressed in leopard skins and pledging before Allah that you will never enter Makkah against their will. Khālid ibn al-Walīd has been sent ahead with their cavalry to Kurāʿ al-Ghamīm.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “How unfortunate Quraysh are! War has consumed them. What harm would it do them to leave me to deal with the rest of the people? If they defeat me, that will be what they desire. But if Allah grants me victory, they may enter Islam with their strength intact. If they choose not to, they may then fight while still possessing strength. What do Quraysh imagine? By Allah, I shall continue striving against them for the cause with which Allah sent me until Allah grants me victory or this neck is severed.”
He then instructed the people to take the route to the right through al-Ḥumḍ, along a road leading to the Pass of al-Marār and al-Ḥudaybiyyah from below Makkah. He led the army along that road. When the Quraysh cavalry saw the dust raised by the army and realised that the Muslims had changed their route, they galloped back to Quraysh.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued until his she-camel knelt down at the Pass of al-Marār. The people said, “She has become stubborn.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “She has not become stubborn, nor is stubbornness part of her nature. Rather, she has been restrained from Makkah by the One who restrained the elephant. By Allah, Quraysh will not invite me today to any proposal involving the maintenance of kinship without my accepting it.”
He then told the people: “Dismount.” They said, “Messenger of Allah, there is no water in the valley beside which the people can camp.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took an arrow from his quiver and gave it to one of his Companions. The man descended into one of the wells and planted the arrow in it, whereupon the water surged forth until the people and their animals gathered abundantly around it.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had settled at the campsite, Budayl ibn Warqāʾ arrived with some men of Khuzāʿah. The Prophet ﷺ told them essentially what he had told Bishr ibn Sufyān. They returned to Quraysh and said, “People of Quraysh, you are acting hastily against Muḥammad. Muḥammad has not come to fight; he has come only to visit this House and honour its sanctity.” Quraysh distrusted them.
Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq reported that al-Zuhrī said: Khuzāʿah—both its polytheists and Muslims—were trusted confidants and sincere advisers of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They concealed from him nothing that occurred in Makkah.
Quraysh said, “Even if that is his sole purpose, by Allah, he will never enter Makkah against our will, lest the Arabs speak of it.”
They sent Mikraz ibn Ḥafṣ, a man of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, to him. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said: “This is a treacherous man.” When Mikraz reached him, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave him essentially the same message he had given the previous envoys. Mikraz returned to Quraysh and conveyed the Prophet’s words.
They then sent al-Ḥulays ibn ʿAlqamah al-Kinānī, who was the leader of the allied tribes at that time. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said: “This man belongs to a people who are deeply devoted to religious observances. Send the sacrificial animals out before him.”
The animals were sent toward him, flowing across the breadth of the valley with their garlands upon them. Their hair had become worn from being detained so long from the place of sacrifice. Out of reverence for what he saw, al-Ḥulays turned back without reaching the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He told Quraysh, “I have seen something that it is not lawful to prevent: sacrificial animals wearing their garlands, their hair worn away from being detained so long from their place of sacrifice.” They replied, “Sit down. You are merely a Bedouin with no knowledge.”
They then sent ʿUrwah ibn Masʿūd al-Thaqafī. He said, “People of Quraysh, I have seen how harshly and abusively you treat the men whom you send to Muḥammad when they return. You know that you are like parents to me and I am like a son to you. When I heard of the difficulty that had befallen you, I gathered those of my people who obeyed me and came to support you personally.”
They replied, “You have spoken truthfully. We do not mistrust you.”
He went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, sat before him and said, “Muḥammad, you have gathered the rabble and brought them against your own family to destroy it! Quraysh have come out with nursing she-camels and their young, dressed in leopard skins and pledging before Allah that you will never enter Makkah against their will. By Allah, I can almost see these people abandoning you tomorrow.”
Abū Bakr was sitting behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Go suck al-Lāt’s clitoris! Would we abandon him?”
ʿUrwah asked, “Who is this, Muḥammad?”
He replied: “This is the son of Abū Quḥāfah.”
ʿUrwah said, “By Allah, were it not for a favour you once did me, I would repay your words in kind. Let that favour settle this.”
ʿUrwah then reached for the beard of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah was standing over the Prophet ﷺ in armour, and he struck ʿUrwah’s hand, saying, “Remove your hand from the beard of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ before, by Allah, it is prevented from returning to you.”
ʿUrwah said, “Woe to you! How harsh and coarse you are!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled.
ʿUrwah asked, “Who is this, Muḥammad?”
He replied: “This is your nephew, al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah.”
ʿUrwah said, “You treacherous man! Was it not only yesterday that I settled the disgrace of your treachery?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave him the same message he had given the previous envoys and informed him that he had not come seeking war.
ʿUrwah then departed, having observed how the Prophet’s Companions treated him: whenever he performed ablution they rushed for the remaining water; whenever he spat they rushed to receive it; and whenever a hair fell from him they picked it up.
He returned to Quraysh and said, “People of Quraysh, I have visited Chosroes in his kingdom, and I have visited Caesar and the Negus in their kingdoms. By Allah, I have never seen any king among his subjects like Muḥammad among his Companions. I have seen people who will never surrender him for anything. Consider carefully what you will do.”
Before that, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had sent Khirāsh ibn Umayyah al-Khuzāʿī to Makkah, mounted upon one of his camels, to inform its chiefs of the purpose for which he had come. Quraysh hamstrung the Messenger’s camel and intended to kill Khirāsh, but the allied tribes protected him. They released him, and he returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ then summoned ʿUmar to send him to Makkah. ʿUmar said, “Messenger of Allah, I fear that Quraysh may kill me. There is no one from Banū ʿAdī in Makkah to protect me, and Quraysh know my hostility and severity toward them. But I can direct you to a man who possesses greater protection there than I do: ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿUthmān and sent him to Quraysh to inform them that he had not come to fight anyone, but only to visit the Sacred House and honour its sanctity.
ʿUthmān travelled to Makkah, where Abān ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ met him. Abān dismounted, seated ʿUthmān before him on his mount and rode behind him, granting him protection until he could deliver the Messenger’s message.
ʿUthmān went to Abū Sufyān and the leading men of Quraysh and conveyed the message of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They told him, “If you wish to circumambulate the House, you may do so.”
He replied, “I would never do so before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ circumambulates it.” Quraysh detained him, and word reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that ʿUthmān had been killed.
Muḥammad reported that al-Zuhrī told him: Quraysh sent Suhayl ibn ʿAmr, of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy, saying, “Go to Muḥammad and conclude a treaty with him, but the treaty must require him to turn back this year. By Allah, the Arabs must never be allowed to say that he entered Makkah against our will.”
When Suhayl ibn ʿAmr came, the Prophet ﷺ said: “The people now desire peace, since they have sent this man.”
When Suhayl reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they spoke at length and exchanged proposals until they agreed upon peace. When the matter had been settled and only the written document remained, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb went to Abū Bakr and asked, “Abū Bakr, is he not the Messenger of Allah? Are we not Muslims? Are they not polytheists?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
Abū Bakr said, “ʿUmar, hold firmly to his stirrup wherever he leads, for I testify that he is the Messenger of Allah.”
ʿUmar replied, “And I testify to that.”
ʿUmar then went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, “Messenger of Allah, are we not Muslims? Are they not polytheists?”
He replied: “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
He replied: “I am Allah’s servant and Messenger. I shall never oppose His command, and He will never abandon me.”
ʿUmar later said: “Because of what I had done and the words I had spoken that day, I continued fasting, praying, giving charity and emancipating slaves until I hoped that the matter had been turned to good.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and said: “Write: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’”
Suhayl ibn ʿAmr said, “I do not acknowledge this. Write instead: ‘In Your name, O Allah.’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Write: ‘In Your name, O Allah. This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, with Suhayl ibn ʿAmr.’”
Suhayl said, “Had I acknowledged that you were the Messenger of Allah, I would not have fought you. Write instead: ‘This is the agreement concluded by Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh and Suhayl ibn ʿAmr.’”
They agreed to suspend warfare for ten years, during which the people would enjoy security and refrain from attacking one another. Any man of Quraysh who came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ without his guardian’s permission would be returned, whereas anyone who left the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for Quraysh would not be returned. Relations between them would be sincere and free of hidden hostility, with neither theft nor treachery.
The written conditions also stipulated that any tribe wishing to enter the alliance and covenant of Muḥammad could do so, and any tribe wishing to enter the alliance and covenant of Quraysh could do so.
Khuzāʿah immediately declared, “We enter the alliance and covenant of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” Banū Bakr declared, “We enter the alliance and covenant of Quraysh.”
The treaty further stipulated: “You will turn back this year and not enter Makkah. Next year we will withdraw from the city, and you may enter it with your Companions and remain for three days. You may carry only a traveller’s weapons—swords kept in their scabbards.”
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was having the agreement written, Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr arrived in chains, having escaped to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Companions had set out with no doubt that victory would occur because of a vision the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had seen. When they witnessed the treaty, the return without entering Makkah and the concessions the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had accepted, they were overwhelmed by such distress that they nearly perished from grief.
When Suhayl saw Abū Jandal, he went to him, struck him in the face and said, “Muḥammad, our agreement was concluded before this man came to you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “You have spoken truthfully.”
Suhayl seized Abū Jandal by the front of his clothing. Abū Jandal cried out at the top of his voice, “Muslims! Will you return me to the polytheists so that they may persecute me for my religion?” This only intensified the people’s anguish.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Abū Jandal, remain patient and seek your reward from Allah. Allah will provide relief and a way out for you and the other oppressed Muslims with you. We have concluded a treaty with these people, giving them our covenant and receiving theirs in return. We will not betray them.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb went to Abū Jandal and walked beside him, saying, “Be patient, Abū Jandal. They are only polytheists, and the blood of any one of them is merely the blood of a dog.” ʿUmar brought the sword’s hilt close to him, hoping that he would seize it and strike his father, but Abū Jandal could not bring himself to kill his father.
The agreement was carried into effect. When they had finished writing it—the Messenger of Allah ﷺ praying within the sacred precinct while his camp stood outside it—he arose and said: “People, slaughter your sacrificial animals and shave your heads.” No one stood.
He repeated the command, but no one stood. He repeated it a third time, yet still no one rose.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered the tent of Umm Salamah and asked: “Umm Salamah, what is the matter with the people?”
She replied, “Messenger of Allah, they have been overwhelmed by the grief you have seen. Do not speak to any of them. Go directly to your sacrificial animal, slaughter it and shave your head. Once you have done so, the people will follow.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out without speaking to anyone. He approached his sacrificial animal and slaughtered it, then sat down and had his head shaved. The people immediately arose, slaughtered their animals and shaved their heads.
When they reached the middle of the road between Makkah and Madinah, Sūrat al-Fatḥ was revealed.
Good: Related by Imām Aḥmad (18910) and Ibn Hishām in al-Sīrah (2/308). Some passages in Ibn Isḥāq’s account are open to question, including the statement that the people numbered seven hundred, as explained shortly before.

9012 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah reported that he stood over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ carrying a sword, with his face covered, while ʿUrwah was present. As ʿUrwah spoke to the Prophet ﷺ, he repeatedly reached for his beard.
Al-Mughīrah said to him, “Keep your hand away from his beard, or it will not return to you.”
ʿUrwah asked, “Who is this?”
He was told, “This is your nephew, al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah.”
ʿUrwah said, “You treacherous man! You have not yet cleansed your head of your treachery.”
Authentic: Related by Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah, as cited in al-Maṭālib (2116), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4583). The wording is that of Ibn Ḥibbān.

9013 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We arrived at al-Ḥudaybiyyah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, numbering fourteen hundred. Its well contained so little water that it could not provide enough for fifty sheep. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat at the edge of the well and either supplicated or spat into it. The water then surged forth, so we drank and drew what we needed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned us to pledge allegiance beneath the tree. I was the first to pledge. When he reached the middle of the people, he said: “Pledge to me, Salamah!”
I replied, “I pledged to you, Messenger of Allah, among the first people.”
He said: “And again.”
Seeing that I was unarmed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave me a shield. When he reached the last of the people, he said: “Will you not pledge to me, Salamah?”
I replied, “I pledged to you among the first people and again in the middle.”
He said: “And again.” I therefore pledged a third time.
He then asked: “Salamah, where is the shield I gave you?”
I replied, “Messenger of Allah, my uncle ʿĀmir met me while he was unarmed, so I gave it to him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and said: “You are like the man of old who said: ‘O Allah, grant me a beloved companion who is dearer to me than my own self.’”
The polytheists then entered into peace negotiations with us. We began moving freely among one another and concluded the treaty.
I was serving Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbayd Allāh: watering and grooming his horse, attending to his needs and eating from his food. I had left my family and property behind, emigrating to Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.
When we concluded peace with the people of Makkah and began mixing with one another, I came to a tree, cleared away its thorns and lay beneath it. Four polytheists from Makkah came and began insulting the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so I detested them.
I moved to another tree. They hung up their weapons and lay down. While they were resting, a caller shouted from the lower part of the valley, “Emigrants! Ibn Zunaym has been killed!”
I drew my sword and rushed upon the four men while they were lying down. I gathered their weapons into a bundle in my hand and said, “By the One who has honoured the face of Muḥammad, none of you will raise his head without my striking the place where his eyes are.”
I drove them before me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My uncle ʿĀmir also brought a man of al-ʿAbalāt named Mikraz, leading him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ upon an armoured horse, together with seventy polytheists.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at them and said: “Release them, so that both the beginning and the recurrence of aggression will be attributed to them.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ pardoned them.
Allah then revealed the complete verse: “He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after giving you victory over them” [al-Fatḥ 48:24].
We then departed for Madinah and camped at a place separated from the polytheist tribe of Banū Liḥyān by a mountain. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed for forgiveness for whoever would climb that mountain during the night and serve as a lookout for the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions. I climbed it two or three times that night.
We then reached Madinah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent his riding camels out with Rabāḥ, his servant, and I accompanied him. I also took Ṭalḥah’s horse to exercise it among the camels.
The following morning, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Fazārī raided the Messenger’s herd, drove away all the camels and killed their herdsman.
I said, “Rabāḥ, take this horse to Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbayd Allāh and inform the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that the polytheists have raided his grazing animals.”
I climbed a hillock, faced Madinah and cried three times, “Alarm! Alarm!” I then pursued the raiders, shooting arrows at them and chanting:
I am the son of al-Akwaʿ;
 today is the day the cowardly will perish.
I caught up with one of them and shot an arrow through his saddle until its point reached his shoulder. I said:
Take that! I am the son of al-Akwaʿ;
 today is the day the cowardly will perish.
By Allah, I continued shooting at them and hamstringing their mounts. Whenever a horseman turned back toward me, I took cover beneath a tree, shot at him and brought down his mount. When they entered a narrow mountain pass, I climbed above them and began pelting them with stones.
I continued pursuing them until I had recovered every camel from the herd of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and left it behind me. They abandoned the camels to me.
I continued chasing and shooting at them until, to lighten their loads, they discarded more than thirty cloaks and thirty spears. Whenever they threw something down, I marked it with a pile of stones so that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions could identify it.
When they reached a narrow mountain pass, a man of the family of Badr al-Fazārī came to them. They sat down to eat their late-morning meal, while I positioned myself at the top of a mountain spur.
The Fazārī asked, “Who is that man I see?”
They replied, “We have suffered terribly from him! By Allah, he has not left us since the darkness before dawn. He has kept shooting at us until he has stripped everything from our hands.”
He said, “Let four of you go up against him.” Four men climbed the mountain toward me. When they came within speaking distance, I asked, “Do you know me?”
They replied, “No. Who are you?”
I said, “I am Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ. By the One who has honoured the face of Muḥammad, I will catch any one of you whom I pursue, while none of you who pursues me will catch me.”
One of them said, “I believe he is telling the truth.” They then returned.
I remained in my position until I saw the horsemen of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ weaving through the trees. The first was al-Akhram al-Asadī, followed by Abū Qatādah al-Anṣārī, and then al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad al-Kindī.
I seized al-Akhram’s reins as the raiders turned and fled. I said, “Akhram, beware of them! Do not let them cut you off before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions catch up.”
He replied, “Salamah, if you believe in Allah and the Last Day and know that Paradise and Hell are real, do not stand between me and martyrdom.” I therefore released him.
He met ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and brought down his horse, but ʿAbd al-Raḥmān stabbed and killed him, then mounted his horse. Abū Qatādah, the horseman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, caught ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, stabbed him and killed him.
By the One who honoured the face of Muḥammad, I pursued them on foot until I could no longer see the Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ or even the dust they raised behind me.
Shortly before sunset, the raiders turned toward a ravine containing water called Dhū Qarad, intending to drink because they were thirsty. When they saw me running behind them, I drove them away from it, and they did not taste a single drop. They fled rapidly up a pass.
I ran after them, caught one of them and struck him with an arrow in the ridge of his shoulder. I said:
Take that! I am the son of al-Akwaʿ;
 today is the day the cowardly will perish.
He exclaimed, “May his mother lose him! Is this the same al-Akwaʿ who pursued us this morning?”
I replied, “Yes, enemy of yourself—the very same al-Akwaʿ from this morning.”
They abandoned two horses on the pass, and I brought them back, driving them toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿĀmir caught up with me carrying a leather vessel containing diluted milk and another containing water. I performed ablution and drank, then went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who had reached the water from which I had driven the raiders.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had recovered the camels and everything I had rescued from the polytheists, including every spear and cloak. Bilāl had slaughtered one of the recovered she-camels and was roasting some of its liver and hump for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
I said, “Messenger of Allah, allow me to select one hundred men and pursue the raiders. I will kill every one of them so that none remains to carry the news.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed until his molar teeth appeared in the firelight. He asked: “Salamah, do you really think you could do that?”
I replied, “Yes, by the One who has honoured you!”
He said: “They are now being entertained as guests in the territory of Ghaṭafān.”
A man from Ghaṭafān then arrived and said, “So-and-so slaughtered a camel for them. As they were skinning it, they saw dust rising and said, ‘The pursuing force has reached you!’ They fled immediately.”
The following morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The best of our horsemen today was Abū Qatādah, and the best of our foot soldiers was Salamah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave me two shares of the spoils: the share of a horseman and that of a foot soldier. He combined both shares for me, then seated me behind him upon al-ʿAḍbāʾ as we returned to Madinah.
While we were travelling, a man of the Anṣār who was considered unbeatable in running kept calling, “Will anyone race me to Madinah? Is there anyone who will race?” He repeated it several times.
When I heard him, I asked, “Do you not show respect to an honourable man or stand in awe of a noble one?”
He replied, “No—unless it is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I said, “Messenger of Allah, may my father and mother be sacrificed for you, allow me to race this man.”
He said: “If you wish.”
I told the man, “Go ahead.” I bent my legs, sprang forward and began running. I let him gain one or two rises in the ground while conserving my strength, then ran after him. I again allowed him one or two rises. I then accelerated until I caught him, struck him between the shoulders and said, “By Allah, I have overtaken you!”
He replied, “I believe you have.” I beat him to Madinah.
By Allah, we remained only three nights before setting out for Khaybar with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My uncle ʿĀmir began chanting to the company:
By Allah, had Allah not guided us, we would not be guided,
 nor would we give charity or pray.
 We can never dispense with Your favour;
 make our feet firm when we meet the enemy,
 and send tranquillity down upon us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Who is this?”
He replied, “I am ʿĀmir.”
He said: “May your Lord forgive you.”
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ specifically prayed for someone’s forgiveness in this manner, that person attained martyrdom. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who was riding a camel, called out, “Prophet of Allah, if only you had allowed us to enjoy ʿĀmir’s company longer!”
When we reached Khaybar, their champion Marḥab came out, strutting with his sword and chanting:
Khaybar knows that I am Marḥab,
 fully armed, a tested warrior,
 when wars advance in blazing fury.
My uncle ʿĀmir came out against him and chanted:
Khaybar knows that I am ʿĀmir,
 fully armed, a daring warrior.
They exchanged two blows. Marḥab’s sword struck ʿĀmir’s shield, and ʿĀmir attempted to strike him from below. His sword rebounded upon himself and severed the artery in his forearm, causing his death.
Salamah said: I came upon several Companions of the Prophet ﷺ saying, “ʿĀmir’s deeds have become void; he killed himself.”
I went to the Prophet ﷺ weeping and asked, “Messenger of Allah, have ʿĀmir’s deeds become void?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Who said that?”
I replied, “Some of your Companions.”
He said: “Whoever said that has lied. Rather, he will receive a double reward.”
He then sent me to fetch ʿAlī, who was suffering from inflamed eyes, saying: “I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger—or whom Allah and His Messenger love.”
I went to ʿAlī and led him by the hand because of his eyes until I brought him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He spat into his eyes, and they were cured. He then gave him the banner.
Marḥab came out chanting:
Khaybar knows that I am Marḥab,
 fully armed, a tested warrior,
 when wars advance in blazing fury.
ʿAlī replied:
I am the one whose mother named him Ḥaydarah,
 like a lion of the forests, fearsome to behold;
 I repay them measure for measure, in a mighty measure.
ʿAlī struck Marḥab on the head and killed him, and the conquest was then achieved at his hands.
Authentic: Related by Muslim (1807).

9014 — Anas reported that Quraysh concluded a treaty with the Prophet ﷺ, represented by Suhayl ibn ʿAmr. The Prophet ﷺ told ʿAlī: “Write: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’”
Suhayl said, “We do not know this phrase, ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’ Write what we recognise: ‘In Your name, O Allah.’”
The Prophet ﷺ then said: “Write: ‘From Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.’”
They said, “Had we known that you were the Messenger of Allah, we would have followed you. Write instead your name and your father’s name.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Write: ‘From Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh.’”
They imposed the condition that anyone who left the Muslims for Quraysh would not be returned, whereas anyone who left Quraysh for the Muslims would be returned to them.
The Companions asked, “Messenger of Allah, should we write this?”
He replied: “Yes. Whoever leaves us for them—may Allah keep him far away. As for anyone who comes to us from them, Allah will provide him relief and a way out.”
Authentic: Related by Muslim (1784:93).

9015 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Ḥarūriyyah seceded and isolated themselves, I said to them:
“On the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concluded a treaty with the polytheists. He told ʿAlī: ‘Write, ʿAlī: “This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.”’
“They said, ‘Had we known that you were the Messenger of Allah, we would not have fought you.’
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘Erase it, ʿAlī. O Allah, You know that I am Your Messenger. Erase it, ʿAlī, and write: “This is the treaty concluded by Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh.”’
“By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was better than ʿAlī, yet he erased his own title, and that erasure did not remove him from prophethood. Have I answered this objection of yours?”
They replied, “Yes.”
Good: Related by Aḥmad (3187).
In summary, the treaty concluded by the Prophet ﷺ with Suhayl ibn ʿAmr contained the following provisions:
Warfare between the Muslims and Quraysh would cease for ten years. The people would enjoy security and refrain from attacking one another. Relations between them would remain sincere and free of hidden hostility, theft and treachery.
Anyone from Quraysh who came to Muḥammad without his guardian’s permission would be returned, whereas anyone who left Muḥammad for Quraysh would not be returned to him.
Any tribe wishing to enter Muḥammad’s alliance and covenant could do so, and any tribe wishing to enter the alliance and covenant of Quraysh could do so. Khuzāʿah joined the Messenger’s covenant, while Banū Bakr joined that of Quraysh.
The Prophet ﷺ and his Companions would return without performing ʿumrah that year. The following year, the polytheists would leave Makkah so that the Muslims could enter and remain for three days. They would carry no weapons other than swords kept in their scabbards.

9016 — Abū Wāʾil reported: On the day of Ṣiffīn, Sahl ibn Ḥunayf stood and said:
“People, distrust your own opinions. We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, and had we considered fighting appropriate, we would have fought. This occurred during the treaty between the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the polytheists.
“ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, are we not upon the truth and they upon falsehood?’
“He replied: ‘Indeed.’
“ʿUmar asked, ‘Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?’
“He replied: ‘Indeed.’
“ʿUmar asked, ‘Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?’
“He replied: ‘Son of al-Khaṭṭāb, I am the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will never abandon me.’”
Still deeply agitated, ʿUmar left and went to Abū Bakr. He asked, “Abū Bakr, are we not upon the truth and they upon falsehood?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?”
Abū Bakr replied, “Son of al-Khaṭṭāb, he is the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will never abandon him.”
The revelation announcing the victory then descended upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He sent for ʿUmar and recited it to him.
ʿUmar asked, “Messenger of Allah, is this truly a victory?”
He replied: “Yes.”
ʿUmar’s heart then became content and he withdrew his objection.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (3182) and Muslim (1785:94).

9017 — Sahl ibn Ḥunayf said:
“Distrust personal opinion. I remember myself on the day of Abū Jandal: had I been able to reject the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I would have done so. Yet Allah and His Messenger know best.
“Before this present conflict, whenever we placed our swords upon our shoulders to confront a terrifying situation, those swords led us toward an outcome we understood. But in this affair, whenever we close one breach, another bursts open before us, and we do not know how to deal with it.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4189) and Muslim (1785:96).

9018 — Sahl ibn Ḥunayf said at Ṣiffīn: “People, distrust your own opinions. By Allah, I remember myself on the day of Abū Jandal: had I been able to reject the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I would have done so. By Allah, whenever we placed our swords upon our shoulders for any cause, they led us toward an outcome we understood—except in this conflict of yours.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (3181) and Muslim (1785:95).

9019 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported that he heard Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah relate an account of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the ʿumrah of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. Part of their report was that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concluded the fixed-term treaty with Suhayl ibn ʿAmr, Suhayl imposed the following condition:
“No man from among us may come to you—even if he follows your religion—without your returning him to us and allowing us to take custody of him.”
Suhayl refused to conclude the treaty except upon this condition. The believers disliked it, were deeply distressed and voiced their objections. When Suhayl refused to accept anything else, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concluded the agreement accordingly. That day he returned Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl to his father, Suhayl ibn ʿAmr. During the treaty period, no man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from Quraysh without being returned, even if he was a Muslim.
Believing women, however, came as emigrants. Umm Kulthūm bint ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ was among those who came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and she was a young unmarried woman. Her family came asking him to return her, whereupon Allah revealed what He revealed concerning the believing women.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2711, 2712, 4180, 4181).
The words “Allah revealed what He revealed concerning the believing women” refer to His statement: “Believers, when believing women come to you as emigrants, test their faith—Allah knows their faith best. If you determine that they are believers, do not return them to the disbelievers. These women are not lawful for the disbelievers, nor are the disbelievers lawful for them” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:10].

9020 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam reported: While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was having the treaty written, Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr arrived in chains, having escaped to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Companions had set out with no doubt that victory would occur because of a vision the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had seen. When they witnessed the treaty, the return without entering Makkah and the concessions the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had accepted, they were overwhelmed by such distress that they nearly perished from grief.
When Suhayl saw Abū Jandal, he went to him, struck him in the face and said, “Muḥammad, our agreement was settled before this man came to you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “You have spoken truthfully.”
Suhayl seized Abū Jandal by the front of his clothing. Abū Jandal cried at the top of his voice, “Muslims! Will you return me to the polytheists so that they may persecute me for my religion?” This only intensified the people’s anguish.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Abū Jandal, remain patient and seek your reward from Allah. Allah will provide relief and a way out for you and the other oppressed Muslims with you. We have concluded a treaty with these people, giving them our covenant and receiving theirs in return. We will not betray them.”
Good: Related by Aḥmad (18910).

9021 — Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān, each confirming the other’s account, reported: The Prophet ﷺ returned to Madinah. Abū Baṣīr, a Muslim man from Quraysh, came to him. Quraysh sent two men to retrieve him, saying, “Remember the covenant you made with us.” The Prophet ﷺ handed him over to them.
They departed with him until they reached Dhū al-Ḥulayfah, where they stopped to eat some dates. Abū Baṣīr said to one of the men, “By Allah, I see that this sword of yours is excellent.”
The man drew it and said, “Yes, by Allah, it is excellent. I have tested it time and again.”
Abū Baṣīr said, “Let me look at it.” The man handed it to him, whereupon Abū Baṣīr struck him with it until he died.
The other man fled to Madinah and ran into the mosque. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said: “This man has certainly witnessed something terrifying.”
When the man reached the Prophet ﷺ, he cried, “By Allah, my companion has been killed, and I shall be killed as well!”
Abū Baṣīr then arrived and said, “Prophet of Allah, by Allah, Allah has fully discharged your obligation. You returned me to them, and then Allah rescued me from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “May his mother lose him! What an inciter of war he would be, if only he had others with him.”
When Abū Baṣīr heard this, he understood that the Prophet ﷺ would return him to Quraysh, so he left and travelled to the seacoast.
Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl also escaped from Quraysh and joined Abū Baṣīr. Thereafter, every man who embraced Islam and escaped from Quraysh joined Abū Baṣīr, until they formed a sizeable band.
By Allah, whenever they heard of a Quraysh caravan travelling toward Syria, they intercepted it, killed its guards and seized its property.
Quraysh therefore sent word to the Prophet ﷺ, imploring him by Allah and by the bonds of kinship to summon these men, declaring that anyone who thereafter came to him from Makkah would be safe and would not be returned. The Prophet ﷺ then sent for them.
Allah revealed: “He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after giving you victory over them…” through His words: “…the zealotry—the zealotry of the Age of Ignorance” [al-Fatḥ 48:24–26].
Their ignorant zealotry consisted of refusing to acknowledge that he was Allah’s Prophet, rejecting the words “In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,” and preventing the Muslims from reaching the Sacred House.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2731).

9022 — Zayd ibn Aslam reported from his father that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was travelling on one of his journeys, with ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb riding beside him at night. ʿUmar asked him about something, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not answer. He asked a second time, but received no answer, and then asked a third time without receiving a reply.
ʿUmar said to himself, “May your mother lose you, ʿUmar! You importuned the Messenger of Allah ﷺ three times, and on each occasion he did not answer you.”
ʿUmar continued: I urged my camel onward and rode ahead of the Muslims, fearing that revelation might descend concerning me. Before long, I heard someone calling loudly for me. I thought, “I feared that Qur’anic revelation would descend concerning me.”
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and greeted him. He said: “Tonight a sūrah was revealed to me that is dearer to me than everything upon which the sun rises.” He then recited: “Indeed, We have granted you a manifest victory” [al-Fatḥ 48:1].
Authentic: Related by Mālik (9) and al-Bukhārī (4177).
The expression “you importuned the Messenger of Allah ﷺ” means that he persisted in questioning him.

9023 — Abū Wāʾil reported: On the day of Ṣiffīn, Sahl ibn Ḥunayf stood and said:
“People, distrust your own opinions. We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, and had we considered fighting appropriate, we would have fought. This occurred during the treaty between the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the polytheists.
“ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, are we not upon the truth and they upon falsehood?’
“He replied: ‘Indeed.’
“ʿUmar asked, ‘Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?’
“He replied: ‘Indeed.’
“ʿUmar asked, ‘Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?’
“He replied: ‘Son of al-Khaṭṭāb, I am the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will never abandon me.’”
Still deeply agitated, ʿUmar went to Abū Bakr and asked, “Abū Bakr, are we not upon the truth and they upon falsehood?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?”
He replied, “Indeed.”
ʿUmar asked, “Why, then, should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?”
Abū Bakr replied, “Son of al-Khaṭṭāb, he is the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will never abandon him.”
The revelation announcing the victory then descended upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He sent for ʿUmar and recited it to him.
ʿUmar asked, “Messenger of Allah, is this truly a victory?”
He replied: “Yes.”
ʿUmar’s heart then became content and he withdrew his objection.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (3182) and Muslim (1785:94).

9024 — Anas ibn Mālik explained the verse, “Indeed, We have granted you a manifest victory” [al-Fatḥ 48:1], saying: “It refers to al-Ḥudaybiyyah.”
The Companions said, “May it bring you happiness and blessing, but what has been granted to us?” Allah then revealed: “So that He may admit believing men and believing women into gardens…” [al-Fatḥ 48:5].
Shuʿbah said: I went to Kūfah and related this entire account from Qatādah. When I later returned and mentioned it to him, he said: “The explanation of ‘Indeed, We have granted you a manifest victory’ is from Anas, while the words ‘May it bring you happiness and blessing’ are from ʿIkrimah.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4172) and Muslim (1786:97).
In another route related by Muslim, Anas ibn Mālik said: When the verses beginning, “Indeed, We have granted you a manifest victory,” through the words, “a supreme triumph” [al-Fatḥ 48:1–5], were revealed during the Prophet’s return from al-Ḥudaybiyyah, the Companions were overcome with sorrow and dejection, and the sacrificial animals had already been slaughtered there. The Prophet ﷺ said: “A verse has been revealed to me that is dearer to me than the entire world.”
The Treaty of al-Ḥudaybiyyah became a cause of the conquest of Makkah. It lasted no more than seventeen or eighteen months before Quraysh assisted its allies, Banū Bakr, against Khuzāʿah, the allies of the Muslims, as will be related in the account of the conquest of Makkah. See al-Bidāyah wa-al-Nihāyah (6/508).

9025 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: I went out before the call to the dawn prayer. The milch camels of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were grazing at Dhū Qarad. A servant of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf met me and said, “The milch camels of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ have been taken.”
I asked, “Who took them?”
He replied, “Ghaṭafān.”
I cried three times, “Alarm! Alarm!” making myself heard throughout the area between the two lava fields of Madinah. I then rushed forward until I caught them as they were drawing water.
I began shooting arrows at them, for I was a skilled archer, while chanting:
I am the son of al-Akwaʿ;
 today is the day the cowardly will perish.
I continued chanting and fighting until I recovered the camels and seized thirty cloaks from them.
The Prophet ﷺ and the people arrived. I said, “Prophet of Allah, I have kept them away from the water while they are thirsty. Send a force against them immediately.”
He replied: “Son of al-Akwaʿ, now that you have prevailed, show leniency.”
We then returned, with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ seating me behind him upon his she-camel until we entered Madinah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4194) and Muslim in al-Jihād (1806:131), both from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from Ḥātim ibn Ismāʿīl, from Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd, who heard Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ.
The singular of liqāḥ is laqḥah: a milk-producing she-camel that has recently given birth.

9026 — Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned during the year of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, and Sūrat al-Fatḥ was revealed to him between Makkah and Madinah. In it, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, granted him Khaybar: “Allah has promised you abundant spoils that you will obtain, and He has hastened this for you” [al-Fatḥ 48:20].
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached Madinah in Dhū al-Ḥijjah and remained there until he set out for Khaybar in Muḥarram. He camped at al-Rajīʿ, a valley between Khaybar and Ghaṭafān, fearing that Ghaṭafān might reinforce the people of Khaybar. He spent the night there and advanced against them the following morning.
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, who said: al-Zuhrī narrated to me, from ʿUrwah, from Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah; through Ibn Isḥāq, al-Bayhaqī also narrated it in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (4/197).

9027 — ʿIrāk ibn Mālik al-Ghifārī reported that Abū Hurayrah arrived in Madinah with a group from his tribe while the Prophet ﷺ was at Khaybar, having appointed Sibāʿ ibn ʿUrfuṭah as his deputy over Madinah.
Abū Hurayrah said: I reached him while he was leading the dawn prayer. In the first unit he recited: “Kāf Hā Yā ʿAyn Ṣād” [Maryam 19:1], and in the second: “Woe to those who give short measure” [al-Muṭaffifīn 83:1].
I said to myself, “Woe to so-and-so! When he receives a measure, he takes it in full, but when he gives a measure, he gives less.”
After the prayer, Sibāʿ provided us with some provisions, and we travelled to Khaybar. The Prophet ﷺ had already conquered it. He spoke to the Muslims on our behalf, and they allowed us to share in their allotted portions.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8552) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2281), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1039) and al-Ḥākim (2/33); al-Bayhaqī also narrated it (9/40), all through Khuthaym ibn ʿIrāk, from his father ʿIrāk, from Abū Hurayrah.

9028 — Suwayd ibn al-Nuʿmān reported that he went with the Prophet ﷺ during the year of Khaybar. When they reached al-Ṣahbāʾ, near Khaybar, the Prophet ﷺ performed the afternoon prayer and called for provisions. Nothing was brought except sawīq, a preparation of roasted grain. He ordered that it be moistened, and he and the Companions ate it. He then rose for the sunset prayer, rinsed his mouth, and the Companions did likewise. He prayed without renewing his ablution.
Authentic: Mālik related it in al-Ṭahārah (51) from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from Bashīr ibn Yasār, the freedman of Banū Ḥārithah, from Suwayd ibn al-Nuʿmān, who related the report.

9029 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We set out with the Prophet ﷺ for Khaybar and travelled by night. A man asked ʿĀmir, “ʿĀmir, will you not recite some of your verses for us?” ʿĀmir was a camel-driver who chanted rhythmically, so he began chanting:
O Allah, had You not guided us, we would not be guided,
 nor would we give charity or pray.
 Forgive, as a ransom for You, the sins we have committed;
 send tranquillity down upon us,
 and make our feet firm when we meet the enemy.
 When we are called to battle, we stand firm,
 though with cries they have summoned forces against us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Who is this driver?”
They replied, “ʿĀmir ibn al-Akwaʿ.”
He said: “May Allah have mercy upon him.”
A man among the company said, “His martyrdom has now become certain, Prophet of Allah. If only you had allowed us to enjoy his company longer!”
We reached Khaybar and besieged its people until we suffered severe hunger. Allah, Exalted, then granted us victory over them. On the evening of the day of conquest, the people lit many fires.
The Prophet ﷺ asked: “What are these fires? What are you cooking?”
They replied, “Meat.”
He asked: “What kind of meat?”
They replied, “The meat of domesticated donkeys.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Pour it away and break the pots.”
A man asked, “Messenger of Allah, may we pour it away and wash the pots instead?”
He replied: “You may do that.”
When the two forces formed their battle lines, ʿĀmir, whose sword was short, attempted to strike a Jewish man’s leg. The tip of his sword rebounded and struck the artery at his own knee, causing his death.
As the Muslims returned, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw me and took my hand. He asked: “What is troubling you?”
I replied, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! They claim that ʿĀmir’s deeds have become void.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Whoever said that has lied. He will receive a double reward”—and he joined two fingers together—“for he was one who strove and exerted himself. Few Arabs have walked the earth like him.”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4196) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1802), both from Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ.

9030 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥah: “Why do you not urge our mounts onward with your chanting?”
He replied, “I have abandoned composing poetry.”
ʿUmar said to him, “Listen and obey.” He then chanted:
O Allah, had You not guided us, we would not be guided,
 nor would we give charity or pray.
 Send tranquillity down upon us,
 and make our feet firm when we meet the enemy.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “O Allah, have mercy upon him.”
ʿUmar said, “It has become certain.”
Authentic: Al-Nasāʾī related it in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (8193) from Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad, from Muḥammad ibn Mūsā ibn Aʿyan, from Ibn Idrīs, from Ismāʿīl, from Qays, from ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
The Prophet ﷺ set out for Khaybar with the Companions of al-Ḥudaybiyyah and did not permit those who had remained behind at al-Ḥudaybiyyah to accompany him, because Allah, Exalted, said:
“Those who remained behind will say when you set out to take the spoils, ‘Allow us to follow you.’ They wish to change the word of Allah. Say, ‘You will never follow us. Allah said so beforehand.’ They will say, ‘Rather, you envy us.’ In fact, they understand only a little” [al-Fatḥ 48:15].
Allah, Glorified and Exalted, promised the spoils of Khaybar exclusively to the people of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, without allowing the Bedouins who had remained behind to share in them. The words, “They wish to change the word of Allah” [al-Fatḥ 48:15], refer to the promise Allah had made to the people of al-Ḥudaybiyyah.

9031 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal reported: While we were besieging Khaybar, someone threw down a leather bag containing fat. I sprang forward to take it, but then turned and saw the Prophet ﷺ, so I felt embarrassed.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4214) and Muslim in al-Jihād (1772:72), both through Shuʿbah, from Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal.

9032 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ performed the dawn prayer near Khaybar while it was still dark, then said: “Allah is the Greatest! Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
The inhabitants came running through the streets. The Prophet ﷺ killed their fighting men and took their women and children captive. The remainder of the report follows.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4200) and Muslim in al-Nikāḥ (1365:85), both through Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Thābit, from Anas.

9033 — Anas reported: The Prophet ﷺ reached Khaybar early in the morning, as its people came out carrying their spades. When they saw him, they cried, “Muḥammad and the army! Muḥammad and the army!” They turned and ran toward the fortress.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hands, proclaimed Allah’s greatness three times and said: “Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
We captured some domesticated donkeys that had come out of the settlement and cooked their meat. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and His Messenger forbid you to eat domesticated donkeys, for they are an impurity from the work of Satan.”
Authentic: Aḥmad related it (12086) from Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah, from Ayyūb, from Muḥammad, from Anas. Al-Bukhārī also related it in al-Jihād (2991) and Muslim in al-Ṣayd wa-l-Dhabāʾiḥ (1940) through other routes from Ayyūb with some wording abridged.

9034 — Anas reported: When the Prophet ﷺ reached Khaybar, he encountered its people as they were going out to their fields carrying their spades. When they saw him with the army, they retreated to their fortress.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Allah is the Greatest! Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
Authentic: Aḥmad related it (12671) from ʿAbd al-Razzāq—and it is in his Tafsīr (2/159)—from Maʿmar, from Qatādah, from Anas.

9035 — Abū Ṭalḥah reported: When the Prophet of Allah ﷺ reached Khaybar in the morning, its people had taken their spades and gone out to their cultivated lands. When they saw the Prophet of Allah ﷺ with the army, they turned and fled.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “Allah is the Greatest! Allah is the Greatest! When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
Authentic: Aḥmad (16347) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (5/99–100) both related it through Saʿīd ibn Abī ʿArūbah, from Qatādah, from Anas, from Abū Ṭalḥah.

9036 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ campaigned against Khaybar. We performed the dawn prayer near it while it was still dark. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ mounted, and Abū Ṭalḥah mounted with me riding behind him.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ rode rapidly through a narrow passage in Khaybar, and my knee touched his thigh. His lower garment shifted from his thigh, and I could see the whiteness of the Prophet’s ﷺ thigh.
When he entered the settlement, he said: “Allah is the Greatest! Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.” He repeated it three times.
The inhabitants had gone out to their work and cried, “Muḥammad!” ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: Some of our companions added, “And the army!” We conquered Khaybar by force.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Ṣalāh (371) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1365:12), both through Ismāʿīl ibn ʿUlayyah, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb, from Anas. This wording is Muslim’s; al-Bukhārī’s is longer.
Al-Khamīs means an army, so called because it is traditionally divided into five sections: the vanguard, the rearguard, the right and left wings, and the centre.

9037 — Anas reported: On the day of Khaybar, I rode behind Abū Ṭalḥah, and my foot touched the foot of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We reached the inhabitants just as the sun rose. They had brought out their livestock and gone out carrying axes, baskets and spades. They cried, “Muḥammad and the army!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.” Allah, Mighty and Majestic, defeated them.
A beautiful captive woman fell to the share of Diḥyah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ acquired her in exchange for seven captives and entrusted her to Umm Sulaym to prepare her for him. I believe he also said that she was to observe her waiting period in Umm Sulaym’s house. She was Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ held her wedding feast with dates, dried curds and clarified butter. Hollows were dug in the ground and leather sheets were spread within them. The dried curds and butter were brought, and the people ate their fill.
The people said, “We do not know whether he has married her or taken her as a bondwoman who may bear him a child. If he screens her, she is his wife; if he does not, she is a bondwoman.”
When he prepared to depart, he screened her, and she sat upon the rear of the camel. They therefore knew that he had married her.
As they approached Madinah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ urged his mount forward, and we did likewise. Al-ʿAḍbāʾ stumbled, throwing both the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Ṣafiyyah. He immediately stood and covered her.
The women looked on and said, “May Allah keep the Jewish woman far away!”
The narrator said: I asked, “Abū Ḥamzah, did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ really fall?”
He replied, “Yes, by Allah, he did.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4200) and Muslim in al-Nikāḥ (1365:87), both through Thābit, from Anas. This wording is Muslim’s; al-Bukhārī’s is abridged, though he records it in many places.

9038 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached Khaybar at night. Whenever he reached a people at night, he would not attack them until morning.
At daybreak, the Jewish inhabitants came out carrying their spades and baskets. When they saw him, they cried, “Muḥammad, by Allah! Muḥammad and the army!”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
Authentic: Mālik related it in al-Jihād (48) from Ḥumayd al-Ṭawīl, from Anas. Al-Bukhārī also related it in al-Maghāzī (4197) from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Yūsuf, from Mālik, with the same report.

9039 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ campaigned against Khaybar—or when he set out toward it—the people overlooked a valley and raised their voices, proclaiming: “Allah is the Greatest! Allah is the Greatest! There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Be gentle with yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. You are calling upon One who hears and is near, and He is with you.”
I was behind the mount of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he heard me saying, “There is no power or might except through Allah.”
He said: “ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qays.”
I replied, “At your service, Messenger of Allah.”
He asked: “Shall I direct you to a phrase from one of the treasures of Paradise?”
I replied, “Yes, Messenger of Allah; may my father and mother be sacrificed for you.”
He said: “‘There is no power or might except through Allah.’”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4202) and Muslim in al-Dhikr wa-l-Duʿāʾ (2704:44), both through ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal, from Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī, from Abū Mūsā.

9040 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: ʿAlī, may Allah be pleased with him, initially remained behind when the Prophet ﷺ went to Khaybar because he was suffering from inflamed eyes. He then said, “Should I remain behind the Prophet ﷺ?” and caught up with him.
On the night before the conquest, the Prophet ﷺ said: “Tomorrow I shall certainly give the banner—or the banner will certainly be taken—by a man whom Allah and His Messenger love, and victory will be granted through him.”
We all hoped to receive it, but then it was said, “Here is ʿAlī.” The Prophet ﷺ gave him the banner, and victory was granted through him.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4209) and Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2407:35), both through Hāshim ibn Ismāʿīl, from Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd, from Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ.

9041 — Sahl ibn Saʿd reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said on the day of Khaybar: “Tomorrow I shall give this banner to a man through whose hands Allah will grant victory. He loves Allah and His Messenger, and Allah and His Messenger love him.”
The people spent the night discussing which of them would receive it. The following morning, they all went early to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, each hoping to be given the banner.
He asked: “Where is ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib?”
They replied, “Messenger of Allah, his eyes are hurting.”
He said: “Send for him.”
ʿAlī was brought, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spat into his eyes and supplicated for him. He was cured as though he had never suffered any pain, and the Prophet ﷺ gave him the banner.
ʿAlī asked, “Messenger of Allah, shall I fight them until they become like us?”
He replied: “Proceed calmly until you reach their territory. Then invite them to Islam and inform them of the rights of Allah that become binding upon them within it. By Allah, for Allah to guide one man through you is better for you than possessing the finest red camels.”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4210) and Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2406:34), both from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from Yaʿqūb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Abū Ḥāzim, from Sahl ibn Saʿd.

9042 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ reported: I heard the Prophet ﷺ say on the day of Khaybar: “I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger, and whom Allah and His Messenger love.”
We stretched forward, hoping to receive it. He said: “Call ʿAlī for me.”
ʿAlī was brought while suffering from inflamed eyes. The Prophet ﷺ spat into his eyes and gave him the banner, and Allah granted victory through him.
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Faḍāʾil (2404:32) through routes from Ḥātim ibn Ismāʿīl, from Bukayr ibn Mismār, from ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, from his father, within a longer report recorded in its proper place.

9043 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Tomorrow I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger, and through whose hands Allah will grant victory.”
ʿUmar said: “I never desired leadership except on that day. I made myself conspicuous, hoping that I would be called to receive it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, gave him the banner and said: “Go forward and do not turn around until Allah grants you victory.”
ʿAlī walked some distance and then stopped without turning around. He called aloud, “Messenger of Allah, for what cause shall I fight the people?”
He replied: “Fight them until they testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah. Once they do so, their lives and property are protected from you except when lawfully due, and their reckoning rests with Allah.”
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Faḍāʾil (2405) from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from Yaʿqūb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qāriʾ, from Suhayl, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah.

9044 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ related a lengthy report containing the following:
By Allah, we remained only three nights before setting out for Khaybar with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My uncle ʿĀmir began chanting to the company:
By Allah, had Allah not guided us, we would not be guided,
 nor would we give charity or pray.
 We can never dispense with Your favour;
 make our feet firm when we meet the enemy,
 and send tranquillity down upon us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Who is this?”
He replied, “I am ʿĀmir.”
He said: “May your Lord forgive you.”
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ specifically prayed for someone’s forgiveness in this manner, that person attained martyrdom. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who was riding a camel, called out, “Prophet of Allah, if only you had allowed us to enjoy ʿĀmir’s company longer!”
When we reached Khaybar, their champion Marḥab came out, strutting with his sword and chanting:
Khaybar knows that I am Marḥab,
 fully armed, a tested warrior,
 when wars advance in blazing fury.
My uncle ʿĀmir came out against him and chanted:
Khaybar knows that I am ʿĀmir,
 fully armed, a daring warrior.
They exchanged two blows. Marḥab’s sword struck ʿĀmir’s shield, and ʿĀmir attempted to strike him from below. His sword rebounded upon himself and severed the artery in his forearm, causing his death.
Salamah said: I came upon several Companions of the Prophet ﷺ saying, “ʿĀmir’s deeds have become void; he killed himself.”
I went to the Prophet ﷺ weeping and asked, “Messenger of Allah, have ʿĀmir’s deeds become void?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Who said that?”
I replied, “Some of your Companions.”
He said: “Whoever said that has lied. Rather, he will receive a double reward.”
He then sent me to fetch ʿAlī, who was suffering from inflamed eyes, saying: “I shall give the banner to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger—or whom Allah and His Messenger love.”
I went to ʿAlī and led him by the hand because of his eyes until I brought him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He spat into his eyes, and they were cured. He then gave him the banner.
Marḥab came out chanting:
Khaybar knows that I am Marḥab,
 fully armed, a tested warrior,
 when wars advance in blazing fury.
ʿAlī replied:
I am the one whose mother named him Ḥaydarah,
 like a lion of the forests, fearsome to behold;
 I repay them measure for measure, in a mighty measure.
ʿAlī struck Marḥab on the head and killed him, and victory was then achieved at his hands.
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1807:132) through ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Iyās ibn Salamah, from his father. The report is given in full in the al-Ḥudaybiyyah section.

9045 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ contracted with the people of Khaybar to cultivate its land in return for half of all the fruit and crops it produced.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Ḥarth wa-l-Muzāraʿah (2329) and Muslim in al-Musāqāh (1551:1), both through Yaḥyā al-Qaṭṭān, from ʿUbayd Allāh, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar. The wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9046 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: When Khaybar was conquered, its Jewish inhabitants asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to allow them to remain and cultivate the land in return for half of its fruit and crops.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “We shall allow you to remain upon these terms for as long as we wish.”
The report also states that the produce from half of Khaybar was distributed according to the allotted shares, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took one-fifth.
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Musāqāh (1551:4) from Abū al-Ṭāhir, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Wahb, from Usāmah ibn Zayd, from Nāfiʿ, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar.

9047 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb expelled the Jews and Christians from the land of the Ḥijāz.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Khaybar, he intended to expel its Jewish inhabitants. Upon its conquest, the land belonged to Allah, His Messenger and the Muslims. When the Prophet ﷺ sought to remove them, they asked him to allow them to remain and perform its agricultural work in return for half the produce.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “We shall allow you to remain upon these terms for as long as we wish.”
They remained there until ʿUmar expelled them to Taymāʾ and Jericho.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Ḥarth wa-l-Muzāraʿah (2338) and Muslim in al-Musāqāh (1551:6), both through ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Ibn Jurayj, from Mūsā ibn ʿUqbah, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar.

9048 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: When the people of Khaybar assaulted ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar and dislocated his limbs, ʿUmar stood to deliver a sermon and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ contracted with the Jewish inhabitants of Khaybar concerning their lands and said: ‘We shall allow you to remain for as long as Allah allows you to remain.’
“ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar went to his property there and was attacked at night, leaving his hands and feet dislocated. We have no enemy there other than them. They are our enemy and the ones we suspect, and I have therefore decided to expel them.”
When ʿUmar resolved upon this, a man from Banū Abī al-Ḥuqayq came to him and said, “Commander of the Faithful, will you expel us even though Muḥammad ﷺ allowed us to remain, contracted with us concerning the land and guaranteed that condition to us?”
ʿUmar replied, “Did you imagine that I had forgotten the words of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: ‘What will your condition be when you are expelled from Khaybar and your young she-camel carries you onward night after night?’”
The man said, “That was merely a jest from Abū al-Qāsim.”
ʿUmar replied, “You have lied, enemy of Allah.”
ʿUmar expelled them and compensated them for the value of their share of the produce with money, camels and goods such as camel saddles, ropes and other items.
Authentic: Related by al-Bukhārī (2730).

9049 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ fought the people of Khaybar until he drove them into their fortress and gained control of their land, crops and date palms. They concluded a treaty with him stipulating that they would leave Khaybar, taking only what their mounts could carry, while all their gold and silver would belong to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He stipulated that they must neither conceal nor hide anything; if they did, they would no longer enjoy covenantal protection. They nevertheless concealed a leather bag containing the wealth and jewellery of Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab, which he had brought to Khaybar when Banū al-Naḍīr were expelled.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab’s uncle: “What happened to Ḥuyayy’s leather bag that he brought from al-Naḍīr?”
He replied, “Expenses and warfare consumed its contents.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “The time has been too short, and the wealth was too great for that.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ handed him over to al-Zubayr, who subjected him to physical pressure during interrogation. The man had previously entered a ruined building, so he said, “I saw Ḥuyayy moving around a ruin over here.” They searched the place and found the leather bag in the ruin.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ executed the two sons of Abū al-Ḥuqayq, one of whom was the husband of Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab. Because they had violated the agreement, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took their women and children captive and divided their property.
He intended to expel them from Khaybar, but they said, “Muḥammad, allow us to remain upon this land to cultivate and maintain it.” Neither the Messenger of Allah ﷺ nor his Companions had sufficient workers to maintain the land, nor were they free to undertake the work themselves.
He therefore allowed the people of Khaybar to remain on the condition that they receive half of every crop, date harvest and other produce, for as long as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wished.
ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥah used to visit them every year, estimate the harvest and make them liable for half. One year they complained to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about the strictness of his assessment and attempted to bribe him.
He said, “Enemies of Allah, would you feed me unlawful gain? I have come to you from the person dearest to me, while you are more hateful to me than an equivalent number of apes and swine. Yet neither my hatred of you nor my love for him will cause me to act unjustly toward you.”
They replied, “It is through such justice that the heavens and earth remain established.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ noticed a greenish bruise around Ṣafiyyah’s eyes and asked: “What is this mark around your eyes?”
She replied, “My head was resting in the lap of Ibn Abī al-Ḥuqayq while I slept, and I dreamed that the moon fell into my lap. When I told him, he struck me and said, ‘Do you desire the king of Yathrib?’”
She said: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was initially the most hateful of people to me, for my husband, father and brother had been killed. He continued explaining the circumstances to me, saying, ‘Your father incited the Arabs against me and did such-and-such,’ until those feelings left my heart.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to give each of his wives eighty wasqs of dates and twenty wasqs of barley every year.
During the caliphate of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, the people of Khaybar acted treacherously toward the Muslims and threw Ibn ʿUmar from the roof of a house. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said, “Whoever possesses a share in Khaybar should attend so that we may distribute it among them.” He then divided it among its Muslim shareholders.
Their leader said, “Do not expel us. Allow us to remain as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr allowed us to remain.”
ʿUmar said to him, “Do you imagine that I have forgotten the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ words to you: ‘What will your condition be when your mount carries you toward Syria, one day after another?’”
ʿUmar then divided Khaybar among the people of al-Ḥudaybiyyah who had participated in its conquest.
Authentic: Related by Abū Dāwūd (3006), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5199), and related by al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan (9/137–138).

9050 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar reported: Al-Zubayr, al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad and I travelled to our properties at Khaybar to inspect them. When we arrived, each of us went separately to his own property.
During the night, while I was asleep upon my bed, I was attacked and both my arms were dislocated at the elbows.
The following morning, my two companions were summoned to help me. They came and asked, “Who did this to you?”
I replied, “I do not know.”
They reset my arms and then brought me to ʿUmar. He said, “This is the work of the Jewish inhabitants.”
He then addressed the people, saying:
“People, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ contracted with the Jewish inhabitants of Khaybar on the condition that we could expel them whenever we wished. They have attacked ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar and dislocated his arms, as you have heard, in addition to their earlier attack upon an Anṣārī. We have no doubt that they were responsible, for we have no enemy there other than them.
“Whoever owns property in Khaybar should go to it, for I am expelling its Jewish inhabitants.”
He then expelled them.
Good: Abū Dāwūd related it (3007) from Imām Aḥmad, and it is in Aḥmad’s Musnad (90), from Yaʿqūb ibn Ibrāhīm, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, from Nāfiʿ, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar. The wording is Aḥmad’s; Abū Dāwūd abridged it.

9051 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: On the day of Khaybar, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ allotted two shares for a horse and one share for a foot soldier.
Nāfiʿ explained: A man who possessed a horse received three shares—two for the horse and one for himself. A man without a horse received one share.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4228) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1762:57), both through ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUmar, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar. The wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9052 — Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim reported: ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and I went to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “You gave Banū al-Muṭṭalib a share from the one-fifth of Khaybar but gave us nothing, even though our degree of kinship to you is the same.”
He replied: “Banū Hāshim and Banū al-Muṭṭalib are one and the same.”
Jubayr said: The Prophet ﷺ did not allot anything to Banū ʿAbd Shams or Banū Nawfal.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4229) from Yaḥyā ibn Bukayr, from al-Layth, from Yūnus, from Ibn Shihāb, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, from Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim.

9053 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī reported: We came to the Prophet ﷺ after he had conquered Khaybar. He allotted shares to us, although he gave no share to anyone else who had not witnessed the conquest.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4233) and Muslim (2502:169).
Regarding the words, “He gave no share to anyone else who had not witnessed the conquest,” Abū Mūsā meant that, apart from the people of the ship, no absent person received a formal share without the consent of those who had acquired the spoils. Abū Hurayrah and his companions were given something only with the willing approval of the Muslims. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/489).

9054 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: “By the One in whose hand is my soul, were it not that the later generations would otherwise be left destitute with nothing, I would distribute every territory conquered during my rule just as the Prophet ﷺ distributed Khaybar.
