Instead, I leave it as a common treasury for them, from which they may receive their shares.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4235) from Saʿīd ibn Abī Maryam, from Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar, from Zayd ibn Aslam, from his father, who heard ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, may Allah be pleased with him.
The word babbānan refers to destitute people who possess nothing.

9055 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ghanm reported: We were stationed on the frontier at the city of Qinnasrīn with Shuraḥbīl ibn al-Simṭ. When he conquered it, he acquired sheep and cattle. He distributed some of them among us and placed the remainder with the general spoils.
I later met Muʿādh ibn Jabal and told him about it. Muʿādh said: “We campaigned at Khaybar with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and acquired sheep there. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ distributed some of them among us and placed the remainder with the general spoils.”
Good: Abū Dāwūd related it (2707)—and through him al-Bayhaqī (9/60)—from Muḥammad ibn al-Muṣaffā, from Muḥammad ibn al-Mubārak, from Yaḥyā ibn Ḥamzah, from Abū ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, from ʿUbādah ibn Nusayy, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ghanm.

9056 — Sahl ibn Abī Ḥathmah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ divided Khaybar into two halves: one half was reserved for emergencies and other needs, while the other half was distributed among the Muslims in eighteen shares.
Authentic: Abū Dāwūd related it (3010) from al-Rabīʿ ibn Sulaymān al-Muʾadhdhin, from Asad ibn Mūsā, from Yaḥyā ibn Zakariyyā, from Sufyān, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from Bashīr ibn Yasār, from Sahl ibn Abī Ḥathmah.

9057 — The Mother of the Believers ʿĀʾishah reported that Fāṭimah, peace be upon her, the daughter of the Prophet ﷺ, sent a request to Abū Bakr for her inheritance from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: from the property Allah had granted him as fayʾ in Madinah and Fadak, and from what remained of the one-fifth of Khaybar.
Abū Bakr replied: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘We prophets are not inherited from; whatever we leave is charity. The family of Muḥammad ﷺ may only receive their sustenance from this property.’ By Allah, I shall not alter any part of the charity of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from the condition in which it existed during his lifetime. I shall administer it exactly as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ administered it.”
Abū Bakr therefore declined to give Fāṭimah any of that property. Fāṭimah became displeased with Abū Bakr concerning the matter and withdrew from speaking to him until she died. She lived for six months after the Prophet ﷺ.
When she died, her husband ʿAlī buried her at night without informing Abū Bakr of her death, and ʿAlī performed the funeral prayer over her.
ʿAlī held a position of respect among the people during Fāṭimah’s lifetime. After she died, he perceived a change in the people’s attitude toward him, so he sought reconciliation with Abū Bakr and wished to pledge allegiance to him. He had not given the pledge during those months.
He sent word to Abū Bakr: “Come to us, but let no one accompany you,” because he disliked the prospect of ʿUmar being present.
ʿUmar said, “By Allah, you must not enter upon them alone.”
Abū Bakr replied, “What could they possibly do to me? By Allah, I shall go to them.”
Abū Bakr entered upon them. ʿAlī pronounced the testimony of faith and said:
“We recognise your virtue and what Allah has granted you. We do not begrudge you any good that Allah has brought to you. However, you acted independently of us concerning the matter of leadership, while we believed that our kinship with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entitled us to some consideration.”
Abū Bakr’s eyes overflowed with tears.
When Abū Bakr spoke, he said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, maintaining ties with the relatives of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is dearer to me than maintaining ties with my own relatives.
“As for the disagreement that arose between us concerning this property, I have not fallen short in pursuing what is right. I have not omitted anything that I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ do with it; I have done exactly as he did.”
ʿAlī said to Abū Bakr, “I shall meet you this afternoon to give the pledge of allegiance.”
After Abū Bakr performed the noon prayer, he ascended the pulpit, pronounced the testimony of faith, mentioned ʿAlī’s situation and his delay in giving the pledge, and related the explanation ʿAlī had given him. He then asked Allah for forgiveness.
ʿAlī then pronounced the testimony of faith, affirmed the great rights of Abū Bakr and explained that his conduct had not arisen from jealousy toward Abū Bakr or from any denial of the distinction Allah had granted him. Rather, he said:
“We believed that we were entitled to some consideration in this matter, but it was decided without consulting us, and this caused us to feel hurt.”
The Muslims were pleased by this and said, “You have acted correctly.” The Muslims then drew close to ʿAlī again after he returned to the recognised course of unity and reconciliation.
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (4240, 4241) and Muslim (1759:52).

9058 — Abū Mūsā reported: News of the Prophet’s ﷺ migration reached us while we were in Yemen, so we set out to emigrate to him. I travelled with two of my brothers, of whom I was the youngest: one was Abū Burdah and the other Abū Ruhm. We were accompanied by either a little over fifty—or specifically fifty-three or fifty-two—men from my tribe.
We boarded a ship, but it carried us to the Negus in Abyssinia. There we met Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib and remained with him until we all travelled together and reached the Prophet ﷺ at the time he conquered Khaybar.
Some people used to say to us—the people of the ship—“We preceded you in emigration.”
Asmāʾ bint ʿUmays, who had arrived with us, visited Ḥafṣah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ. Asmāʾ had been among those who emigrated to the Negus.
ʿUmar entered upon Ḥafṣah while Asmāʾ was with her. When he saw Asmāʾ, he asked, “Who is this?”
Ḥafṣah replied, “Asmāʾ bint ʿUmays.”
ʿUmar asked, “Is this the Abyssinian woman—the woman who travelled by sea?”
Asmāʾ replied, “Yes.”
ʿUmar said, “We preceded you in emigration, so we have a greater claim to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ than you do.”
Asmāʾ became angry and replied, “No, by Allah! You were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: he fed those of you who were hungry and instructed those of you who were ignorant. Meanwhile, we lived in the distant and hostile land of Abyssinia—and we endured that for the sake of Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.”
“By Allah, I shall neither eat nor drink until I tell the Messenger of Allah ﷺ what you have said. We were persecuted and lived in fear. I shall mention this to the Prophet ﷺ and ask him about it. By Allah, I shall neither lie, distort your words nor add anything to them.”
When the Prophet ﷺ arrived, Asmāʾ said, “Prophet of Allah, ʿUmar said such-and-such.”
He asked: “What did you say to him?”
She related her response.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “He has no greater claim to me than you do. He and his companions have one emigration, while you—the people of the ship—have two emigrations.”
Asmāʾ said: “I saw Abū Mūsā and the people of the ship coming to me in successive groups to ask about this report. Nothing in the world brought them greater happiness or was more significant to them than what the Prophet ﷺ had said about them.”
Abū Burdah reported that Asmāʾ said: “I saw Abū Mūsā repeatedly asking me to relate this report to him again.”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4230, 4231) and Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2502, 2503:169), both from Muḥammad ibn al-ʿAlāʾ, from Abū Usāmah, from Burayd ibn ʿAbd Allāh, from Abū Burdah, from Abū Mūsā.

9059 — Abū Hurayrah reported: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Khaybar after its conquest and said, “Messenger of Allah, allot me a share.”
A man from the family of Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ said, “Do not allot him a share, Messenger of Allah.”
Abū Hurayrah said, “This is the man who killed Ibn Qawqal.”
The son of Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ replied, “How astonishing that a small wild animal should descend upon us from the summit of Ḍaʾn and reproach me for killing a Muslim man whom Allah honoured at my hands, while He did not disgrace me at his hands!”
The narrator said: I do not know whether the Prophet ﷺ allotted Abū Hurayrah a share or not.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (2827) from al-Ḥumaydī, from Sufyān, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿAnbasah ibn Saʿīd, from Abū Hurayrah.
“Ibn Qawqal” was al-Nuʿmān ibn Mālik ibn Thaʿlabah. Qawqal was a title given to Thaʿlabah. Al-Nuʿmān was martyred at Uḥud.
The man described as “one of the family of Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ” was Abān ibn Saʿīd.
His words, “a small wild animal descended upon us from the summit of Ḍaʾn,” use wabr—a small wild animal resembling a cat—as a derogatory reference to Abū Hurayrah.
“Descended upon us” means that he suddenly appeared among them. “The summit of Ḍaʾn” means its extremity. It has been said that Ḍaʾn refers to a mountain summit, since such places are commonly grazing grounds for sheep. It has also been said that Ḍān, without a hamzah, was a mountain belonging to Daws, the tribe of Abū Hurayrah. Another narration reads ḍāl, with an l instead of an n, referring to a wild lote tree.
The statement, “Allah honoured him at my hands,” means that al-Nuʿmān ibn Mālik ibn Qawqal was killed by Abān and thereby honoured by Allah with martyrdom. Abān himself was not killed while still a disbeliever—which would have disgraced him by causing him to enter the Fire—but lived until he repented and embraced Islam.
The speaker who said, “I do not know whether he allotted him a share,” was Ibn ʿUyaynah.

9060 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Abān ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ as commander of a detachment from Madinah toward Najd. Abān and his companions arrived before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Khaybar after its conquest, with their horses’ girths made of palm fibre.
Abān said, “Messenger of Allah, allot us shares.”
Abū Hurayrah said, “Messenger of Allah, do not allot them shares.”
Abān replied, “You again, little wild animal, descending upon us from the summit of Ḍāl!”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Sit down, Abān.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not allot them shares.
Good: Related by Abū Dāwūd (2723) and Saʿīd ibn Manṣūr in his Sunan (2/285).

9061 — ʿUmayr, the freedman of Ābī al-Laḥm, reported: I witnessed Khaybar with my masters. They spoke to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning me and informed him that I was enslaved.
He ordered that I be equipped with a sword, but it was so long for me that I dragged it along the ground. He then ordered that I be given some miscellaneous household goods.
I also presented to him an incantation that I used when treating those afflicted with insanity. He instructed me to omit part of it and retain the remainder.
Another narration states: “He gave me some miscellaneous household goods but did not allot me a formal share.”
Authentic: Related by Abū Dāwūd (2730), al-Tirmidhī (1557), Ibn Mājah (2855) and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (7493).
“Miscellaneous household goods” refers to ordinary domestic items such as cooking pots and similar objects.

9062 — Abū Laylā reported: I witnessed the conquest of Khaybar with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When its people were defeated, we entered their encampment, and the Muslims took whatever ordinary goods they found. Before long, the cooking pots began to boil.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that the pots be overturned. He then distributed the animals among us, allotting one sheep to every ten people.
Authentic: Related by Aḥmad (19058), al-Dārimī (2513) and al-Ḥākim (2/134). The wording is that of Aḥmad.

9063 — Thābit ibn al-Ḥārith al-Anṣārī reported: On the day of Khaybar, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave something from the spoils to Sahlah bint ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAdī and to a daughter who had been born to her.
Good: Related by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (2/75).
It is also established in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim (1812) that the Prophet ﷺ gave something from the spoils to the women who treated the wounded, although he did not assign them formal shares.

9064 — ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal reported: We were besieging a fortress at Khaybar when someone threw down a leather bag containing fat. I sprang forward to take it, but then turned and saw the Prophet ﷺ, so I felt embarrassed before him.
Another wording states: “I turned and saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiling.”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in Farḍ al-Khums (3153) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1772), both through Ḥumayd ibn Hilāl, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mughaffal.

9065 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: During our military expeditions, we would acquire honey and grapes. We would eat them without submitting them for inclusion among the collected spoils.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in Farḍ al-Khums (3154) from Musaddad, from Ḥammād ibn Zayd, from Ayyūb, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar.

9066 — Muḥammad ibn Abī al-Mujālid reported: The people of the mosque sent me to Ibn Abī Awfā to ask him what the Prophet ﷺ had done with the food acquired at Khaybar.
I went to him and asked, “Did he take one-fifth of it?”
He replied, “No, it was too insignificant for that. Whenever one of us needed some of it, he took what he required.”
Another wording states: “A man would come, take the amount sufficient for his needs and then leave.”
Authentic: Abū Dāwūd (2704) and Aḥmad (19124) related it, and al-Ḥākim authenticated it (2/126), through two routes from Abū Isḥāq al-Shaybānī, from Muḥammad ibn Abī al-Mujālid.

9067 — ʿĀʾishah reported: When Khaybar was conquered, we said, “Now we shall eat our fill of dates.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4242) from Muḥammad ibn Bashshār, from Ḥarmī, from Shuʿbah, from ʿUmārah, from ʿIkrimah, from ʿĀʾishah.

9068 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: We did not eat our fill until we conquered Khaybar.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4243) from al-Ḥasan, from Murrah ibn Ḥabīb, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dīnār, from his father, from Ibn ʿUmar.

9069 — Anas reported: When the Emigrants arrived in Madinah from Makkah, they possessed nothing, whereas the Anṣār owned the land and date-palm properties. The Anṣār therefore entered into an arrangement with them: they would give the Emigrants half the annual produce of their properties, while undertaking the labour and expenses themselves.
The mother of Anas, known as Umm Sulaym, was also the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Ṭalḥah, Anas’s maternal brother. She gave the Messenger of Allah ﷺ some date palms of hers, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave them to his freedwoman Umm Ayman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd.
Ibn Shihāb said: Anas ibn Mālik informed me that after the Prophet ﷺ had completed the campaign against the people of Khaybar and returned to Madinah, the Emigrants returned to the Anṣār the grants of produce that the Anṣār had previously given them.
The Prophet ﷺ returned the date palms of Anas’s mother to her and gave Umm Ayman replacement palms from his own orchard.
Ibn Shihāb said: Umm Ayman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd, had been a young female servant belonging to ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, and she was originally from Abyssinia.
When Āminah gave birth to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after his father’s death, Umm Ayman cared for him and helped raise him until he grew up. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ later emancipated her and arranged her marriage to Zayd ibn Ḥārithah.
She died five months after the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Hibah (2630) and Muslim in al-Jihād wa-l-Siyar (1771:70), both through Ibn Wahb, from Yūnus, from Ibn Shihāb, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9070 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ campaigned against Khaybar. We performed the dawn prayer near it while it was still dark. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ mounted, and Abū Ṭalḥah mounted with me riding behind him.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ rode rapidly through a narrow passage in Khaybar, and my knee touched his thigh. His lower garment shifted from his thigh, and I could see the whiteness of the Prophet’s ﷺ thigh.
When he entered the settlement, he said: “Allah is the Greatest! Khaybar is ruined. When we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.” He repeated it three times.
The inhabitants had gone out to their work and cried, “Muḥammad, by Allah!”
ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz said: Some of our companions added, “Muḥammad and the army!”
He said: We conquered Khaybar by force, and the captives were assembled. Diḥyah came and said, “Messenger of Allah, give me a captive woman.”
He replied: “Go and take a captive woman.” Diḥyah selected Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy.
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “Prophet of Allah, have you given Diḥyah Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy, the leading woman of Banū Qurayẓah and Banū al-Naḍīr? She is suitable for no one but you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Call him and have him bring her.”
Diḥyah brought her. When the Prophet ﷺ saw her, he told him: “Take another captive woman instead.”
The Prophet ﷺ emancipated Ṣafiyyah and married her.
Thābit asked Anas, “Abū Ḥamzah, what did he give her as a bridal gift?”
He replied, “Her own freedom: he emancipated her and married her.”
While they were travelling, Umm Sulaym prepared Ṣafiyyah for the Prophet ﷺ and presented her to him during the night. The following morning, the Prophet ﷺ was a bridegroom and said: “Whoever has any food should bring it.”
He spread out a leather sheet. One man brought dried curds, another brought dates and another brought clarified butter. They mixed them together to prepare ḥays. This became the wedding feast of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Ṣalāh (371) and Muslim in al-Nikāḥ (1365:84), both through Ismāʿīl ibn ʿUlayyah, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb, from Anas.

9071 — Anas reported: The Prophet ﷺ took Ṣafiyyah among the captives, emancipated her and married her.
Thābit asked Anas, “What did he give her as a bridal gift?”
He replied, “He gave her her own freedom as the bridal gift by emancipating her.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4201) from Ādam, from Shuʿbah, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb, who heard Anas ibn Mālik.

9072 — Anas reported: We reached Khaybar, and when Allah granted the Prophet ﷺ victory over the fortress, he was informed of the beauty of Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab. Her husband had been killed, and she was a newlywed.
The Prophet ﷺ selected her for himself and departed with her. When we reached the embankment of al-Ṣahbāʾ, she became ritually eligible for marriage, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ consummated the marriage with her.
He then prepared ḥays upon a small leather sheet and told me: “Invite those around you.” That was his wedding feast for Ṣafiyyah.
We then departed for Madinah. I saw the Prophet ﷺ make a screened space for her behind him using a cloak. He would sit beside his camel and offer his knee, and Ṣafiyyah would place her foot upon it in order to mount.
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4211) through two routes from Yaʿqūb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Zuhrī, from ʿAmr, the freedman of al-Muṭṭalib, from Anas.

9073 — Anas reported: Ṣafiyyah initially fell to Diḥyah as part of his allotted share. The people began praising her before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, saying, “We have never seen anyone among the captives like her.”
The Prophet ﷺ sent for Diḥyah and compensated him with what he desired. He then entrusted Ṣafiyyah to my mother and said: “Prepare her.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then departed from Khaybar. When he had left Khaybar behind him, he stopped and pitched a tent for her.
The following morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Whoever possesses any surplus provisions should bring them to us.”
The men began bringing their surplus dates and roasted grain until they had assembled a considerable quantity and made ḥays from it.
They ate the ḥays and drank rainwater from pools beside them.
Anas said: That was the wedding feast the Messenger of Allah ﷺ held for Ṣafiyyah.
We continued travelling until we saw the walls of Madinah. We became eager to reach it and urged our mounts forward. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also urged his mount onward, with Ṣafiyyah seated behind him.
The mount of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stumbled, throwing both him and Ṣafiyyah to the ground.
None of the people looked toward either of them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood and covered Ṣafiyyah.
When we approached him, he said: “We have suffered no harm.”
When we entered Madinah, the young women belonging to his wives came out to look at Ṣafiyyah and expressed satisfaction at hearing that she had fallen.
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Nikāḥ (1365:88) through routes from Sulaymān ibn al-Mughīrah, from Thābit, from Anas.

9074 — Abū Hurayrah reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ consummated his marriage with Ṣafiyyah, Abū Ayyūb spent the night guarding the Prophet’s ﷺ doorway.
When morning came and he saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he proclaimed Allah’s greatness. Abū Ayyūb was carrying his sword.
He said, “Messenger of Allah, she was a young woman who had only recently been married, and her father, brother and husband had been killed. I therefore did not feel that you were safe from her.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and spoke words of goodness to him.
Good: Al-Ḥākim related it (4/28–29) and authenticated it through ʿAbd Allāh ibn Isḥāq al-Khurāsānī, from Yaḥyā ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zibriqān, from ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn ʿAṭāʾ, from Khālid al-Ḥadhdhāʾ, from Kathīr ibn Zayd, from al-Walīd ibn Rabāḥ, from Abū Hurayrah.

9075 — Anas reported: Ṣafiyyah learned that Ḥafṣah had called her “the daughter of a Jew.”
The Prophet ﷺ entered upon Ṣafiyyah while she was weeping and asked: “What has made you cry?”
She replied, “Ḥafṣah said that I am the daughter of a Jew.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “You are indeed descended from a prophet, your uncle was a prophet, and you are married to a prophet. What, then, does she have to boast about over you?”
He then said: “Be mindful of Allah, Ḥafṣah.”
Authentic: Al-Tirmidhī (3894), Aḥmad (12392), and Ibn Ḥibbān (7211) all related it through ʿAbd al-Razzāq—and it is in his Muṣannaf (20921)—from Maʿmar, from Thābit, from Anas.

9076 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entrusted the land of Khaybar to its cultivators in return for half of its fruit and crops. He used to give each of his wives one hundred wasqs every year: eighty wasqs of dates and twenty wasqs of barley.
When ʿUmar became caliph, he divided Khaybar. He gave the wives of the Prophet ﷺ a choice: either portions of land and water rights would be assigned to them, or their annual quantities of produce would continue to be guaranteed.
They made different choices. Some selected land and water rights, while others selected the guaranteed annual produce. ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah were among those who selected land and water rights.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Ḥarth wa-l-Muzāraʿah (2328) and Muslim in al-Musāqāh wa-l-Muzāraʿah (1551:2), both through ʿUbayd Allāh, from Nāfiʿ, from Ibn ʿUmar.

9077 — Abū Hurayrah reported: When Khaybar was conquered, the Prophet ﷺ was presented with a sheep containing poison.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Gather before me all the Jewish people who are present here.” They were assembled before him.
He said: “I shall ask you about something. Will you answer me truthfully?”
They replied, “Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked them: “Who is your forefather?”
They named a certain man.
He said: “You have lied. Rather, your forefather is so-and-so.”
They replied, “You have spoken the truth.”
He asked: “Will you answer me truthfully if I ask you about something else?”
They replied, “Yes, Abū al-Qāsim. If we lie, you will recognise our lie just as you recognised it concerning our forefather.”
He asked them: “Who are the inhabitants of the Fire?”
They replied, “We shall remain in it for only a short time, after which you will take our place there.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Remain there despised! By Allah, we shall never take your place in it.”
He then asked: “Will you answer me truthfully if I ask you about something?”
They replied, “Yes, Abū al-Qāsim.”
He asked: “Did you place poison in this sheep?”
They replied, “Yes.”
He asked: “What caused you to do that?”
They replied, “We intended that, if you were a liar, we would be rid of you; but if you were truly a prophet, it would not harm you.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Jizyah wa-l-Muwādaʿah (3169), from ʿAbdullāh ibn Yūsuf, from al-Layth, who said: Saʿīd narrated to me, from Abū Hurayrah.

9078 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that a Jewish woman brought the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a poisoned sheep, and he ate some of it.
The woman was brought before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he questioned her about it. She said, “I intended to kill you.”
He replied: “Allah would never grant you power to accomplish that”—or he said: “power over me.”
The Companions asked, “Shall we kill her?”
He replied: “No.”
Anas said: I continued to recognise the effects of the poison upon the palate of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Hibah (2617) and Muslim in al-Salām (2190), both through Khālid ibn al-Ḥārith, from Shuʿbah, from Hishām ibn Zayd, from Anas.
This woman was Zaynab bint al-Ḥārith, the sister of Marḥab the Jewish champion. Some reports state that she was later executed because Bishr ibn al-Barāʾ ibn Maʿrūr had also eaten from the poisoned sheep. When he died, she was handed over to his heirs, who executed her.
This was mentioned by al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ, as al-Nawawī stated in his commentary on Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim. This means that she was not executed initially, but when Bishr ibn al-Barāʾ died, her execution was ordered.

9079 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that a Jewish woman presented the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with a poisoned sheep. He summoned her and asked: “What caused you to do what you did?”
She replied, “I wished—or intended—that if you were a prophet, Allah would inform you of it; but if you were not a prophet, I would rid the people of you.”
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ experienced any effect from the poison, he underwent cupping. On one journey, after entering the state of consecration, he felt some of its effects and underwent cupping.
Good: Imām Aḥmad related it (2784) from Surayj, from ʿAbbād, from Hilāl, from ʿIkrimah, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9080 — ʿĀʾishah reported that during the illness in which he died, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say: “ʿĀʾishah, I continue to feel the pain caused by the food I ate at Khaybar. Now is the time when I feel my aorta being severed by that poison.”
Good: Al-Ḥākim related it (3/58) from Abū Bakr Aḥmad ibn Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā al-Ashqar, from Yūsuf ibn Mūsā al-Marwazī, from Aḥmad ibn Ṣāliḥ, from ʿAnbasah, from Yūnus, from Ibn Shihāb, from ʿUrwah, from ʿĀʾishah.

9081 — Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd reported: I saw the scar of a blow upon Salamah’s leg and asked, “Abū Muslim, what caused this wound?”
He replied, “This blow struck me on the day of Khaybar. The people said, ‘Salamah has been wounded.’
“I went to the Prophet ﷺ, and he blew lightly upon the wound three times. I have not suffered any pain from it up to this very moment.”
Authentic: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4206) from al-Makkī ibn Ibrāhīm, from Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd.

9082 — Abū Hurayrah reported: We witnessed Khaybar. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said concerning a man in his company who claimed to be a Muslim: “This man is among the people of the Fire.”
When battle began, the man fought with exceptional intensity until he sustained numerous wounds. Some of the people nearly began to doubt the Prophet’s statement.
The man could no longer endure the pain of his wounds, so he reached into his quiver, removed some arrows and cut his own throat with them.
Several Muslims hurried to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Messenger of Allah, Allah has confirmed the truth of your statement. So-and-so committed suicide and killed himself.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Stand, so-and-so, and announce that none but a believer will enter Paradise. Allah may indeed support this religion through a wicked man.”
Agreed upon: Related by al-Bukhārī (3062, 4203) and Muslim in the Book of Faith (111:178).

9083 — Anas reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Khaybar, al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿIlāṭ said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have wealth and family in Makkah, and I wish to go to them. Will I be free of blame if I speak against you or say something?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ permitted him to say whatever was necessary.
When he arrived, he went to his wife and said, “Gather everything you have for me. I intend to purchase some of the spoils taken from Muḥammad and his Companions, for they have been defeated and their property seized.”
This news spread throughout Makkah. The Muslims became downcast, while the polytheists openly displayed joy and delight. When the news reached al-ʿAbbās, he was so distressed that he became unable to stand.
Maʿmar said: ʿUthmān al-Jazarī informed me, from Miqsam, who said: Al-ʿAbbās took one of his sons, named Qutham, lay down and placed him upon his chest, while saying:
My beloved Qutham,
 who resembles the man of the prominent nose—
 the Prophet, the possessor of blessings—
 whatever anyone may claim.
Thābit reported from Anas: Al-ʿAbbās then sent one of his servants to al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿIlāṭ, saying, “Woe to you! What news have you brought, and what is this you are saying? What Allah has promised is better than the news you have brought.”
Al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿIlāṭ told the servant, “Convey my greetings of peace to Abū al-Faḍl and tell him to arrange a private room for me to visit him, for the true news is something that will delight him.”
When the servant reached the door of the house, he called out, “Receive glad tidings, O Abū al-Faḍl!” Al-ʿAbbās leapt up joyfully and kissed him between the eyes. The servant told him what al-Ḥajjāj had said, so al-ʿAbbās set him free.
Al-Ḥajjāj then came to him and informed him that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had conquered Khaybar, captured its people’s wealth, and distributed their property according to the shares ordained by Allah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had chosen Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy for himself and offered her the choice of being set free and becoming his wife or returning to her people. She chose to be set free and become his wife.
He continued, “However, I came here because I have property in Makkah which I wished to gather and take away. I sought the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ permission, and he allowed me to say whatever was necessary. Keep my true news secret for three days; then disclose whatever you wish.”
His wife gathered all the jewellery and possessions she had and handed them over to him. He then departed swiftly with them.
After three days, al-ʿAbbās went to al-Ḥajjāj’s wife and asked, “What has your husband done?” She informed him that he had departed on such-and-such a day. She then said, “May Allah not cause you grief, O Abū al-Faḍl. We were deeply saddened by the news that reached you.”
He replied, “Indeed, may Allah not cause me grief! By Allah’s praise, nothing occurred except what we desired: Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ victory over Khaybar, its wealth was distributed according to Allah’s appointed shares, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ chose Ṣafiyyah for himself. If you have any need of your husband, then go and join him.”
She said, “By Allah, I believe you are telling the truth.” He replied, “I am indeed truthful, and the matter is exactly as I have told you.”
Al-ʿAbbās then went to the gathering places of Quraysh. Whenever he passed them, they would say mockingly, “May nothing but good befall you, O Abū al-Faḍl!”
He told them, “Nothing but good has befallen me, praise be to Allah! Al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿIlāṭ informed me that Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ victory over Khaybar, its wealth was distributed according to Allah’s appointed shares, and he chose Ṣafiyyah for himself. Al-Ḥajjāj asked me to conceal the news for three days. He came only to collect his wealth and whatever property he had here, and then depart.”
Thus Allah transferred the sorrow that had afflicted the Muslims to the polytheists. The Muslims who had withdrawn sorrowfully into their homes came out and went to al-ʿAbbās, who told them the news. The Muslims rejoiced, while the grief, anger and sorrow fell upon the polytheists.
Authentic: Related by Aḥmad (12409), Abū Yaʿlā (3479), al-Ṭabarānī (3/247), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1816), al-Bayhaqī (9/150), and Ibn Ḥibbān (4530), all through ʿAbd al-Razzāq—as in his Muṣannaf (9771)—from Maʿmar, from Thābit, from Anas.

9084 — Abū Hurayrah reported: We went with the Prophet ﷺ to Khaybar, and Allah granted us victory. We did not take gold or silver as spoils; rather, we took goods, food and clothing.
We then proceeded to the valley. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had with him a servant who had been given to him by a man from Judhām named Rifāʿah ibn Zayd, from Banū al-Ḍubayb.
When we stopped in the valley, the servant of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood up to unload his saddle. He was struck by an arrow, which caused his death.
We said, “May martyrdom be a blessing for him, O Messenger of Allah!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “No! By Him in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, the cloak he took from the spoils on the day of Khaybar before it was included in the distribution is blazing as fire upon him.”
The people became alarmed. A man then brought one or two leather sandal straps and said, “O Messenger of Allah, I took these on the day of Khaybar.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “A strap of fire—or two straps of fire.”
Agreed upon: Mālik related it in al-Jihād (25), from Thawr ibn Zayd al-Dīlī, from Sālim Abū al-Ghayth, the freedman of Ibn Muṭīʿ, from Abū Hurayrah. Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4234) and Muslim in al-Īmān (115), both through Mālik. The wording is Muslim’s.
Wādī al-Qurā was a collection of settlements situated between Khaybar and Taymāʾ. A community of Jews lived there, and a group of Arabs had joined them. When the Muslims stopped there, the Jews received them with volleys of arrows while they were unprepared.

9085 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb reported: On the day of Khaybar, a group of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ came forward, saying, “So-and-so is a martyr, and so-and-so is a martyr,” until they passed by a man and said, “So-and-so is a martyr.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “No. I saw him in the Fire because of a cloak—or an outer garment—that he had secretly taken from the spoils.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said, “O Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, go and proclaim among the people that none shall enter Paradise except the believers.”
ʿUmar said: So I went out and proclaimed, “Indeed, none shall enter Paradise except the believers.”
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Īmān (114) from Zuhayr ibn Ḥarb, from Hāshim ibn al-Qāsim, from ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Simāk al-Ḥanafī Abū Zumayl, from Ibn ʿAbbās, from ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
Al-Wāqidī said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ arranged his Companions in battle formation and set them in their ranks. He entrusted his standard to Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah and gave banners to al-Ḥubāb ibn al-Mundhir, Sahl ibn Ḥunayf and ʿAbbād ibn Bishr.
He then invited the people of Wādī al-Qurā to Islam and informed them that if they embraced it, their property would be protected, their blood would be spared and their reckoning would rest with Allah.
One of their men came forward to fight in single combat, so al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām advanced against him and killed him. Another man came forward, and ʿAlī advanced and killed him. Then another came forward, and Abū Dujānah advanced and killed him. This continued until eleven of their men had been killed.
Whenever one of their men was killed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ invited those who remained to embrace Islam.
Whenever the time for prayer arrived that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would lead his Companions in prayer. He would then return and invite them to Islam and to faith in Allah Almighty and His Messenger.
He fought them until evening. The following morning he advanced against them again, and before the sun had risen the height of a spear, they surrendered. Wādī al-Qurā was conquered by force, and Allah granted the Muslims their property as spoils. They acquired a great quantity of furnishings and other goods.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained at Wādī al-Qurā for four days and distributed the captured property among his Companions. He left the land and date palms in the hands of the Jews and entered into an agricultural agreement with them concerning it.
When the Jews of Taymāʾ learned what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had done at Khaybar, Fadak and Wādī al-Qurā, they concluded a treaty with him agreeing to pay the jizyah, and their property remained in their possession.
During the caliphate of ʿUmar, he expelled the Jews of Khaybar and Fadak, but he did not expel the people of Taymāʾ and Wādī al-Qurā, because those two places were regarded as part of the territory of al-Shām. He considered the region extending from the nearer side of Wādī al-Qurā toward Madinah to be part of the Ḥijāz, while the territory beyond it belonged to al-Shām.
He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then departed and returned to Madinah after completing the campaigns of Khaybar and Wādī al-Qurā and after Allah Almighty had granted him the spoils.
Al-Wāqidī’s Maghāzī (2/710–713).

9086 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We launched an expedition against Fazārah under the command of Abū Bakr, whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had appointed over us. When we were an hour’s journey from their water, Abū Bakr ordered us to stop and rest during the latter part of the night. He then launched the attack, reached the water and killed some of those present while taking others captive.
I saw a group of people that included women and children, and I feared they would reach the mountain before me. I therefore shot an arrow between them and the mountain. When they saw the arrow, they stopped, and I drove them ahead of me.
Among them was a woman from Banū Fazārah wearing a piece of tanned leather—he explained that al-qishʿ means a leather mat—and with her was a daughter who was among the most beautiful of the Arabs. I drove them onward until I brought them to Abū Bakr, and he awarded me her daughter as an additional share.
We returned to Madinah, and I had not uncovered her garment. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ met me in the marketplace and said, “O Salamah, give me the woman.”
I replied, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, she has greatly pleased me, but I have not uncovered her garment.”
The following day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met me again in the marketplace and said, “O Salamah, give me the woman—may Allah bless your father!”
I replied, “She is yours, O Messenger of Allah. By Allah, I have not uncovered her garment.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent her to the people of Makkah and used her to ransom several Muslims who had been held captive there.
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Jihād (1755) from Zuhayr ibn Ḥarb, from ʿUmar ibn Yūnus, from ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Iyās ibn Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ, from his father.

9087 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed Abū Bakr as our commander, and we launched an expedition against a group of polytheists. We attacked them by night and fought them. Our battle cry that night was: “Put them to death! Put them to death!” Salamah said: That night, I personally killed seven men from different households of the polytheists.
Authentic: Abū Dāwūd (2638, 2596), Aḥmad (16498), Ibn Ḥibbān (4744), and al-Ḥākim (2/107) related it through routes from ʿIkrimah ibn ʿAmmār, from Iyās ibn Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ, from his father.

9088 — Usāmah ibn Zayd reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us against al-Ḥuraqah. We attacked the people in the morning and defeated them. A man from the Anṣār and I caught up with one of their men. When we closed in upon him, he said, “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.”
The Anṣārī held back, but I stabbed the man with my spear and killed him.
When we returned, news of this reached the Prophet ﷺ. He said, “O Usāmah, did you kill him after he said: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah’?”
I replied, “He said it only to protect himself.”
He continued repeating this question until I wished that I had not embraced Islam before that day.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4269) and Muslim in al-Īmān (96:159), both through Hushaym, from Ḥuṣayn Abū Ẓabyān, from Usāmah ibn Zayd ibn Ḥārithah. Their wording is the same.
Muslim narrated it through another chain from Usāmah ibn Zayd, who said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us on an expedition. We attacked al-Ḥuraqāt, a clan of Juhaynah, in the morning. I caught up with a man who said, “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,” but I stabbed him. I then felt troubled by what I had done, so I mentioned it to the Prophet ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “Did he say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ and you still killed him?”
I replied, “O Messenger of Allah, he said it only out of fear of the weapon.”
He said, “Why did you not open his heart so that you could know whether he said it sincerely or not?”
He continued repeating this to me until I wished that I had embraced Islam only on that day.

9089 — It is reported from Jundab ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Bajalī: During the turmoil of Ibn al-Zubayr, he sent a message to ʿAsʿas ibn Salāmah saying, “Gather for me a group of your brothers so that I may speak to them.” He sent a messenger to them, and when they had assembled, Jundab came wearing a yellow hooded cloak. He said, “Continue discussing what you were discussing,” until it became his turn to speak.
When it became his turn, he removed the hood from his head and said: I have come to you, though I did not intend to relate to you something concerning your Prophet. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent a detachment of Muslims against a group of polytheists, and they met in battle.
There was a man among the polytheists who would single out any Muslim he wished, attack him and kill him. One of the Muslims waited for an opportunity to catch him unawares—and we used to say that this Muslim was Usāmah ibn Zayd. When he raised his sword over the polytheist, the man declared, “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,” but he killed him.
When the bearer of glad tidings came to the Prophet ﷺ, the Prophet questioned him, and he related what had happened, including what that man had done. The Prophet ﷺ summoned the man and asked him, “Why did you kill him?”
He replied, “O Messenger of Allah, he inflicted great harm upon the Muslims and killed so-and-so and so-and-so,” naming several people. “I attacked him, and when he saw the sword, he said: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah!’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “Did you kill him?”
He replied, “Yes.”
He said, “Then what will you do with ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah’ when it comes on the Day of Resurrection?”
He said, “O Messenger of Allah, seek forgiveness for me.”
He repeated, “What will you do with ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah’ when it comes on the Day of Resurrection?”
He continued saying nothing more to him than: “What will you do with ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah’ when it comes on the Day of Resurrection?”
Authentic: Muslim related it in al-Īmān (97:160) from Aḥmad ibn al-Ḥasan ibn Khirāsh, from ʿAmr ibn ʿĀṣim, from Maʿmar, from his father, from Khālid al-Athbaj, nephew of Ṣafwān ibn Muḥriz, from Ṣafwān ibn Muḥriz, from Jundab ibn ʿAbdullāh.

9090 — It is reported from Ibn ʿAbbās: The verse, “O you who believe, obey Allah, obey the Messenger and those in authority among you” [al-Nisāʾ 4:59], was revealed concerning ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah al-Sahmī, whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent in command of an expedition.
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Tafsīr (4584) and Muslim in al-Imārah (31:1834), both through Ḥajjāj ibn Muḥammad, from Ibn Jurayj, from Yaʿlā ibn Muslim, from Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9091 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent out an expedition and appointed a man from the Anṣār as its commander, ordering the men to obey him. The commander became angry and asked, “Did the Prophet ﷺ not command you to obey me?”
They replied, “Yes.”
He said, “Gather firewood for me.” They gathered it, and he said, “Light a fire.” They lit it, and he then commanded, “Enter it.”
They were about to do so, but then began restraining one another and saying, “We fled to the Prophet ﷺ to escape the Fire.”
They remained like that until the fire died down and the commander’s anger subsided. When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he said, “Had they entered it, they would not have emerged from it until the Day of Resurrection. Obedience is required only in what is right.”
Agreed upon: Al-Bukhārī related it in al-Maghāzī (4340) and Muslim in al-Imārah (40:1840), both through al-Aʿmash, from Saʿd ibn ʿUbaydah, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿAlī.

9092 — Abū Saʿīd reported: ʿAlqamah ibn Mujazziz was placed in command of an expedition in which I participated. When he reached the destination of our campaign—or while we were somewhere along the road—he gave a portion of the army permission to depart and appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah ibn Qays al-Sahmī as their commander. He was one of the participants at Badr and possessed a playful nature—that is, he liked to joke. I was among those who returned with him.
We stopped at a place along the road, and the people lit a fire, either to prepare some food or to warm themselves.
He asked them, “Do you not owe me hearing and obedience?” They replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Then whatever I command you to do, will you do it?” They replied, “Yes.”
He said, “By the obedience you owe me, I command you to leap into this fire.”
Some of them arose, while others held them back. When he thought they were about to leap into it, he said, “Restrain yourselves; I was only joking with you.”
When they returned, they mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “If any of those placed in authority over you commands you to disobey Allah, do not obey him.”
Good: Ibn Mājah (2863), Aḥmad (11639), Ibn Ḥibbān (4558), and al-Ḥākim (3/630–631) related it through Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAlqamah, from ʿUmar ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Thawbān, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī. The wording is Aḥmad’s.

9093 — Abū Mūsā reported: We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on a military expedition. We were six men sharing one camel, which we rode in turns.
Our feet became wounded. Both of my feet were torn and my toenails fell off. We used to wrap pieces of cloth around our feet, so it was called the Expedition of Dhāt al-Riqāʿ—“the Expedition of the Patches”—because of the pieces of cloth we bound around our feet.
Abū Burdah said: Abū Mūsā narrated this report, but afterward he disliked having done so. It was as though he disliked disclosing any of his own deeds.
Abū Usāmah said: Someone other than Burayd added for me: “Allah will reward him for it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4128) and Muslim (1816).

9094 — It is reported from Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh: He took part with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in an expedition toward Najd. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began the return journey, Jābir returned with him. The time of the midday rest overtook them in a valley containing many thorny trees.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted, and the people dispersed among the thorny trees, seeking shade beneath them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped beneath an acacia tree and hung his sword from it.
Jābir said: We fell asleep for a while, and then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called us. We came to him and found a Bedouin sitting beside him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “This man drew my sword while I was asleep. I awoke to find it unsheathed in his hand. He asked me, ‘Who will protect you from me?’ I replied, ‘Allah.’ Here he is now, sitting before you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not punish him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4135) and Muslim (13:843).
The expression “al-ʿiḍāh” refers to every type of thorny tree.
Regarding the words, “We found a Bedouin sitting beside him,” his name was Ghawrath ibn al-Ḥārith, as mentioned by al-Bukhārī (4136) in a suspended report.
Regarding the words, “Who will protect you from me? I replied: Allah,” it is stated in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim that he asked this twice.

9095 — Jābir reported: We travelled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until we reached Dhāt al-Riqāʿ. Whenever we came upon a tree that provided plentiful shade, we would leave it for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
A man from among the polytheists came while the sword of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was hanging from a tree. He took the Prophet’s ﷺ sword, drew it from its sheath and asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, “Do you fear me?”
He replied, “No.”
The man asked, “Then who will protect you from me?”
He replied, “Allah will protect me from you.”
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ then threatened the man, so he sheathed the sword and hung it back on the tree.
The call to prayer was then made. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed two units with one group, after which they withdrew, and he prayed two units with the other group.
Thus, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed four units of prayer, while each group performed two.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (311:843).

9096 — Ṣāliḥ ibn Khawwāt reported from one who performed the Prayer of Fear with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of Dhāt al-Riqāʿ: One group formed rows with him while another faced the enemy. He prayed one unit with the group accompanying him and then remained standing while they completed the prayer by themselves. They then withdrew and formed ranks facing the enemy. The other group came, and he prayed with them the remaining unit of his prayer. He then remained seated while they completed their prayer by themselves, after which he concluded the prayer with them by giving the salutation.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (1), al-Bukhārī (4129) and Muslim (310:842).
The unnamed Companion was Khawwāt ibn Jubayr, may Allah be pleased with him, Abū Ṣāliḥ. It has also been said that he was Sahl ibn Abī Ḥathmah. Al-Nawawī and Ibn Ḥajar considered the first view stronger. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/422).

9097 — It is reported from Sahl ibn Abī Ḥathmah: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led his Companions in the Prayer of Fear, arranging them in two rows behind him. He prayed one unit with those immediately behind him and then remained standing while they completed one unit by themselves. They then moved forward, and those who had previously been in front withdrew to the rear. He prayed one unit with them and then remained seated while those who had remained behind completed one unit by themselves. He then concluded the prayer with the salutation.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4131) and Muslim (309:841). The wording is Muslim’s.
Al-Bukhārī (4131) also narrated through another chain from Sahl ibn Abī Ḥathmah, who said: The imam stands facing the direction of prayer with one group accompanying him, while another group stands in the direction of the enemy with their faces turned toward the enemy. He performs one unit with those accompanying him. They then stand and complete one unit by themselves, bowing and making two prostrations where they are. They then move to the positions of the other group, and that group comes forward. The imam performs one unit with them, thereby completing two units himself. They then bow and make two prostrations by themselves.

9098 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: I participated with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in an expedition toward Najd. We came face-to-face with the enemy and formed ranks against them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood to lead us in prayer. One group stood and prayed with him, while another group faced the enemy.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ bowed with those accompanying him and made two prostrations. They then withdrew to the position of the group that had not yet prayed, and that group came forward. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed one unit with them and made two prostrations, after which he concluded his prayer with the salutation.
Each of them then stood and completed one unit individually, bowing once and making two prostrations.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (942) and Muslim (305:839).

9099 — It is reported from Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam: He asked Abū Hurayrah, “Did you perform the Prayer of Fear with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?” Abū Hurayrah replied, “Yes.” He asked, “When?” He replied, “During the year of the expedition to Najd.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1240), al-Nasāʾī (3/173–174) and Aḥmad (8260).

9100 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā reported: We were with the Prophet ﷺ when he performed ʿumrah. He circumambulated the Kaʿbah, and we circumambulated with him. He prayed, and we prayed with him. He walked between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, while we shielded him from the people of Makkah so that no one could harm him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4188).
In another narration recorded by al-Bukhārī (4255): “When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed ʿumrah, we shielded him from the young men of the polytheists and prevented them from harming the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”

9101 — It is reported from Ibn ʿUmar: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out intending to perform ʿumrah, but the disbelievers of Quraysh prevented him from reaching the Sacred House. He slaughtered his sacrificial animals and shaved his head at al-Ḥudaybiyah. He concluded a treaty with them permitting him to perform ʿumrah the following year, provided that he carried no weapons against them except swords and remained in Makkah only for as long as they permitted.
The following year he performed ʿumrah and entered Makkah according to the terms of the treaty. After he had remained there for three days, they ordered him to leave, so he departed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4252).

9102 — Mujāhid reported: ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr and I entered the mosque and found ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar sitting beside the apartment of ʿĀʾishah. Some people were performing the forenoon prayer in the mosque.
We asked him about their prayer, and he said, “It is an innovation.”
ʿUrwah then asked him, “How many times did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ perform ʿumrah?” He replied, “Four times, one of them in Rajab.” We disliked contradicting him.
We heard ʿĀʾishah, the Mother of the Believers, brushing her teeth in her apartment. ʿUrwah called, “O Mother, O Mother of the Believers, do you not hear what Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān is saying?”
She asked, “What is he saying?” He replied, “He says that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed four ʿumrahs, one of them in Rajab.”
She said, “May Allah have mercy upon Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never performed an ʿumrah without him being present, yet he never performed ʿumrah in Rajab.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1775, 1776) and Muslim (220:1255).

9103 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions arrived in Makkah after the fever of Yathrib had weakened them.
The polytheists said, “Tomorrow, a people weakened by fever and severely afflicted by it will come to you.” They therefore sat in the area facing al-Ḥijr.
The Prophet ﷺ instructed his Companions to walk briskly during three circuits and to walk normally between the two corners so that the polytheists could witness their strength.
The polytheists said, “Are these the people whom you claimed had been weakened by fever? They are stronger than such-and-such!”
Ibn ʿAbbās said: Nothing prevented the Prophet ﷺ from ordering them to walk briskly during every circuit except his concern to spare them hardship.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4256) and Muslim (240:1266).

9104 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed four ʿumrahs, all of them in Dhū al-Qaʿdah except the one associated with his pilgrimage: the ʿumrah of al-Ḥudaybiyah in Dhū al-Qaʿdah, the ʿumrah of the following year in Dhū al-Qaʿdah, the ʿumrah from al-Jiʿrānah—where he distributed the spoils of Ḥunayn—in Dhū al-Qaʿdah, and the ʿumrah associated with his pilgrimage.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4148) and Muslim (217:1253).

9105 — Yazīd ibn al-Aṣamm reported: Maymūnah bint al-Ḥārith informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ married her while he was not in the state of consecration. He added: She was both my maternal aunt and the maternal aunt of Ibn ʿAbbās.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1411).

9106 — Maymūnah reported: The Prophet ﷺ married me at Sarif while neither of us was in the state of consecration.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1839), Aḥmad (26815), Ibn Ḥibbān (4137) and Ibn al-Jārūd (445).

9107 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ married Maymūnah while he was in the state of consecration.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5114) and Muslim (46:1410).

9108 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ married Maymūnah while he was in the state of consecration, consummated the marriage with her after leaving that state, and she died at Sarif.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4258).
According to the majority of scholars, the Prophet ﷺ married her after completing his ʿumrah and leaving the state of consecration. This was the account of Abū Rāfiʿ, who served as the intermediary between her and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.

9109 — Al-Barāʾ reported: When the Prophet ﷺ set out to perform ʿumrah in Dhū al-Qaʿdah, the people of Makkah refused to allow him to enter until he concluded an agreement with them permitting him to remain there for three days.
When they drew up the document, they wrote: “This is what Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, has agreed upon.”
They said, “We do not acknowledge this. Had we known that you were the Messenger of Allah, we would not have prevented you from anything. Rather, you are Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh.”
He replied, “I am the Messenger of Allah, and I am Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh.”
He then said to ʿAlī, “Erase the words ‘Messenger of Allah.’”
ʿAlī replied, “No, by Allah, I shall never erase you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took the document, though he did not write proficiently, and wrote: “This is what Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh has agreed upon: he shall not enter Makkah bearing weapons except swords kept in their sheaths; he shall not take anyone from its inhabitants with him, even if that person wishes to follow him; and Quraysh shall not prevent any of his Companions from remaining there if he wishes to do so.”
When the Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah and the appointed period had elapsed, they came to ʿAlī and said, “Tell your companion to leave us, for the appointed time has ended.”
The Prophet ﷺ departed, and the daughter of Ḥamzah followed him, calling, “O uncle! O uncle!”
ʿAlī reached for her, took her by the hand and said to Fāṭimah, “Take your cousin and care for her.”
ʿAlī, Zayd and Jaʿfar then disputed over who should have custody of her.
ʿAlī said, “I took her, and she is the daughter of my paternal uncle.”
Jaʿfar said, “She is the daughter of my paternal uncle, and her maternal aunt is my wife.”
Zayd said, “She is the daughter of my brother.”
The Prophet ﷺ awarded her custody to her maternal aunt and said, “The maternal aunt holds the position of the mother.”
He said to ʿAlī, “You are from me, and I am from you.”
He said to Jaʿfar, “You resemble me in appearance and character.”
He said to Zayd, “You are our brother and our freedman and ally.”
ʿAlī asked, “Will you not marry the daughter of Ḥamzah?”
He replied, “She is the daughter of my foster brother through nursing.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4251) and Muslim (90:1783). The wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9110 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed Zayd ibn Ḥārithah as commander during the Battle of Muʾtah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “If Zayd is killed, then Jaʿfar shall command; and if Jaʿfar is killed, then ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah shall command.”
ʿAbdullāh said: I was among them during that battle. We searched for Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib and found him among the slain. We found more than ninety wounds on his body from spear thrusts and arrows.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4260).

9111 — ʿAwf ibn Mālik al-Ashjaʿī reported: I went out with the Muslims who accompanied Zayd ibn Ḥārithah during the Battle of Muʾtah. A reinforcement soldier from Yemen accompanied me, and he possessed nothing besides his sword.
One of the Muslims slaughtered a camel, and the reinforcement soldier asked him for a piece of its hide. He gave it to him, and the soldier fashioned it into something resembling a shield.
We continued onward and encountered the Byzantine forces. Among them was a man riding a chestnut horse with a gilded saddle and gilded weapons. This Byzantine fighter began attacking and harassing the Muslims.
The reinforcement soldier waited for him behind a rock. When the Byzantine passed by him, the soldier hamstrung his horse. The man fell, and the soldier overcame and killed him, taking possession of his horse and weapons.
When Allah granted deliverance to the Muslims, Khālid ibn al-Walīd sent for the soldier and took the slain man’s spoils from him.
ʿAwf said: I went to Khālid and asked, “O Khālid, do you not know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ awarded the possessions of a slain combatant to the person who killed him?”
He replied, “Yes, but I considered these spoils excessive.”
I said, “You must return them to him, or I will certainly report the matter to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” But Khālid refused to return them.
ʿAwf said: When we assembled before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I related the story of the reinforcement soldier and what Khālid had done.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “O Khālid, what caused you to do what you did?”
He replied, “O Messenger of Allah, I considered the spoils excessive.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “O Khālid, return to him what you took from him.”
ʿAwf said: I then said, “There you are, Khālid! Did I not fulfil what I promised you?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “What is this about?”
I informed him of what had happened, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became angry. He said, “O Khālid, do not return it to him. Will you not leave my commanders to me? You receive the benefits of their leadership, while they bear its hardships.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23997). It is also found in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim (44:1753), although the words “the Battle of Muʾtah” are not mentioned there.
Imam Aḥmad also narrated it (23987), with the wording: “We undertook an expedition toward the border of al-Shām.”

9112 — It is reported from Ibn ʿUmar: He stood over Jaʿfar that day after he had been killed and said: I counted fifty wounds upon him from spear thrusts and sword strikes, and none of them was in his rear—that is, in his back.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4260).

9113 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed Zayd ibn Ḥārithah as commander during the Battle of Muʾtah and said, “If Zayd is killed, then Jaʿfar shall command; and if Jaʿfar is killed, then ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah shall command.”
ʿAbdullāh said: I was among them during that battle. We searched for Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib and found him among the slain. We found more than ninety wounds on his body from spear thrusts and arrows.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4261).
The narration stating “more than ninety” is the stronger report because it has come through another chain. Alternatively, the report of fifty wounds may refer specifically to what was counted while viewing his back, whereas the report of more than ninety includes those wounds and the wounds on the remainder of his body.

9114 — Ibn ʿUmar, whenever he greeted the son of Jaʿfar, would say: “Peace be upon you, O son of the possessor of the two wings.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4264).

9115 — ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr reported: My foster father, who belonged to Banū Murrah ibn ʿAwf and participated in the Battle of Muʾtah, narrated to me: By Allah, it is as though I can still see Jaʿfar when he leapt down from his chestnut mare and hamstrung it. He then fought the enemy until he was killed.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2573) and al-Ḥākim (3/209).

9116 — ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr reported: My foster father, who belonged to Banū Murrah ibn ʿAwf, narrated to me: When Jaʿfar was killed, ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah took the standard and advanced with it while mounted upon his horse. He began urging himself to dismount, although he hesitated somewhat, and then recited:
I swear, O soul, you shall descend;
 You shall descend, willingly or be compelled.
 Though the people gather and raise the cry of war,
 Why do I see you disliking Paradise?
 For far too long you have lived in ease;
 What are you but a drop within an old waterskin?
He also recited:
O soul, if you are not killed, you will still die.
 You have now entered the very heat of death.
 What you wished for has now been granted;
 If you do as those two did, you will be rightly guided.
He meant his two companions, Zayd and Jaʿfar.
He then dismounted. After he had dismounted, one of his cousins brought him a meat-covered bone and said, “Strengthen yourself with this, for you have endured much during these days.”
He took it from his hand and bit off a mouthful. Then he heard the violent clash of battle among the people and said to himself, “And you are still occupied with this world!”
He threw it from his hand, took his sword, advanced and fought until he was killed.
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/379).

9117 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr reported: ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah once lost consciousness. His sister ʿAmrah began lamenting over him, saying, “O my mountain! O such-and-such!” enumerating his virtues.
When he regained consciousness, he said, “You did not say anything about me except that I was asked: ‘Are you truly like that?’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4267).

9118 — Anas reported: Before news of their deaths reached the people, the Prophet ﷺ announced the deaths of Zayd, Jaʿfar and Ibn Rawāḥah. He said, “Zayd took the standard and was struck down. Then Jaʿfar took it and was struck down. Then Ibn Rawāḥah took it and was struck down.” His eyes were shedding tears. He continued, “Then one of the swords of Allah took the standard, and Allah granted them victory.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4262).

9119 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ delivered an address and said, “Zayd took the standard and was struck down. Jaʿfar then took it and was struck down. ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah then took it and was struck down. Khālid ibn al-Walīd then took it without having previously been appointed commander, and victory was granted through him.”
He also said, “It would not please us for them to be here with us.”
Ayyūb said: Or he said, “It would not please them to be here with us,” while his eyes were shedding tears.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2798).

9120 — Abū Qatādah, the horseman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dispatched the Army of the Commanders and said, “Zayd ibn Ḥārithah shall command you. If Zayd is struck down, then Jaʿfar shall command; and if Jaʿfar is struck down, then ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah al-Anṣārī shall command.”
Jaʿfar rose and said, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you, O Prophet of Allah! I did not expect that you would appoint Zayd over me.”
He replied, “Proceed, for you do not know which of those arrangements is better.”
He then mentioned the announcement of the deaths of Zayd, Jaʿfar and ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah and said, “Khālid ibn al-Walīd then took the standard. He had not been one of the appointed commanders; rather, he assumed command himself.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised two of his fingers and said, “O Allah, he is a sword from among Your swords, so grant him victory.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (8159) and Aḥmad (22551), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7048).

9121 — ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr reported: My foster father narrated to me: Thābit ibn Aqram, a man of Banū al-ʿAjlān, then took the standard and said, “O Muslims, agree upon a man from among yourselves to command you.”
They said, “You.”
He replied, “I will not do so.”
The people agreed upon Khālid ibn al-Walīd. When he took the standard, he held the enemy back, manoeuvred the Muslims away from them and withdrew while the enemy also pulled back, until he safely led the people away.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq.

9122 — Khālid ibn al-Walīd reported: Nine swords broke in my hand on the day of Muʾtah, and nothing remained in my hand except a broad Yemeni sword.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4265, 4266).

9123 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: When news arrived of the deaths of Ibn Ḥārithah, Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib and ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah, may Allah be pleased with them, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat down with grief visibly apparent upon him.
ʿĀʾishah said: I was watching through an opening in the door—that is, through a crack in it. A man came to him and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the women of Jaʿfar…” and mentioned their weeping.
He ordered the man to tell them to stop. The man went and then returned, saying, “I told them to stop,” but mentioned that they had not obeyed him.
He ordered him again. The man went and returned, saying, “By Allah, they have overcome us.”
She related that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Then throw dust into their mouths.”
ʿĀʾishah said: I said to the man, “May Allah rub your nose in the dust! By Allah, you will not do what you were instructed to do, nor have you spared the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from further trouble.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4263) and Muslim (30:935).
The Prophet’s ﷺ words, “Throw dust into their mouths,” express an emphatic command to restrain them, although the man was unable to carry it out. For this reason, ʿĀʾishah said, “May Allah rub your nose in the dust! By Allah, you will not do it.”
That is: You are incapable of carrying out the instruction to stop them, yet you have burdened the Messenger of Allah ﷺ by repeatedly returning to him.

9124 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: When news of Jaʿfar’s death arrived, we could see grief upon the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/209).
The Names of Those Martyred on the Day of Muʾtah
Ibn Isḥāq listed those who were martyred on the day of Muʾtah. They were:
1. Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib.
2. Zayd ibn Ḥārithah.
3. Masʿūd ibn al-Aswad ibn Ḥārithah ibn Naḍlah.
4. Wahb ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ.
5. ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah.
6. ʿAbbād ibn Qays.
7. Al-Ḥārith ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Usāmah.
8. Surāqah ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAṭiyyah ibn Khansāʾ.
Ibn Hishām said: Among those also martyred on the day of Muʾtah, according to Ibn Shihāb, were:
9. Abū Kulayb.
10. Jābir—the two of them being the sons of ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn ʿAwf.
11. ʿAmr.
12. ʿĀmir—the two of them being the sons of Saʿd ibn al-Ḥārith.

9125 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for me and said, “Put on your clothes and take your weapons, then come to me.”
I came to him while he was performing ablution. He looked me over intently and then lowered his gaze. He said, “I wish to send you in command of an army. May Allah keep you safe and grant you spoils, and I shall give you a generous portion of wealth.”
I replied, “O Messenger of Allah, I did not embrace Islam for the sake of wealth. Rather, I embraced Islam out of a desire for Islam and to be with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He said, “O ʿAmr, how excellent is righteous wealth for a righteous man!”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17763), Ibn Ḥibbān (3210) and al-Ḥākim (2/236).
The word “azʿabu” comes from the expression zaʿabahu, meaning: “I shall give you some wealth.” Al-zaʿb means giving or pushing something forth.

9126 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed him commander of the army of Dhāt al-Salāsil. He said: I came to him and asked, “Which person is most beloved to you?”
He replied, “ʿĀʾishah.”
I asked, “Among men?”
He replied, “Her father.”
I asked, “Then who?”
He replied, “ʿUmar.”
He then named several other men, so I remained silent out of fear that he might place me last among them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4358) and Muslim (8:2384).

9127 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent him on the expedition of Dhāt al-Salāsil. His companions asked his permission to light a fire, but he forbade them.
They spoke to Abū Bakr, who then spoke to ʿAmr about it. ʿAmr said, “If any of them lights a fire, I will throw him into it.”
They encountered the enemy and defeated them. The Muslims wished to pursue them, but ʿAmr prevented them.
When the army returned, they mentioned these matters to the Prophet ﷺ and complained to him about ʿAmr.
ʿAmr said, “O Messenger of Allah, I disliked permitting them to light fires because the enemy might see how few they were. I also disliked allowing them to pursue the enemy because reinforcements might have joined them, enabling them to turn back and attack the Muslims.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ praised his decision.
ʿAmr then asked, “O Messenger of Allah, who is the person most beloved to you?”
He asked, “Why do you ask?”
ʿAmr replied, “So that I may love those whom you love.”
He said, “ʿĀʾishah.”
ʿAmr asked, “Among men?”
He replied, “Abū Bakr.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4540), whose wording is cited here, Ibn Abī Shaybah (37792) and al-Tirmidhī (3886).

9128 — ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: I experienced a nocturnal emission on a bitterly cold night during the expedition of Dhāt al-Salāsil. I feared that I might die if I performed the ritual bath, so I performed dry ablution and then led my companions in the dawn prayer.
They mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ, who asked, “O ʿAmr, did you lead your companions in prayer while you were in a state of major ritual impurity?”
I informed him of what had prevented me from bathing and said, “I heard Allah say: ‘Do not kill yourselves. Indeed, Allah is ever Merciful to you.’ [al-Nisāʾ 4:29]”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled and said nothing.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (334), Aḥmad (17812) and al-Ḥākim (1/77–78).

9129 — Nāfiʿ reported from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent a detachment toward Najd in which ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar participated. They captured many camels. Each man’s allotted share was twelve camels—or eleven camels—and each was given one additional camel.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (15), al-Bukhārī (3134) and Muslim (1749).
Its details are related by Abū Dāwūd (2743) from Ibn ʿUmar, who said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent a detachment to Najd, and I went with it. We captured a great quantity of livestock, and our commander awarded each man one camel as an additional share.
We then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who divided the spoils among us. After the one-fifth had been removed, every man received twelve camels.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not count against us what our commander had already given us, nor did he criticize him for what he had done. Thus, with the additional award, every man received thirteen camels.

9130 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ḥadrad reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us to Iḍam. I departed with a group of Muslims that included Abū Qatādah al-Ḥārith ibn Ribʿī and Muḥallim ibn Jaththāmah ibn Qays.
We continued until, while we were at Baṭn Iḍam, ʿĀmir al-Ashjaʿī passed us riding a young camel and carrying possessions and a leather container of milk.
When he passed us, he greeted us with peace, so we refrained from harming him. Muḥallim ibn Jaththāmah, however, attacked and killed him because of a previous matter between them, and he took his camel and possessions.
When we returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what had happened, the following Qur’anic verse was revealed concerning us:
“O you who believe, when you go forth in the cause of Allah, investigate carefully, and do not say to one who offers you peace, ‘You are not a believer,’ seeking the temporary gains of worldly life, for with Allah are abundant spoils. You yourselves were like that before, but Allah bestowed His favor upon you; therefore, investigate carefully. Indeed, Allah is ever fully aware of what you do.” [al-Nisāʾ 4:94]
Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (23881), Ibn Abī Shaybah (38168) and Ibn al-Jārūd (777).

9131 — Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah both reported: Among the terms of the treaty concluded between the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Quraysh on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyah was that whoever wished to enter the alliance and covenant of Muḥammad could do so, and whoever wished to enter the alliance and covenant of Quraysh could do so.
Khuzāʿah immediately declared, “We shall enter the alliance and covenant of Muḥammad ﷺ,” while Banū Bakr declared, “We shall enter the alliance and covenant of Quraysh.”
They remained under that truce for approximately seventeen or eighteen months.
Banū Bakr, who had entered the alliance and covenant of Quraysh, then launched a night attack upon Khuzāʿah, who had entered the alliance and covenant of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The attack occurred at a water source belonging to Khuzāʿah called al-Watīr, near Makkah.
Quraysh said, “Muḥammad will not learn of our involvement. It is night, and no one can see us.” They therefore assisted Banū Bakr with horses and weapons and fought alongside them, thereby violating their covenant with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿAmr ibn Sālim rode to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after what had occurred between Khuzāʿah and Banū Bakr at al-Watīr. He reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in Madinah to inform him of the news, having composed several lines of poetry.
When he arrived, he recited them before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
O Allah, I appeal to Muḥammad
 By the ancient alliance of our father and his father.
You were once as our children, and we as your parents;
 Then we embraced Islam and never withdrew our loyalty.
So grant the Messenger of Allah powerful victory,
 And summon Allah’s servants to come as reinforcements.
Among them is the Messenger of Allah, fully prepared;
 If oppression is imposed upon him, his face darkens with resolve.
Within an army surging like a foaming sea;
 Indeed, Quraysh has broken its promise to you.
They have violated your solemn covenant
 And claimed that you would summon no one.
Yet they are humbler and fewer in number;
 They have set an ambush for me at Kadāʾ.
They attacked us at al-Watīr while we slept,
 And killed us while bowing and prostrating.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “You shall be supported, O ʿAmr ibn Sālim.”
A cloud then appeared before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said, “This cloud is pouring forth with the victory of Banū Kaʿb.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered the people to prepare for military action but concealed his destination from them. He asked Allah to keep news of his movement hidden from Quraysh so that he could take them by surprise in their own territory.
Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/6–7) and recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/390).

9132 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The leader of Khuzāʿah recited:
O Allah, I appeal to Muḥammad
 By the ancient alliance of our father and his father.
May Allah guide you—grant us powerful victory,
 And summon Allah’s servants to come as reinforcements.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1817).

9133 — Jābir reported: During the year of the conquest, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out for Makkah in Ramaḍān and travelled until he reached Kurāʿ al-Ghamīm.
The people were fasting, both those travelling on foot and those riding. He was told, “Fasting has become difficult for the people, and they are watching to see what you will do.”
He called for a cup and raised it to his mouth where the people could see him. He then drank. Some of the people broke their fast, while others continued fasting.
