The Prophet ﷺ was told, “Some of them have continued fasting.” He said, “Those are the disobedient ones.”
The Companions who were travelling on foot gathered and said, “Let us present ourselves for the supplications of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, for the journey has become severe and the hardship prolonged.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ told them, “Seek assistance through nasal—walking at a quick pace—for it shortens the distance for you and makes your burden feel lighter.”
Jābir said: We did so and became swift and light in our movement.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1880) and Ibn Ḥibbān (2706).
The word “al-nasal” means walking quickly.

9134 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Abū Bakr entered while she was sifting some wheat. He asked, “What is this? Has the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered you to prepare for departure?”
She replied, “Yes, so prepare yourself.”
He asked, “Where are we going?”
She replied, “He has not named any destination to us; he has only ordered us to prepare.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/12).
It is also recorded without a chain of transmission in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/397), where it states: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then informed the people that he was marching toward Makkah and commanded them to prepare diligently. He prayed, “O Allah, withhold the spies and reports from Quraysh until we take them by surprise in their own territory.”

9135 — ʿUbaydullāh ibn Abī Rāfiʿ, the scribe of ʿAlī, reported: I heard ʿAlī say: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent al-Zubayr, al-Miqdād and me, saying, “Proceed until you reach Rawḍat Khākh. There you will find a woman carrying a letter. Take it from her.”
We set out with our horses racing swiftly until we reached the meadow, where we found the woman.
We said to her, “Produce the letter.”
She replied, “I have no letter.”
We said, “You will produce the letter, or we will remove your clothing.”
She then removed it from the braids of her hair.
We brought it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It contained a message from Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to some of the polytheists in Makkah, informing them of some of the plans of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “O Ḥāṭib, what is this?”
He replied, “O Messenger of Allah, do not be hasty in judging me. I was a man attached to Quraysh”—meaning that he was their ally but not originally one of them—“whereas the Emigrants accompanying you have relatives in Makkah who protect their families and property.
“Because I lacked such blood ties among them, I wished to perform a favor for them so that they might protect my relatives. I did not do it because I had abandoned my religion or become pleased with disbelief after accepting Islam.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “He has indeed spoken truthfully to you.”
ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, allow me to strike the neck of this hypocrite.”
He replied, “He participated at Badr. How do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon those who participated at Badr and said, ‘Do whatever you wish, for I have forgiven you.’”
Allah then revealed the sūrah:
“O you who believe, do not take My enemies and your enemies as allies, offering them affection even though they have disbelieved in the truth that has come to you. They drive out the Messenger and yourselves merely because you believe in Allah, your Lord. If you have truly gone forth striving in My cause and seeking My pleasure, how can you secretly show them affection? I know best what you conceal and what you reveal. Whoever among you does this has certainly strayed from the straight path.” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:1]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4274) and Muslim (16:2494).
The word “al-ẓaʿīnah” means a woman.
Regarding the statement, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent al-Zubayr, al-Miqdād and me,” another narration in al-Bukhārī and Muslim names Abū Marthad al-Ghanawī instead of al-Miqdād.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said: “It is possible that all three accompanied him and that one narrator reported from ʿAlī what the other narrator did not mention.” End quote. Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/520).
In another narration: “ʿUmar’s eyes filled with tears,” and he said, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”

9136 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah wrote to the people of Makkah, informing them that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to march against them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was directed to the woman carrying the letter, so he sent men after her, and the letter was taken from her hair.
He asked, “O Ḥāṭib, did you do this?”
He replied, “Yes. I did not do it out of treachery toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ”—Yūnus’s wording was: “out of treachery, O Messenger of Allah”—“nor out of hypocrisy.
“I knew that Allah would grant victory to His Messenger and complete his mission. However, I had once held an honored position among them, and my mother was living with them, so I wished to establish this favor with them.”
ʿUmar said, “Shall I not strike off this man’s head?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied, “Would you kill a man who participated at Badr? How do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon the people of Badr and said, ‘Do whatever you wish.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (14774), Abū Yaʿlā (2265) and Ibn Ḥibbān (4797).

9137 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb reported: Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah wrote a letter to the people of Makkah. Allah informed His Prophet ﷺ of it, so he sent ʿAlī and al-Zubayr after the letter.
They caught up with a woman riding a camel and removed the letter from one of the locks of her hair, exactly as the Prophet of Allah ﷺ had told them.
He summoned Ḥāṭib and asked, “O Ḥāṭib, did you write this letter?”
He replied, “Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He asked, “What caused you to do that?”
He replied, “By Allah, I am sincerely devoted to Allah and His Messenger. However, I was an outsider among the people of Makkah, while my family lived among them, and I feared for them. I therefore wrote a letter that could not harm Allah or His Messenger in any way, hoping that it might benefit my family.”
ʿUmar said: I drew my sword and said, “O Messenger of Allah, place Ḥāṭib in my power, for he has disbelieved, and I will strike off his head.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “O Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, how do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon this group who participated at Badr and said, ‘Do whatever you wish, for I have forgiven you.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2695) and al-Ḥākim (4/77).

9138 — It is reported from Ibn ʿUmar: Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah was brought before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who asked him, “Did you write this letter?”
He replied, “Yes. By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, faith has not changed within my heart. But every man of Quraysh had close relatives and a household who would protect his family. I wrote the letter hoping that, through it, Allah would protect my family.”
ʿUmar said, “Permit me to deal with him.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked, “Would you kill him?”
He replied, “Yes, if you permit me.”
He said, “How do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon the people of Badr and said, ‘Do whatever you wish.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah in his Muṣannaf (37883), Aḥmad (5878) and Abū Yaʿlā in his Musnad (5522).

9139 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah reported: His father, who was accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and had participated at Badr, wrote a letter to the disbelievers of Quraysh.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī and al-Zubayr and said, “Proceed until you catch a woman carrying a letter, and bring it to me.”
They set out until they met her and said, “Give us the letter you are carrying.” They informed her that they would not leave until they had removed every garment she was wearing.
She asked, “Are you not two Muslim men?”
They replied, “Yes, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has informed us that you are carrying a letter.”
When she realized that she could not escape them, she untied the letter from her hair and handed it to them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Ḥāṭib and read the letter to him. He asked, “Do you recognize this letter?”
He replied, “Yes.”
He asked, “What caused you to do this?”
He replied, “My children and relatives are there, and I was an outsider among you, O people of Quraysh.”
ʿUmar said, “Permit me to kill Ḥāṭib.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “No, because he participated at Badr. You do not know; perhaps Allah looked upon the people of Badr and said, ‘Do whatever you wish, for I shall forgive you.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (3/206) and al-Ḥākim in al-Mustadrak (3/301).

9140 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ departed from Madinah during Ramaḍān with ten thousand men. This was eight and a half years after his arrival in Madinah.
He and the Muslims accompanying him travelled toward Makkah while he and they were fasting. When he reached al-Kadīd—a water source between ʿUsfān and Qudayd—he broke his fast, and they also broke theirs.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4276) and Muslim (88:1113).

9141 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ proceeded after appointing Abū Ruhm Kulthūm ibn Ḥuṣayn al-Ghifārī as governor of Madinah. He departed after ten days of Ramaḍān had passed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ fasted, and the people fasted with him, until he reached al-Kadīd, between ʿUsfān and Amaj, where he broke his fast.
He continued until he camped at Marr al-Ẓahrān with ten thousand Muslims, including men from Muzaynah and Sulaym, while every tribe was represented by a number of Muslims.
The Emigrants and the Anṣār came out in full force with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and not one of them remained behind.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ camped at Marr al-Ẓahrān. All reports of his movements had been concealed from Quraysh. No news concerning the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached them, and they did not know what he intended to do.
During those nights, Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām and Budayl ibn Warqāʾ went out to investigate and search for any news they might discover or hear.
Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib had already joined the Messenger of Allah ﷺ somewhere along the road.
Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah had also met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ somewhere between Makkah and Madinah and sought permission to enter his presence.
Umm Salamah spoke to him on their behalf, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, one is your cousin, and the other is the son of your paternal aunt and your brother-in-law.”
He replied, “I have no need of either of them. As for my cousin, he attacked my honor; and as for the son of my paternal aunt and my brother-in-law, he is the one who said to me in Makkah what he said.”
When this response was conveyed to them—and Abū Sufyān had a young son with him—he said, “By Allah, he must permit me to enter, or I shall take this son of mine by the hand and wander through the earth until we die of thirst and hunger.”
When this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he felt compassion for them and granted them permission to enter. They came before him and embraced Islam.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ camped at Marr al-Ẓahrān, al-ʿAbbās said, “What a terrible morning awaits Quraysh! By Allah, if the Messenger of Allah ﷺ enters Makkah by force before they seek his protection, it will mean the ruin of Quraysh for all time.”
He said: I mounted the white mule of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and rode until I reached the arāk trees. I thought: Perhaps I will encounter a woodcutter, a milk seller or someone travelling to Makkah on some errand, who can inform its people of the location of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that they may come out and seek his protection before he enters by force.
He said: By Allah, while I was riding the mule and searching for what I had set out to find, I heard Abū Sufyān and Budayl ibn Warqāʾ conversing with one another.
Abū Sufyān said, “I have never seen fires or an army like those of tonight.”
Budayl replied, “By Allah, these are the fires of Khuzāʿah, stirred into action by war.”
Abū Sufyān said, “By Allah, Khuzāʿah is too weak and too few in number for these to be its fires and army.”
Al-ʿAbbās said: I recognized his voice and called, “O Abū Ḥanẓalah!”
He recognized my voice and asked, “Abū al-Faḍl?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He asked, “What is happening? May my father and mother be sacrificed for you!”
I said, “Woe to you, Abū Sufyān! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ is here among the people.”
He exclaimed, “What a terrible morning awaits Quraysh, by Allah! What can be done? May my father and mother be sacrificed for you!”
I said, “By Allah, if he captures you, he will surely have your neck struck. Mount this mule behind me so that I may bring you to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and seek his protection for you.”
He mounted behind me, while his two companions returned.
I rode onward with him. Whenever we passed one of the Muslims’ campfires, they asked, “Who is this?” When they recognized the mule of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they said, “It is the uncle of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ riding his mule.”
Eventually, I passed the fire of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. He asked, “Who is this?” and came toward me.
When he saw Abū Sufyān seated on the rear of the animal, he said, “Abū Sufyān, the enemy of Allah! Praise be to Allah, Who has placed you in our power without any treaty or covenant.”
He hurried toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while I urged the mule forward. I overtook him by the small distance with which a slow animal can outpace a man travelling on foot.
I leapt from the mule and entered the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and ʿUmar entered as well.
ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, this is Abū Sufyān. Allah has placed him in our power without any treaty or covenant, so allow me to strike off his head.”
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have granted him protection.”
I then sat beside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, held him close and said, “No, by Allah, no man besides me shall speak privately with him tonight.”
When ʿUmar continued pressing the matter, I said, “Take it easy, ʿUmar! By Allah, had he been a man from Banū ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb, you would not have said this. You say it only because you know that he is a man from Banū ʿAbd Manāf.”
ʿUmar replied, “Take it easy, al-ʿAbbās! By Allah, the day you embraced Islam, your acceptance of Islam was more beloved to me than the acceptance of Islam by al-Khaṭṭāb would have been had he embraced it. That was only because I knew that your Islam was more beloved to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ than the Islam of al-Khaṭṭāb would have been.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Take him to your camp, O ʿAbbās, and bring him to me in the morning.”
I took him to my camp, and he spent the night with me.
In the morning, I brought him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When he saw him, he said, “Woe to you, Abū Sufyān! Is it not time for you to know that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah?”
He replied, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! How noble, kind to your relatives and forbearing you are! By Allah, I have come to realize that if there had been another deity besides Allah, it would have benefited me in some way.”
He said, “Woe to you, Abū Sufyān! Is it not time for you to know that I am the Messenger of Allah?”
He replied, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! How forbearing, noble and kind to your relatives you are! By Allah, until this moment I still harbored some doubt about that.”
Al-ʿAbbās said, “Woe to you, Abū Sufyān! Embrace Islam and testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah before your neck is struck.”
He then pronounced the testimony of truth and embraced Islam.
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, Abū Sufyān is a man who loves distinction, so grant him something.”
He replied, “Yes. Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe; whoever closes his door shall be safe; and whoever enters the Sacred Mosque shall be safe.”
When Abū Sufyān turned to leave, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “O ʿAbbās, hold him at the narrow part of the valley near the projecting edge of the mountain so that the armies of Allah may pass before him and he may see them.”
I took him and detained him at the place where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had instructed me.
The tribes then passed beneath their respective banners. Whenever a tribe passed, Abū Sufyān asked, “O ʿAbbās, who are these?”
I would reply, “Sulaym.”
He would say, “What have I to do with Sulaym?”
Another tribe would pass, and he would ask, “O ʿAbbās, who are these?”
I would reply, “Muzaynah.”
He would say, “What have I to do with Muzaynah?”
The tribes continued passing. No tribe passed without him asking, “Who are these?” I would reply, “Banū so-and-so,” and he would say, “What have I to do with Banū so-and-so?”
Finally, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed in his green-clad battalion, which contained the Emigrants and the Anṣār. Because of their armor, nothing could be seen of them except their eyes.
Abū Sufyān exclaimed, “Glory be to Allah, O ʿAbbās! Who are these?”
I replied, “This is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ among the Emigrants and the Anṣār.”
He said, “No one has the ability or strength to confront these men. By Allah, O Abū al-Faḍl, the dominion of your nephew has become tremendous this morning!”
I replied, “O Abū Sufyān, it is prophethood.”
He said, “Then how excellent it is!”
I said, “Hurry to your people!”
When he reached them, he cried at the top of his voice, “O people of Quraysh! Muḥammad has come against you with a force you cannot withstand. Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe.”
His wife, Hind bint ʿUtbah, rose against him and seized his moustache, saying, “Kill this fat, wretched man! What a disgraceful scout for his people!”
He said, “Woe to you! Do not let this woman deceive you concerning your own lives. A force has come against you that you cannot withstand. Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe.”
They replied, “Woe to you! What benefit can your house provide?”
He replied, “Whoever closes his door shall be safe, and whoever enters the Sacred Mosque shall be safe.”
The people then dispersed to their homes and to the Sacred Mosque.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, as recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/399–405), and by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/10–15). The wording is al-Ṭabarānī’s.

9142 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported: During the year of the conquest, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed us that we would depart after two nights of Ramaḍān had passed. We set out while fasting, and when we reached al-Kadīd, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered us to break our fast.
The following morning, some people were fasting while others were not. When we reached the closest stopping place facing the enemy, he ordered us to break our fast, so all of us did so.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (11825).

9143 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched during the year of the conquest and news of this reached Quraysh, Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām and Budayl ibn Warqāʾ went out seeking information about the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
They continued until they reached Marr al-Ẓahrān, where they saw fires resembling the fires of ʿArafah.
Abū Sufyān asked, “What are these? They look like the fires of ʿArafah!”
Budayl ibn Warqāʾ replied, “They are the fires of Banū ʿAmr.”
Abū Sufyān said, “Banū ʿAmr are too few for this.”
Some of the guards of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw them, caught them and brought them before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Abū Sufyān then embraced Islam.
When the Prophet ﷺ resumed the march, he said to al-ʿAbbās, “Hold Abū Sufyān at the projecting edge of the mountain so that he may observe the Muslims.”
Al-ʿAbbās detained him there, and the tribes accompanying the Prophet ﷺ began passing before Abū Sufyān, battalion after battalion.
A battalion passed, and he asked, “O ʿAbbās, who are these?”
He replied, “These are Ghifār.”
Abū Sufyān said, “What have I to do with Ghifār?”
Juhaynah then passed, and he said the same. Saʿd ibn Hudhaym passed, and he said the same; then Sulaym passed, and he again said the same.
Finally, a battalion unlike any he had previously seen approached. He asked, “Who are these?”
Al-ʿAbbās replied, “These are the Anṣār, commanded by Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah, who is carrying the banner.”
Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah said, “O Abū Sufyān, today is the day of fierce battle; today the sanctity of the Kaʿbah will be violated.”
Abū Sufyān said, “O ʿAbbās, what a day for defending one’s people!”
Another battalion then arrived. It was the smallest of the battalions and included the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions. The Prophet’s ﷺ banner was carried by al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed Abū Sufyān, he said, “Do you not know what Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah said?”
He asked, “What did he say?”
Abū Sufyān informed him.
The Prophet ﷺ said, “Saʿd was mistaken. Rather, this is a day on which Allah will honor the Kaʿbah and a day on which the Kaʿbah will be clothed.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that his banner be planted at al-Ḥajūn.
ʿUrwah said: Nāfiʿ ibn Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim informed me that he heard al-ʿAbbās ask al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām, “O Abū ʿAbdullāh, is this where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered you to plant the banner?”
He said: On that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered Khālid ibn al-Walīd to enter through the upper part of Makkah by way of Kadāʾ, while the Prophet ﷺ entered by way of Kudā.
Two horsemen from Khālid’s force were killed that day: Ḥubaysh ibn al-Ashʿar and Kurz ibn Jābir al-Fihrī.
Authentic, except for the statement, “On that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered Khālid ibn al-Walīd to enter through the upper part of Makkah by way of Kadāʾ, while the Prophet ﷺ entered by way of Kudā,” which is anomalous. Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4280).

9144 — Abū Hurayrah reported: Delegations came to Muʿāwiyah during Ramaḍān, and we used to prepare food for one another. Abū Hurayrah frequently invited us to his lodging.
I thought: Why should I not prepare food and invite them to my lodging? So I ordered that a meal be prepared.
I met Abū Hurayrah in the evening and said, “The invitation is at my lodging tonight.”
He replied, “You have preceded me.”
I said, “Yes.”
I invited them, and Abū Hurayrah said, “O assembly of the Anṣār, shall I not relate to you a report concerning yourselves?”
He then mentioned the conquest of Makkah, saying: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ advanced until he reached Makkah. He placed al-Zubayr over one flank, Khālid over the other flank and Abū ʿUbaydah over the troops without armor. They proceeded through the valley, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was within a battalion.
He looked and saw me, then called, “Abū Hurayrah!”
I replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said, “Let none come to me except the Anṣār.”
A narrator other than Shaybān added that he said, “Call the Anṣār to me.”
They gathered around him.
Quraysh had gathered its rabble and followers and said, “We will send these men forward. If they achieve anything, we shall join them; but if they are defeated, we shall give whatever is demanded of us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, “Do you see the rabble and followers of Quraysh?”
He gestured with his hands, placing one over the other to indicate striking them down, and said, “Then meet me at al-Ṣafā.”
We advanced, and anyone among us who wished to kill one of them did so, while none of them offered any effective resistance.
Abū Sufyān came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the main body of Quraysh has been exposed to destruction. There will be no Quraysh after today!”
He said, “Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe.”
Some of the Anṣār said to one another, “The man has been overcome by longing for his hometown and compassion for his tribe.”
Abū Hurayrah said: Revelation then came. When revelation descended upon him, its arrival was not hidden from us. No one would raise his eyes toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until the revelation had ended.
When the revelation ended, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “O assembly of the Anṣār!”
They replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He asked, “Did you say: ‘The man has been overcome by longing for his hometown’?”
They replied, “That did occur.”
He said, “No. I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger. I emigrated to Allah and to you. My life is with you, and my death shall be with you.”
They approached him weeping and said, “By Allah, we said what we said only because of our deep attachment to Allah and His Messenger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Allah and His Messenger accept your truthfulness and excuse you.”
The people then went to the house of Abū Sufyān, while others closed the doors of their homes.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ proceeded to the Black Stone, touched it and then circumambulated the House.
He passed an idol beside the House that they used to worship. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was holding a bow by its curved end.
When he reached the idol, he began piercing it in the eye while saying, “Truth has come, and falsehood has vanished.”
After completing his circumambulation, he went to al-Ṣafā and ascended it until he could see the House. He raised his hands, praised Allah and supplicated as Allah willed him to supplicate.
In another wording, he said, “What, then, would my name and identity be? No! I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (84:1780).
The two flanks are the right and left wings of the army, with its center positioned between them.
The word “al-ḥussar” means those who had no armor.
The words “Call the Anṣār to me” mean: summon them to me.
The words “The main body of Quraysh has been exposed to destruction” are narrated in another version as “The main body of Quraysh has been annihilated.” The two expressions are similar, meaning that Quraysh had been overwhelmed by killing and faced destruction. “Their greenery” refers to their main body or collective strength.
The word “al-ḍann” means withholding something precious out of intense attachment to it.

9145 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Rabāḥ reported: We came as a delegation to Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān, and Abū Hurayrah was among us. Each man would prepare food for his companions on an assigned day.
When my turn arrived, I said, “O Abū Hurayrah, today is my turn.”
They came to the lodging before our food was ready, so I said, “O Abū Hurayrah, why do you not relate something to us from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until our food is ready?”
He said: We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of the conquest. He placed Khālid ibn al-Walīd over the right flank, al-Zubayr over the left flank and Abū ʿUbaydah over the infantry in the valley.
He said, “O Abū Hurayrah, summon the Anṣār to me.”
I called them, and they came hurrying.
He asked, “O assembly of the Anṣār, do you see the rabble of Quraysh?”
They replied, “Yes.”
He said, “When you meet them tomorrow, cut them down completely.”
He gestured with his hand, placing his right hand over his left, and said, “Your meeting place is al-Ṣafā.”
No one from the enemy exposed himself to them that day without their striking him down.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ascended al-Ṣafā, and the Anṣār came and gathered around it.
Abū Sufyān came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the main body of Quraysh has been destroyed. There will be no Quraysh after today!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe; whoever lays down his weapons shall be safe; and whoever closes his door shall be safe.”
The Anṣār said, “The man has been overcome by compassion for his tribe and longing for his hometown.”
Revelation then descended upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He said, “You said: ‘The man has been overcome by compassion for his tribe and longing for his hometown.’ What, then, would my name and identity be?” He repeated this three times.
“I am Muḥammad, the servant of Allah and His Messenger. I emigrated to Allah and to you. My life is with you, and my death shall be with you.”
They said, “By Allah, we said it only because of our deep attachment to Allah and His Messenger.”
He said, “Allah and His Messenger accept your truthfulness and excuse you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (86:1780).

9146 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: During the year of the conquest, al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib brought Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he embraced Islam at Marr al-Ẓahrān.
Al-ʿAbbās said, “O Messenger of Allah, Abū Sufyān is a man who loves distinction, so perhaps you could grant him something.”
He said, “Yes. Whoever enters the house of Abū Sufyān shall be safe, and whoever closes his door shall be safe.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3021) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/31).

9147 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah also met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Nīq al-ʿUqāb, between Makkah and Madinah, and sought permission to enter his presence.
Umm Salamah spoke to him on their behalf, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, one is your cousin, and the other is the son of your paternal aunt and your brother-in-law.”
He replied, “I have no need of either of them. My cousin attacked my honor, while the son of my paternal aunt is the one who said to me in Makkah what he said.”
When this response reached them—and Abū Sufyān had a young son with him—he said, “By Allah, he must permit me to enter, or I shall take this son of mine by the hand and wander through the earth until we die of thirst and hunger.”
When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he felt compassion for them and permitted them to enter. They came before him and embraced Islam.
Abū Sufyān then recited verses concerning his conversion and apologized to the Prophet ﷺ for his previous conduct:
By your life, on the day I carried a banner
 That the cavalry of al-Lāt might defeat Muḥammad’s cavalry,
I was like a bewildered night traveller enveloped in darkness;
 But now is the time when I am guided and follow guidance.
A guide other than myself guided me and directed me to Allah—
 The very one whom I had driven away at every turn.
I strove to turn away and distance myself from Muḥammad,
 Though I was called one of his kin even without claiming descent from him.
They are what they are: whoever did not speak according to their desires,
 Even if possessing sound judgment, was blamed and denounced.
I sought to please them, though I did not truly belong with them,
 Until guidance reached me wherever I sat.
Tell Thaqīf that I have no desire to fight them;
 Tell Thaqīf to direct their threats toward someone other than me.
I was not among the army that struck ʿĀmir,
 Nor did it occur through any deed of my tongue or hand.
Those were tribes that came from distant lands,
 Scattered groups arriving from Sahām and Surdud.
Ibn Isḥāq said: They claimed that when he recited before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, “The very one whom I had driven away at every turn directed me to Allah,” the Messenger of Allah ﷺ struck him gently on the chest and said, “You did indeed drive me away at every turn.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/43–44) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/27–28). It is also recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/399).

9148 — ʿĀʾishah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Satirize Quraysh in poetry, for it is more painful to them than being showered with arrows.”
He summoned Ibn Rawāḥah and said, “Satirize them.” He did so, but his poetry did not fully satisfy the intended purpose.
He then summoned Kaʿb ibn Mālik and afterward summoned Ḥassān ibn Thābit.
When Ḥassān entered, he said, “The time has come for you to summon this lion that strikes with its tail.”
He extended his tongue and moved it about, saying, “By Him Who sent you with the truth, I shall tear them apart with my tongue as leather is cut.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Do not be hasty. Abū Bakr is the most knowledgeable of Quraysh concerning their genealogies, and I share lineage with them. Wait until he distinguishes my ancestry for you.”
Ḥassān went to Abū Bakr and then returned, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, he has distinguished your lineage for me. By Him Who sent you with the truth, I shall extract you from among them as a hair is drawn from dough.”
ʿĀʾishah said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say to Ḥassān, “The Holy Spirit will continue supporting you for as long as you defend Allah and His Messenger.”
She also said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say, “Ḥassān satirized them, bringing relief to the believers and satisfying his own heart.”
Ḥassān recited:
You satirized Muḥammad, so I answered in his defense;
 And with Allah lies the reward for that.
You satirized Muḥammad, righteous and upright,
 The Messenger of Allah, whose nature is faithfulness.
My father, his father and my honor
 Are all a shield protecting Muḥammad’s honor from you.
May I lose my daughter if you do not see our cavalry
 Raising clouds of dust from both sides of Kadāʾ.
Their horses race against the reins as they ascend,
 Bearing upon their shoulders thirsting spears.
Our steeds continue charging, drenched in exertion,
 While women strike their faces with their veils.
If you stand aside from us, we shall perform ʿumrah;
 Victory will come, and the veil will be removed.
Otherwise, prepare yourselves for the blows of a day
 On which Allah will grant honor to whomever He wills.
Allah has said: “I have sent a servant
 Who proclaims the truth, in which there is no obscurity.”
Allah has said: “I have prepared an army—
 They are the Anṣār, whose readiness is for battle.”
Every day we face from Maʿadd
 Insults, fighting or poetic attack.
How could one among you who satirizes the Messenger of Allah
 Be equal to one who praises and supports him?
Jibrīl, the messenger of Allah, is among us,
 And the Holy Spirit has no equal.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (157:2490).

9149 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Our stopping place—if Allah wills, when Allah grants victory—will be al-Khayf, where they pledged themselves together upon disbelief.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4284) and Muslim (345:1314).
The words “where they pledged themselves together upon disbelief” mean that they formed an alliance and covenant to boycott Banū Hāshim and Banū al-Muṭṭalib until they surrendered the Prophet ﷺ to them.
Al-Khayf was the place where Quraysh concluded its agreement to boycott Banū Hāshim and the Muslims. It was known as Khayf Banī Kinānah and al-Ḥajūn, and today it is known as al-Muḥaṣṣab.
This was because ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib had taken possession of the Prophet’s ﷺ family residence, while ʿAlī and Jaʿfar inherited nothing because they were Muslims.

9150 — Usāmah ibn Zayd reported: During the time of the conquest, he asked, “O Messenger of Allah, where will you stay tomorrow?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied, “Has ʿAqīl left us any residence?”
He then said, “A believer does not inherit from a disbeliever, nor does a disbeliever inherit from a believer.”
Al-Zuhrī was asked, “Who inherited from Abū Ṭālib?”
He replied, “ʿAqīl and Ṭālib inherited from him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4282, 4283) and Muslim (1:1614; 440:1351).

9151 — ʿĀʾishah reported: During the year of the conquest, the Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah by way of Kadāʾ, which lies in the upper part of Makkah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4290) and Muslim (225:1258).

9152 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered Makkah wearing a helmet. When he removed it, a man came and said, “Ibn Khaṭal is clinging to the coverings of the Kaʿbah.”
He said, “Kill him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1846) and Muslim (1357).

9153 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered Makkah—meaning on the day of its conquest—wearing a black turban and not being in the state of consecration.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1358).

9154 — ʿAmr ibn Ḥurayth reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ addressed the people while wearing a black turban.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1359).

9155 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Makkah, several young boys from Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib came to welcome him. He seated one in front of him and another behind him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1798).
In another narration recorded by al-Bukhārī (5966) through a different chain, Ibn ʿAbbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived carrying Qutham in front of him and al-Faḍl behind him—or Qutham behind him and al-Faḍl in front. Which of them was worse, and which was better?

9156 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī reported: On the day of the conquest, I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ riding his she-camel and reciting Sūrat al-Fatḥ—or a portion of Sūrat al-Fatḥ—prolonging and repeating the tones of his recitation.
The narrator said: Muʿāwiyah then recited in imitation of Ibn Mughaffal’s recitation and said, “Were it not that the people would gather around you, I would repeat the tones just as Ibn Mughaffal did when imitating the Prophet ﷺ.”
I asked Muʿāwiyah, “How did he repeat and prolong his voice?”
He replied, “Ā, ā, ā,” three times.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7540) and Muslim (237:794).

9157 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported: When Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah said to Abū Sufyān, “Today is the day of fierce battle; today the sanctity of the Kaʿbah will be violated,” the Prophet ﷺ said, “Saʿd was mistaken. Rather, this is a day on which Allah will honor the Kaʿbah and a day on which the Kaʿbah will be clothed.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4280).
The word “kadhaba” here means: he was mistaken.

9158 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Makkah, Qays was positioned at the front of his army. Saʿd—meaning Qays’s father—asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to remove him from that position out of fear that he might undertake some rash action. The Prophet ﷺ therefore removed him from it.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1819).

9159 — Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah reported: After the Prophet ﷺ had become securely established in Makkah during the year of the conquest, he circumambulated the Kaʿbah while riding a camel. He touched the Corner with a hooked staff in his hand, while I watched him.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1878). It is also recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/411).

9160 — Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ settled in Makkah and the people became secure, he went to the Sacred House and circumambulated it seven times while riding. He touched the Corner with a hooked staff in his hand.
After completing his circumambulation, he summoned ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah and took the key of the Kaʿbah from him. The Kaʿbah was opened, and the Prophet ﷺ entered it.
He found inside it a figure of a dove made from sticks. He broke it with his hand and threw it away.
He then stood at the door of the Kaʿbah while the people had gathered around him in the Sacred Mosque.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, as recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/411–412).

9161 — ʿAbdullāh reported: The Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah while three hundred and sixty idols stood around the Kaʿbah. He began striking them with a stick in his hand and saying:
“Truth has come, and falsehood has vanished. Indeed, falsehood is ever bound to vanish.” [al-Isrāʾ 17:81]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2478) and Muslim (1781).

9162 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Makkah, he refused to enter the Sacred House while idols remained inside it. He ordered that they be removed.
Images of Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl were brought out, with divining arrows depicted in their hands.
The Prophet ﷺ said, “May Allah condemn them! They certainly knew that these two never sought decisions through divining arrows.”
He then entered the House, proclaimed Allah’s greatness in its various corners and left without praying inside it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4288).
The azlām were arrows upon which the Arabs during the pre-Islamic period wrote commands such as “Do it” on some and “Do not do it” on others, after which they placed them inside a container.
When one of them intended to undertake something, he would place his hand inside and draw an arrow. If the arrow ordering him to proceed appeared, he would carry out his intention; but if the prohibiting arrow appeared, he would refrain.

9163 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ entered the House and found within it an image of Ibrāhīm and an image of Maryam. He said, “They have certainly heard that the angels do not enter a house containing images! Here is Ibrāhīm depicted—why is he shown seeking decisions through divining arrows?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3351).
Al-istiqsām means seeking to discover through divining arrows what has supposedly been apportioned and decreed for a person regarding something whose outcome is unknown.
When one of them intended to travel, marry or undertake another important matter, he would draw the azlām, which were arrow shafts.
Upon one was written, “My lord commands me”; upon another, “My lord forbids me”; while another was left blank.
If “My lord commands me” appeared, he proceeded. If “My lord forbids me” appeared, he refrained. If the blank arrow appeared, he returned it and drew again until either the command or prohibition emerged. See al-Nihāyah.

9164 — Jābir reported: We entered Makkah with the Prophet ﷺ while three hundred and sixty idols worshipped besides Allah stood inside and around the Sacred House.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that they be overturned upon their faces and then recited:
“Truth has come, and falsehood has vanished. Indeed, falsehood is ever bound to vanish.” [al-Isrāʾ 17:81]
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then entered the House and performed two units of prayer inside it.
He saw images of Ibrāhīm, Ismāʿīl and Isḥāq inside it. They had depicted the divining arrows in Ibrāhīm’s hand as though he used them to seek decisions.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “May Allah condemn them! Ibrāhīm never sought decisions through divining arrows.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called for saffron and smeared it over those images.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (38060).

9165 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: On the day of Ḥunayn, when Hawāzin confronted the Muslims, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had ten thousand men and the freed people of Makkah with him. They turned and fled. He then related the complete report, which will appear later in full.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4333) and Muslim (135:1059).

9166 — Jarīr reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “The Emigrants and the Anṣār are allies of one another, while the freed people of Quraysh and the emancipated people of Thaqīf are allies of one another until the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (19215) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (2/356–357), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7260).

9167 — Muṭīʿ ibn al-Aswad reported: On the day of the conquest of Makkah, I heard the Prophet ﷺ say, “No Qurashī shall be executed as a captive after this day until the Day of Resurrection.”
Another narration adds: None of the men named al-ʿĀṣī from Quraysh had embraced Islam except Muṭīʿ. His name had been al-ʿĀṣī, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ renamed him Muṭīʿ.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (88:1782).

9168 — Al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn al-Barṣāʾ reported: On the day of the conquest of Makkah, I heard the Prophet ﷺ say, “This city shall not be invaded after this day until the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1611), Aḥmad (15404) and al-Ḥākim (3/627).
The meaning of the report is that no Qurashī would afterward be executed as a captive because of disbelief and apostasy following their entrance into Islam on the day of the conquest. It does not mean that none of them would ever be unjustly executed, for what later befell members of Quraysh is well known. Al-Nawawī explained this.
Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah said: They would never collectively return to disbelief and would not be invaded because of disbelief. Al-Ṭaḥāwī mentioned this in Mushkil al-Āthār (2/228–229).
Likewise, the words “Makkah shall not be invaded after this day” mean that it would not be invaded because its people had collectively returned to disbelief.

9169 — Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “No new tribal alliance is to be formed in Islam, while every virtuous alliance established during the pre-Islamic period is only strengthened by Islam.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2530), from Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah, who said: ʿAbdullāh ibn Numayr and Abū Usāmah narrated to us, from Zakariyyā, from Saʿd ibn Ibrāhīm, from his father, from Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim, who reported it. This is an abridged version; ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported it at greater length.

9170 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: During the year of the conquest, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ addressed the people from the steps of the Kaʿbah. After praising Allah, he said among other things:
“O people, every virtuous alliance established during the pre-Islamic period is only strengthened by Islam. No new tribal alliance is to be formed in Islam, and there is no migration after the conquest.”
“The Muslims are united as one against all others. The lives of all Muslims have equal sanctity. A believer is not to be killed in retaliation for a disbeliever, and the blood money of a disbeliever is half the blood money of a Muslim.”
“Indeed, there is no shighār marriage in Islam, nor the pre-Islamic practices of janab and jalab. Their obligatory charities are to be collected within their own territories.”
“The protection granted by the least of the Muslims is binding upon them all, and the furthest detachment of the Muslims must return a share to the army as a whole.”
He then descended.
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (7012), from Ibrāhīm ibn Abī al-ʿAbbās and Ḥusayn ibn Muḥammad, both of whom said: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī al-Zinād narrated to us, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn Abī Rabīʿah, from ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb, from his father, from his grandfather, who reported it.

9171 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb reported from his father, from his grandfather: When Makkah was conquered for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he said, “Lay down your weapons, except that Khuzāʿah may act against Banū Bakr.”
He permitted them to act until the ʿAṣr prayer had been performed and then said, “Lay down your weapons.”
The following day, a man from Khuzāʿah encountered a man from Banū Bakr at Muzdalifah and killed him.
When this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he stood to deliver a sermon. I saw him leaning his back against the Kaʿbah. He said:
“The people most transgressive before Allah are those who kill within the Sacred Precinct, those who kill someone other than the actual killer in retaliation and those who kill because of the blood feuds of the pre-Islamic period.”
A man stood and said, “So-and-so is my son.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “There is no claim of illegitimate lineage in Islam. The practices of the pre-Islamic period have ended. The child belongs to the marital bed, while the fornicator receives the stone.”
They asked, “What is al-athlab?”
He replied, “The stone.”
He said, “For each finger, the blood money is ten camels; and for each wound that exposes the bone, it is five camels.”
He also said, “There is no voluntary prayer after the dawn prayer until the sun rises, and no voluntary prayer after the ʿAṣr prayer until the sun sets.”
He said, “A woman may not be married together with her paternal aunt or her maternal aunt, and a woman may not give a gift from marital property without her husband’s permission.”
In another narration: When Allah granted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ victory over Makkah, he said, “Lay down your weapons, except that Khuzāʿah may act against Banū Bakr.”
He permitted them to act until they had performed the ʿAṣr prayer and then said, “Lay down your weapons.”
The following day, a man from Khuzāʿah encountered a man from Banū Bakr at Muzdalifah and killed him.
When this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he stood and delivered a sermon, saying:
“The people most transgressive before Allah are those who commit aggression within the Sacred Precinct, those who kill someone other than the actual killer and those who kill because of the blood feuds of the pre-Islamic period.”
A man said, “O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so is my son. I committed fornication with his mother during the pre-Islamic period.”
He replied, “There is no claim of illegitimate lineage in Islam. The practices of the pre-Islamic period have ended. The child belongs to the marital bed, while the fornicator receives al-athlab.”
It was asked, “O Messenger of Allah, what is al-athlab?”
He replied, “The stone.”
“For each finger, the blood money is ten camels; and for each wound that exposes the bone, it is five camels. There is no voluntary prayer after the dawn prayer until the sun has risen, and no voluntary prayer after the ʿAṣr prayer until the sun sets.”
“A woman may not be married together with her paternal aunt or maternal aunt, and a woman may not give a gift from marital property without her husband’s permission.”
“Fulfil the virtuous alliances of the pre-Islamic period, for Islam only strengthens them; but do not establish new tribal alliances in Islam.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6692, 6933, 6681), Ibn al-Jārūd (1052) and al-Bayhaqī (2542), all through routes from ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb, from his father, from his grandfather, in both longer and abridged forms. Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq occurs in some routes, but he explicitly stated that he heard it from ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb; moreover, he was not alone in transmitting it, as Ḥusayn al-Muʿallim and others corroborated him.

9172 — A man from among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ reported: The Prophet ﷺ delivered a sermon on the day of the conquest of Makkah and said:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone. He granted victory to His servant and defeated the confederate armies by Himself.”
In another narration, Hushaym reported the wording: “Praise belongs to Allah, Who fulfilled His promise and granted victory to His servant.”
“Indeed, every object of pride, privilege, blood claim and other claim from the pre-Islamic period is abolished beneath these two feet of mine, except the custodianship of the Sacred House and the provision of water to the pilgrims.”
“Indeed, the victim of quasi-intentional killing”—Hushaym once specified, “with a whip, stick or stone”—“requires an aggravated blood payment of one hundred camels, forty of which must be pregnant.”
In another narration, he said, “Forty camels ranging in age from thaniyyah to mature bāzil, all of them pregnant.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (15388), from Hushaym, who said: Khālid informed us, from al-Qāsim ibn Rabīʿah ibn Jawsh, from ʿUqbah ibn Aws, from a man among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ, who reported it.

9173 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ addressed the people on the day of the conquest of Makkah and said:
“O people, Allah has removed from you the arrogance of the pre-Islamic period and its pride in forefathers.”
“People are of only two kinds: a righteous, God-fearing person who is honored by Allah, and a wicked, miserable person who is insignificant before Allah.”
“All people are the children of Ādam, and Allah created Ādam from dust.”
Allah said:
“O humanity, We created you from a male and a female and made you into peoples and tribes so that you may know one another. Indeed, the most honored of you before Allah is the most God-fearing. Allah is truly All-Knowing, All-Aware.” [al-Ḥujurāt 49:13]
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3270), from ʿAlī ibn Ḥujr, who said: ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar informed us; ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dīnār narrated to us, from Ibn ʿUmar, who reported it.
The Second Sermon

9174 — Abū Shurayḥ al-ʿAdawī reported: He said to ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd while the latter was dispatching military forces toward Makkah, “Permit me, O governor, to relate to you words spoken by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the morning following the day of the conquest.”
“My ears heard it, my heart retained it and my eyes saw him when he spoke it.”
He praised and glorified Allah and then said:
“Allah made Makkah sacred; the people did not make it sacred.”
“It is not lawful for anyone who believes in Allah and the Last Day to shed blood within it or cut down its trees.”
“If anyone attempts to justify fighting within it by citing the fighting of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, then tell him: Allah granted permission to His Messenger, but He has not granted permission to you.”
“I was permitted to fight within it for only a brief period of one day. Its sanctity has now returned today just as it was sacred yesterday.”
“Let those who are present convey this to those who are absent.”
Abū Shurayḥ was asked, “What did ʿAmr say to you?”
He replied: ʿAmr said, “I know this better than you, Abū Shurayḥ. The Sacred Precinct does not shelter a rebel, a fugitive guilty of bloodshed or a fugitive guilty of theft.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4295) and Muslim in al-Ḥajj (446:1354), both through al-Layth ibn Saʿd, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Saʿīd al-Maqburī, from Abū Shurayḥ al-ʿAdawī, who said to ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd and then reported it.

9175 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: On the day of the conquest of Makkah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “There is no migration after the conquest, but striving and sincere intention remain. When you are called to mobilize, then mobilize.”
He also said, “Allah made this city sacred on the day He created the heavens and the earth. It therefore remains sacred by Allah’s decree until the Day of Resurrection.”
“Fighting within it was not permitted to anyone before me, and it was permitted to me for only a brief period of one day. It remains sacred by Allah’s decree until the Day of Resurrection.”
“Its thorny vegetation is not to be cut, its game is not to be frightened, its lost property is not to be picked up except by one who will publicly announce it, and its fresh vegetation is not to be cut.”
Al-ʿAbbās said, “O Messenger of Allah, except idhkhir, for they use it for their blacksmiths and their homes.”
He replied, “Except idhkhir.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Jazāʾ al-Ṣayd (1834) and Muslim in al-Ḥajj (1353), both from the hadith of Jarīr, from Manṣūr, from Mujāhid, from Ṭāwūs, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who reported it.
The Third Sermon

9176 — Abū Hurayrah reported: When Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ victory over Makkah, he stood before the people, praised and glorified Allah and then said:
“Allah restrained the elephant from Makkah and gave His Messenger and the believers authority over it.”
“It was not made lawful for anyone before me. It was made lawful for me for only a brief period of one day, and it shall not be made lawful for anyone after me.”
“Its game is not to be frightened, its thorny vegetation is not to be cut and its lost property is not lawful to take except for one who will announce it.”
“The family of a person who has been killed has the choice between two options: accepting blood money or demanding legal retaliation.”
Al-ʿAbbās said, “Except idhkhir, for we use it in our graves and homes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “Except idhkhir.”
A man from Yemen named Abū Shāh stood and said, “Have this written for me, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Write it for Abū Shāh.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Luqatah (2434) and Muslim in al-Ḥajj (1355), both from the hadith of al-Walīd ibn Muslim, who said: al-Awzāʿī narrated to us; Yaḥyā ibn Abī Kathīr narrated to me; Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated to me, from Abū Hurayrah, who reported it.
The Fourth Sermon

9177 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: During the year of the conquest, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in Makkah, he heard him say, “Allah and His Messenger have forbidden the sale of wine, carrion, swine and idols.”
It was asked, “O Messenger of Allah, what about the fat of dead animals? It is used to coat ships, condition hides and provide oil for people’s lamps.”
He replied, “No, it is forbidden.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said, “May Allah condemn the Jews. When Allah forbade animal fat to them, they rendered it down, sold it and consumed its price.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Buyūʿ (2236) and Muslim in al-Musāqāh (1581), both from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, who said: Layth narrated to us, from Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb, from ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ, from Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh, who reported it.

9178 — ʿAmr ibn Salamah reported: We lived beside a water source situated along a route travelled by the people. Travellers would pass us, and we would ask them, “What is happening with the people? What is happening? Who is this man?”
They would reply, “He claims that Allah has sent him and revealed such-and-such to him.”
I would memorize those words, and it was as though they were being firmly implanted within my heart.
The Arabs were delaying their acceptance of Islam until the conquest. They would say, “Leave him to deal with his people. If he triumphs over them, then he is a true prophet.”
When the conquest took place, every tribe hastened to embrace Islam, and my father was the first of my people to do so.
When my father returned, he said, “By Allah, I have come to you from the true Prophet ﷺ. He instructed: ‘Perform such-and-such prayer at such-and-such time and such-and-such prayer at such-and-such time. When the time for prayer arrives, let one of you call the prayer, and let the one who knows the most Qur’an lead you.’”
They looked among themselves and found no one who knew more Qur’an than I did because of what I had learned from the passing travellers.
They therefore placed me before them as their prayer leader, although I was only six or seven years old.
I was wearing a cloak that would draw up and expose me whenever I prostrated.
A woman from the tribe said, “Will you not cover your reciter’s backside from us?”
They purchased cloth and made a shirt for me. I had never been as delighted by anything as I was by that shirt.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4302).

9179 — Mujāshiʿ ibn Masʿūd al-Sulamī reported: After the conquest, I brought my brother Abū Maʿbad to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, accept his pledge to emigrate.”
He replied, “The migration has passed with those who performed it.”
I asked, “Then upon what will you accept his pledge?”
He replied, “Upon Islam, striving and goodness.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4305–4306) and Muslim (84:1863).

9180 — Abū al-Aswad reported: He saw the Prophet ﷺ accepting the people’s pledges on the day of the conquest. He sat near Qarn Masfalah and accepted their pledges upon Islam and the testimony.
He said: I asked, “What was the testimony?”
He replied: Muḥammad ibn al-Aswad ibn Khalaf informed me that the Prophet ﷺ accepted their pledge upon belief in Allah and the testimony that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is His servant and Messenger.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15431) from ʿAbd al-Razzāq; it is also in ʿAbd al-Razzāq’s Muṣannaf (9820) and al-Ḥākim (3/296).

9181 — ʿĀʾishah reported concerning the women’s pledge: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never touched a woman with his hand. Rather, he would obtain her verbal pledge, and after she gave it, he would say, “Go, for I have accepted your pledge.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (89:1866).

9182 — Umaymah bint Ruqayqah reported: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with a group of women who pledged allegiance to him upon Islam. They said, “O Messenger of Allah, we pledge to you that we shall not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit fornication, kill our children, fabricate falsehood concerning children between our hands and feet or disobey you in what is right.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “To the extent of your ability and capacity.”
She said: We replied, “Allah and His Messenger are more merciful to us than we are to ourselves. Come, O Messenger of Allah, and let us pledge to you by hand.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I do not shake hands with women. My words to one hundred women are the same as my words to a single woman.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (2), al-Tirmidhī (1597), Ibn Mājah (2874), al-Nasāʾī (7/149) and Aḥmad (27006).

9183 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Fāṭimah bint ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah came to pledge allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ, and he required from her the pledge stated in the verse:
“O Prophet, when the believing women come to you pledging that they will not associate anything with Allah, nor steal, nor commit fornication…” [al-Mumtaḥanah 60:12]
She placed her hand upon her head out of modesty, and the Prophet ﷺ admired the modesty he saw in her.
ʿĀʾishah said, “Accept it, woman. By Allah, we pledged only upon these conditions.”
She replied, “Then yes,” and the Prophet ﷺ accepted her pledge according to the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (25175), ʿAbd al-Razzāq in his Muṣannaf (9827), Ibn Ḥibbān (4554) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (70).

9184 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not shake hands with women when accepting their pledges.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6998).
The statement that he did not shake hands with women during the pledge means that he obtained their covenant verbally without physical contact.

9185 — Asmāʾ bint Yazīd reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I do not shake hands with women when accepting the pledge.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (27594).

9186 — Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped at Dhū Ṭuwā, Abū Quḥāfah said to one of his youngest daughters, “My daughter, take me up Mount Abū Qubays so that I may look from it.”
His eyesight had already failed.
She took him to an overlooking point, and he asked, “My daughter, what do you see?”
She replied, “I see a large, dark mass gathered together.”
He said, “That is the cavalry.”
She said, “I see a man moving back and forth among that mass.”
He said, “My daughter, that is the organizer”—meaning the man who commands and directs the cavalry.
She then said, “By Allah, the dark mass has now spread out.”
He said, “Then, by Allah, the cavalry has begun advancing. Hurry me back to my house.”
She descended with him, but the cavalry encountered them before they reached his house.
The girl was wearing a silver necklace. A man encountered her and tore it from her neck.
She said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered Makkah and then entered the Sacred Mosque, Abū Bakr brought his father to him.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said, “Why did you not leave the elderly man in his home so that I could go to him there?”
Abū Bakr replied, “O Messenger of Allah, it is more fitting that he walk to you than that you walk to him.”
The Prophet ﷺ seated him before him, stroked his chest and said, “Embrace Islam.” He then embraced Islam.
When Abū Bakr brought him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, his hair was as white as the thaghāmah plant. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Change the color of this hair.”
Abū Bakr then stood, took his sister by the hand and said, “By Allah and Islam, I appeal to whoever took my sister’s necklace to return it.”
No one responded, so he said, “My dear sister, seek your reward from Allah for your necklace.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26956) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (24/88–89), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7208).

9187 — Anas ibn Mālik was asked about the Prophet’s ﷺ use of hair dye and replied: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had only a few gray hairs. Abū Bakr and ʿUmar after him, however, dyed their hair with henna and katam.
He continued: On the day of the conquest of Makkah, Abū Bakr brought his father, Abū Quḥāfah, carrying and supporting him until he placed him before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Abū Bakr, “Had you left the elderly man in his home, we would have gone to him,” as an honor to Abū Bakr.
Abū Quḥāfah embraced Islam. His beard and hair were as white as the thaghāmah plant.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Change the color of both, but avoid black.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12635) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2981), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5472).

9188 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Hind bint ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, there was once no household on earth whose people I more desired to see humiliated than the people of your household. But today there is no household on earth whose people I more desire to see honored than the people of your household.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “And it will increase further, by Him in Whose hand is my soul.”
She then said, “O Messenger of Allah, Abū Sufyān is a miserly man. Would I be committing a sin if I fed our dependants from his wealth?”
He replied, “No, provided that you take only what is reasonable.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6641) and Muslim (1714).
The word “al-khibāʾ” means a household or dwelling.

9189 — Wahb ibn Munabbih reported: I asked Jābir, “Did they take any spoils on the day of the conquest?” He replied, “No.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3023) and Ibn Saʿd in al-Ṭabaqāt (2/143).

9190 — Umm Hāniʾ reported: During the year of the conquest, I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and found him bathing while his daughter Fāṭimah screened him.
I greeted him, and he asked, “Who is this?”
I replied, “I am Umm Hāniʾ bint Abī Ṭālib.”
He said, “Welcome, Umm Hāniʾ.”
After finishing his bath, he stood and performed eight units of prayer while wrapped in a single garment.
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, my maternal brother ʿAlī claims that he will kill a man to whom I have granted protection—so-and-so ibn Hubayrah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “We grant protection to whomever you have protected, O Umm Hāniʾ.”
Umm Hāniʾ said: This occurred during the forenoon.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (359), al-Bukhārī (3171) and Muslim (336).

9191 — Umm Hāniʾ reported: The Prophet ﷺ entered her house on the day of the conquest of Makkah and performed eight units of prayer. I never saw him perform a prayer more lightly than that one, although he completed the bowing and prostration properly.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4292) and Muslim (80:336).
The two preceding reports may be reconciled by understanding that the Prophet ﷺ first prayed in the residence where he was staying, and Umm Hāniʾ came there to complain concerning ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. The Prophet ﷺ later visited her home and prayed there as well.
Thus, she reported both what she witnessed in the Prophet’s ﷺ residence and what she witnessed in her own home.

9192 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah on the day of the conquest wearing a helmet. When he removed it, a man came and said, “Ibn Khaṭal is clinging to the coverings of the Kaʿbah.”
He said, “Kill him.”
Mālik said: As far as we understand—and Allah knows best—the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was not in the state of consecration that day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (247), al-Bukhārī (4286) and Muslim (45:1357).

9193 — Abū Barzah al-Aslamī reported: I killed ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Khaṭal while he was clinging to the covering of the Kaʿbah.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (19794).

9194 — Saʿd reported: On the day of the conquest of Makkah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ granted safety to the people except for four men and two women, whom he named, including Ibn Abī Sarḥ.
He related the report and said: As for Ibn Abī Sarḥ, he concealed himself with ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called the people to pledge allegiance, ʿUthmān brought Ibn Abī Sarḥ and stood him before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, saying, “O Prophet of Allah, accept ʿAbdullāh’s pledge.”
The Prophet ﷺ raised his head and looked at him three times, refusing each time. After the third request, he accepted his pledge.
He then turned to his Companions and said, “Was there not a discerning man among you who, upon seeing me withhold my hand from accepting his pledge, would rise and kill him?”
They replied, “O Messenger of Allah, we did not know what was in your mind. Why did you not signal to us with your eyes?”
He said, “It is not appropriate for a prophet to employ deceptive signals of the eyes.”
Abū Dāwūd said: ʿAbdullāh was ʿUthmān’s foster brother through nursing. Al-Walīd ibn ʿUqbah was ʿUthmān’s maternal half-brother, and ʿUthmān administered the prescribed punishment upon him when he drank wine.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2683) and al-Nasāʾī (4067).
Al-Wāqidī said in al-Maghāzī (2/725): Those whom the Prophet ﷺ ordered to be killed were six men and four women:
1. ʿIkrimah ibn Abī Jahl.
2. Habbār ibn al-Aswad.
3. ʿAbdullāh ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Sarḥ.
4. Miqyas ibn Ṣubābah al-Laythī.
5. Al-Ḥuwayrith ibn Nuqaydh.
6. ʿAbdullāh ibn Hilāl ibn Khaṭal al-Adramī.
7. Hind bint ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah.
8. Sārah, the freedwoman of ʿAmr ibn Hāshim.
9 and 10. Two singing girls belonging to Abū Khaṭal, named Qarīnah and Quraybah.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said: “I gathered their names from the various scattered reports,” and then listed them.

9195 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: On the day of the conquest, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ approached from the upper part of Makkah riding his mount with Usāmah ibn Zayd seated behind him. Bilāl and ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah, one of the custodians of the Kaʿbah, accompanied him.
He made his mount kneel in the Sacred Mosque and ordered ʿUthmān to bring the key of the House. It was then opened.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered with Usāmah, Bilāl and ʿUthmān. He remained inside for a considerable part of the day and then came out, whereupon the people raced to enter.
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar was the first to enter. He found Bilāl standing behind the door and asked him, “Where did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ pray?”
Bilāl pointed to the place where the Prophet ﷺ had prayed.
ʿAbdullāh said: I forgot to ask him how many units he had prayed.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2988) and Muslim (388:1329).

9196 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived, he refused to enter the Sacred House while idols remained inside it. He ordered that they be removed.
They brought out images of Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl with divining arrows depicted in their hands.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “May Allah condemn them! By Allah, they certainly knew that these two never sought decisions through divining arrows.”
He entered the House, proclaimed Allah’s greatness throughout its various parts and did not pray inside it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1601).
The report of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar is given precedence over that of Ibn ʿAbbās because Ibn ʿUmar affirmed that the prayer occurred, whereas Ibn ʿAbbās denied it and based his denial upon Usāmah’s report.
Bilāl’s affirmation and Usāmah’s denial may be reconciled as follows: when they entered the Kaʿbah, they occupied themselves with supplication.
Usāmah saw the Prophet ﷺ supplicating and then became occupied with his own supplication in one area while the Prophet ﷺ was in another. The Prophet ﷺ subsequently prayed. Bilāl saw him because he was nearby, whereas Usāmah did not see him because of his distance, his preoccupation with supplication and the darkness caused by the closed door.

9197 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: Quraysh became deeply concerned about the woman who committed theft during the Prophet’s ﷺ lifetime in the campaign of the conquest.
They asked, “Who will speak to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on her behalf?”
They said, “Who would dare to do so except Usāmah ibn Zayd, the beloved of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
She was brought before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and Usāmah ibn Zayd spoke to him on her behalf. The face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ changed color, and he said, “Are you interceding concerning one of the prescribed limits of Allah?”
Usāmah said, “Seek forgiveness for me, O Messenger of Allah.”
That evening, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood and delivered a sermon. He praised Allah as He deserves and then said:
“To proceed: Those before you were destroyed because when a person of high status stole, they left him unpunished, but when a weak person stole, they carried out the prescribed punishment upon him.”
“By Him in Whose hand is my soul, if Fāṭimah, the daughter of Muḥammad, were to steal, I would cut off her hand.”
He then ordered that the punishment be carried out upon the woman who had stolen, and her hand was cut off.
Yūnus reported that Ibn Shihāb said that ʿUrwah related from ʿĀʾishah: Her repentance afterward was excellent, and she married. She would later come to me, and I would convey her needs to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4304) and Muslim (8:1688).

9198 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported: On that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered Khālid ibn al-Walīd to enter through the upper part of Makkah by way of Kadāʾ, while the Prophet ﷺ entered by way of Kudā.
Two horsemen from Khālid ibn al-Walīd’s force were killed that day: Ḥubaysh ibn al-Ashqar and Kurz ibn Jābir al-Fihrī.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4280).

9199 — Jābir was asked: “Did you take any spoils on the day of the conquest?” He replied, “No.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3023).

9200 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ remained in Makkah for nineteen days, performing the four-unit prayers as two units.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4298).

9201 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb reported: On the day of the conquest, the Prophet ﷺ performed all the prayers with a single ablution and wiped over his leather socks.
ʿUmar said to him, “Today you have done something that you did not ordinarily do.”
He replied, “I did it deliberately, O ʿUmar.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (277).

9202 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us on an expedition and said, “If you find so-and-so and so-and-so, burn them with fire.”
Then, when we were preparing to depart, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I previously ordered you to burn so-and-so and so-and-so, but no one may punish with fire except Allah. Therefore, if you find them, kill them without burning them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3016).
Al-Tirmidhī (1571) added: “They were two men from Quraysh.”
The first of the two unnamed men was Habbār ibn al-Aswad, who harmed Zaynab, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, when she left Makkah to migrate to Madinah. He continued prodding her camel with his spear until it threw her to the ground. She miscarried the child she was carrying and suffered severe bleeding.
The second man was Nāfiʿ ibn Qays.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dispatched this expedition under the command of Ḥamzah ibn ʿAmr al-Aslamī. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (6/149).
They did not catch Habbār or his companion, so they returned. Habbār later embraced Islam at al-Jiʿrānah after the conquest of Makkah and subsequently came to Madinah. For further details, see al-Iṣābah (8969).

9203 — Ḥamzah ibn ʿAmr al-Aslamī, a Companion of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent him with a group of men against a man from ʿUdhrah and said, “If you capture so-and-so, burn him with fire.”
They set out, but when they had disappeared from his sight, he called them back—or sent someone after them and recalled them. He then said, “If you capture him, kill him, but do not burn him with fire, for no one may punish with fire except the Lord of the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16035, 16036).

9204 — Abū al-Ṭufayl reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Makkah, he sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to Nakhlah, where the idol al-ʿUzzā stood.
Khālid ibn al-Walīd reached it. It stood among hillocks covered with acacia trees, so he cut down the acacia trees and demolished the shrine built over them.
He then returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him, but the Prophet ﷺ said, “Go back, for you have not yet accomplished anything.”
Khālid returned. When the custodians of al-ʿUzzā saw him, they fled deep into the mountain, crying, “O ʿUzzā, drive him mad! O ʿUzzā, blind him! Otherwise, die in humiliation!”
Khālid reached the site and found a naked woman with dishevelled hair, throwing dust upon her head.
He struck her forcefully with his sword until he killed her, then returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him.
The Prophet ﷺ said, “That was al-ʿUzzā.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (902), whose wording is cited here, and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11483).

9205 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to Banū Jadhīmah. He invited them to Islam, but they did not know how to express, “We have embraced Islam.” Instead, they repeatedly said, “We have left our former religion! We have left our former religion!”
Khālid began killing some of them and taking others captive. He handed one captive to each of us.
Then, on a certain day, Khālid ordered every man among us to kill his captive.
I said, “By Allah, I will not kill my captive, nor will any man among my companions kill his captive.”
When we returned to the Prophet ﷺ, we informed him of what had happened. The Prophet ﷺ raised his hands and said twice, “O Allah, I declare my innocence before You concerning what Khālid has done.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4339).
Jadhīmah—with a fatḥah on the letter jīm and a kasrah on the letter dhāl—was Jadhīmah ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAbd Manāh ibn Kinānah.
This expedition took place immediately after the conquest of Makkah in Shawwāl, before the departure for Ḥunayn. The Prophet ﷺ sent Khālid as a caller to Islam, not as a combatant, but he attacked Banū Jadhīmah and killed some of them.
Ibn Isḥāq mentioned it in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/428).

9206 — Ibn Abī Ḥadrad al-Aslamī reported: On that day, I was among the cavalry of Khālid ibn al-Walīd. A young man from Banū Jadhīmah, approximately my own age, had his hands bound to his neck with a rope. A group of women stood not far from him. He called to me, “Young man!”
I asked, “What do you want?”
He said, “Will you take hold of this rope and lead me to those women so that I may fulfil a need with them, after which you may bring me back and do with me whatever you wish?”
I replied, “By Allah, what you have asked is easy.”
I took hold of his rope and led him until he stood before the women. He then said, “Farewell, Ḥubaysh, now that life is about to end”:
Do you remember when I pursued you and found you
 At Ḥilyah, or encountered you at al-Khawāniq?
Did a lover not deserve to be granted his desire
 After enduring night journeys and burning midday heat?
No blame belongs to me, for when our families were together I said:
 Reward my love before one of death’s striking calamities.
Return my affection before separation carries us far away
 And the commander departs with the beloved who must be parted from me.
I have never betrayed the secret of a trust,
 Nor has any beauty after you delighted my eyes.
Only what befell the tribe distracted me from love—
 Unless true mutual affection can overcome even that.
Ibn Isḥāq said: Yaʿqūb ibn ʿUtbah ibn al-Mughīrah ibn al-Akhnas narrated to me from al-Zuhrī, from Ibn Abī Ḥadrad al-Aslamī, who reported that she replied, “And farewell to you. May you be greeted seven and ten times, then once and eight times in succession.”
He said: I then led him back, and his neck was struck.
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as recorded in The Sīrah of Ibn Hishām (2/433–434).

9207 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent out an expedition. They captured spoils and took a man among them. The man said, “I am not one of these people. I fell in love with a woman and followed her here. Allow me to look at her once, and afterward do with me whatever you wish.”
There stood a tall, dark-complexioned woman. He said to her, “Farewell, Ḥubaysh, before life comes to an end”:
Had I followed you and caught up with you
 At Ḥilyah, or reached you at al-Khawāniq,
Would a lover not deserve to be granted his desire
 After enduring night journeys and burning midday heat?
