She replied, “Yes, may I be ransomed for you.” He said: They brought him forward and struck his neck. Then the woman came and stood over him, gasped once or twice, and died.
When they returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they informed him of what had happened. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Was there not a single merciful man among you?”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (8610) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (5/118).

9208 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had completed the Conquest of Makkah, Mālik ibn ʿAwf al-Naṣrī assembled Banū Naṣr, Banū Jusham, Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr, a few groups from Banū Hilāl, and some people from Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir and ʿAwf ibn ʿĀmir. All of Thaqīf also joined him—the Aḥlāf and Banū Mālik. He then marched with them against the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, taking along their wealth, women, and children.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard about them, he sent ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ḥadrad al-Aslamī and said: “Go and enter among the people so that you may gather intelligence about them for us.” He entered among them and remained there for a day or two. He then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him about them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb: “Do you not hear what Ibn Abī Ḥadrad is saying?” ʿUmar replied, “He has lied.” Ibn Abī Ḥadrad said, “By Allah, if you accuse me of lying, O ʿUmar, you have previously rejected the truth.” ʿUmar said, “Do you not hear, O Messenger of Allah, what Ibn Abī Ḥadrad is saying?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “You yourself were once astray, O ʿUmar, and Allah Almighty guided you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sent to Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah and asked to borrow one hundred suits of armor that he possessed, together with their necessary equipment. Ṣafwān asked, “Are you taking them by force, O Muḥammad?” He replied: “No, it is a guaranteed loan, which we shall return to you.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then set out.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/48–49).

9209 — Anas reported: On the Day of Ḥunayn, Hawāzin, Ghaṭafān, and others advanced with their livestock and children. The Prophet ﷺ had ten thousand men with him that day, as well as the freed people of Makkah. They retreated from him until he was left alone. That day he made two distinct calls without mixing one with the other.
He turned to his right and called: “O company of the Anṣār!” They replied, “Here we are at your service, O Messenger of Allah! Rejoice, for we are with you.” Then he turned to his left and called: “O company of the Anṣār!” They replied, “Here we are at your service, O Messenger of Allah! Rejoice, for we are with you.”
He was riding a white mule. He dismounted and declared: “I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger.” The polytheists were then defeated, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ acquired abundant spoils. He distributed them among the Emigrants and the freed people of Makkah but gave nothing to the Anṣār.
The Anṣār said, “When hardship comes, it is we who are called, but the spoils are given to others.” This reached him, so he gathered them inside a tent and said: “O company of the Anṣār, what is this statement that has reached me concerning you?” They remained silent. He then said: “O company of the Anṣār, are you not pleased that the people should depart with worldly possessions while you depart with Muḥammad, taking him back to your homes?” They replied, “Indeed, O Messenger of Allah, we are pleased.”
He then said: “If the people were to take one valley and the Anṣār another mountain pass, I would take the pass of the Anṣār.”
Hishām said: I asked, “O Abū Ḥamzah, were you present for that?” He replied, “Where else would I have been?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4333) and Muslim (1059:135). This wording is Muslim’s.

9210 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: We conquered Makkah and then marched to Ḥunayn. The polytheists came in the finest battle formation I had ever seen. The cavalry formed a line, the fighting men formed another line, the women were positioned behind them, followed by the sheep and then the camels.
We were a large force, having reached six thousand men, and Khālid ibn al-Walīd commanded one flank of our cavalry. Our cavalry began turning back behind us, and it was not long before our horsemen were driven away. The Bedouins and others whom we knew fled as well.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called: “O Emigrants! O Emigrants!” Then he called: “O Anṣār! O Anṣār!”
Anas said: This is a report from an eyewitness. We replied, “Here we are at your service, O Messenger of Allah!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ advanced. By Allah, we had scarcely reached the enemy before Allah defeated them.
We took possession of that wealth and then proceeded to al-Ṭāʾif, where we besieged them for forty nights. We then returned and camped near Makkah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began giving some men one hundred camels each.
Authentic, except for the words “six thousand”: Narrated by Muslim (1059:136).

9211 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We fought Hawāzin alongside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. While we were eating our morning meal with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, a man arrived riding a red camel. He made it kneel, took a rope from the camel’s saddle strap and tethered it, then came forward and ate with the people while looking around.
There were weak men among us, our riding animals were frail, and some of us were on foot. Suddenly, the man hurried away, reached his camel, untied it, made it kneel, mounted it, and urged it forward. The camel sped away, and a man riding a grayish she-camel pursued him.
Salamah said: I set out running. I first reached the rear of the she-camel, then moved ahead until I reached the rear of the man’s camel. I continued forward until I seized the camel’s halter and made it kneel. When its knee touched the ground, I drew my sword and struck the man’s head, causing it to fall.
I then returned leading the camel, with its saddle and the man’s weapons still upon it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the people met me, and he asked: “Who killed the man?” They replied, “Ibn al-Akwaʿ.” He said: “All of his personal spoils belong to him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1754:45)—whose wording this is—and al-Bukhārī (3051).

9212 — Sahl ibn al-Ḥanẓaliyyah reported that they traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn and continued marching for a long time until evening. When it was time for prayer in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, a horseman arrived and said, “O Messenger of Allah, I rode ahead of you until I climbed such-and-such a mountain. I found all of Hawāzin gathered at Ḥunayn, together with their women, camels, and sheep.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled and said: “Those will be the spoils of the Muslims tomorrow, if Allah wills.” He then asked: “Who will guard us tonight?” Anas ibn Abī Marthad al-Ghanawī replied, “I will, O Messenger of Allah.” He said: “Mount.” He mounted his horse and came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who instructed him: “Proceed toward this mountain pass until you reach its highest point, and do not let us be taken by surprise from your direction tonight.”
When morning came, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to his place of prayer and performed two rakʿahs. He then asked: “Have you detected any sign of your horseman?” They replied, “No, O Messenger of Allah, we have not detected him.” The call announcing the commencement of prayer was made, and while praying, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ kept glancing toward the mountain pass.
After completing the prayer and giving salām, he said: “Rejoice! Your horseman has come.” We began looking through the trees in the mountain pass, and there he was, approaching until he stopped before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and greeted him. He said, “I continued until I reached the highest point of this mountain pass, where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had ordered me to remain. When morning came, I surveyed both mountain passes but saw no one.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked him: “Did you dismount at any time during the night?” He replied, “No, except to pray or relieve myself.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ told him: “You have secured the reward; there would be no blame upon you even if you performed no comparable deed after this.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2501), al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (8819), al-Ḥākim (2/83), and al-Bayhaqī (9/149).

9213 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched toward Ḥunayn. He then related the report, which includes: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent to Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah and asked him to lend one hundred suits of armor that he possessed, together with their necessary equipment. Ṣafwān asked, “Are you taking them by force, O Muḥammad?” He replied: “No, it is a guaranteed loan, which we shall return to you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī (6/89), from al-Ḥākim (3/48–49).

9214 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported that a man from Qays asked him, “Did you flee from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn?” Al-Barāʾ replied, “But the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not flee. Hawāzin were skilled archers that day. When we charged them, they retreated, so we fell upon the spoils. They then confronted us with arrows. I certainly saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ upon his white mule, while Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith held its bridle, and he was saying:
I am the Prophet—this is no lie;
 I am the son of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
In another narration, he said: “As for me, I testify that the Prophet ﷺ did not turn away. Rather, the hasty men rushed forward, and Hawāzin showered them with arrows.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4317) and Muslim (1776:80).
This was a magnificent stand by the Prophet ﷺ, demonstrating his confidence in Allah Almighty and his reliance upon Him. He was mounted upon a mule that could not run swiftly, yet he urged it toward the enemy. This is among the clearest proofs of his prophethood ﷺ. Allah Most High sent down what He mentioned:
“Then Allah sent down His tranquility upon His Messenger and upon the believers, and He sent down forces that you did not see.” [Al-Tawbah 9:26]

9215 — Abū Isḥāq reported that a man asked al-Barāʾ, “O Abū ʿUmārah, did you flee on the Day of Ḥunayn?” He replied, “No, by Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not turn away. However, some young and lightly equipped men among his Companions went forward without armor and with little or no weaponry. They encountered a people who were skilled archers, whose arrows scarcely ever missed—the combined forces of Hawāzin and Banū Naṣr. They showered them with arrows and hardly missed their targets.
“They then returned toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was upon his white mule, with Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib leading it. He dismounted, sought Allah’s help and victory, and said:
‘I am the Prophet—this is no lie;
 I am the son of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.’
“He then arranged them in ranks.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2930) and Muslim (1776:78).

9216 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: When we approached the valley of Ḥunayn, we descended into a deep, steeply sloping valley of Tihāmah, through which we could only descend sharply. It was in the darkness of early morning. The enemy had concealed themselves in its mountain paths, bends, and narrow passages, having assembled, prepared, and made themselves ready.
By Allah, while we were descending, nothing startled us except that their battalions suddenly charged us as though they were a single man. The people were defeated and retreated, continuing onward without anyone turning to look at another.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ moved toward the right and called: “Come to me, O people! Come to me! I am the Messenger of Allah; I am Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh.” But nothing could stop the retreat; the camels crowded and collided with one another as the people fled. Only a small group of the Emigrants, the Anṣār, and members of his household remained with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Among the Emigrants who remained steadfast with him were Abū Bakr and ʿUmar. From his household were ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib; al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib; his son al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās; Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith; Rabīʿah ibn al-Ḥārith; Ayman ibn ʿUbayd, the son of Umm Ayman; and Usāmah ibn Zayd.
A man from Hawāzin was riding a red camel at the front of the people. In his hand was a black banner fixed to the head of a long spear, while Hawāzin followed behind him. Whenever he caught up with someone, he stabbed him with his spear; and whenever the people moved beyond his reach, he raised his spear as a signal to those behind him, and they followed him.
Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatādah narrated to me from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Jābir, from his father Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh, who said: While that standard-bearer from Hawāzin was upon his camel doing what he was doing, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and a man from the Anṣār rushed toward him, intending to kill him.
ʿAlī approached him from behind and struck the camel’s hocks, causing it to collapse upon its hindquarters. The Anṣārī sprang upon the man and struck him with a blow that severed his foot halfway up the shin, causing him to fall from his saddle. The people then engaged one another in battle.
By Allah, when those who had fled returned from their retreat, they found the captives already bound before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15027), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1863), and al-Bayhaqī in Al-Dalāʾil (5/126–127).

9217 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: “I certainly saw us on the Day of Ḥunayn, when both forces were turning away in retreat, while fewer than one hundred men remained with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1689) and in Al-ʿIlal al-Kabīr (2/715). He said: “This is a good, authentic, strange hadith.”

9218 — Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib reported: I was present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn. Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and I remained close to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and did not leave him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was riding a white mule that Farwah ibn Nufāthah al-Judhāmī had given him as a gift.
When the Muslims and the disbelievers met, the Muslims turned and retreated. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began urging his mule toward the disbelievers. Al-ʿAbbās said: I was holding the bridle of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ mule, restraining it so that it would not rush forward, while Abū Sufyān was holding the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ stirrup.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “O ʿAbbās, call the Companions of the samurah tree.” Al-ʿAbbās had a powerful voice, so he said: I called at the top of my voice, “Where are the Companions of the samurah tree?” By Allah, when they heard my voice, they turned back as readily as cows return to their calves, saying, “Here we are! Here we are!”
They then fought the disbelievers. The call was directed to the Anṣār: “O company of the Anṣār! O company of the Anṣār!” Then the call was restricted to Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj: “O Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj! O Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ, mounted upon his mule and stretching himself upward, looked toward their fighting and said: “Now the furnace of battle has grown fierce.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then picked up some pebbles and threw them into the faces of the disbelievers. He said: “They are defeated, by the Lord of Muḥammad!” I went to look, and the battle still appeared to be continuing as before. Yet, by Allah, as soon as he threw those pebbles at them, I continued to see their strength weaken and their cause turn toward defeat.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1775:76).
“The Companions of the samurah tree” were those who pledged the Pledge of al-Riḍwān beneath that tree.
“I continued to see their strength weaken” means that their power continued to diminish.
The wording “Farwah ibn Nufāthah gave it to him” also appears in another narration as “Farwah ibn Nuʿāmah.” Al-Nawawī said that the first is the correct and well-known version.

9219 — Kathīr ibn al-ʿAbbās reported: Al-ʿAbbās and Abū Sufyān were with him—meaning the Prophet ﷺ. He addressed them and said: “Now the furnace of battle has grown fierce.” He also said: “Call out: O Companions of Sūrat al-Baqarah!”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1772).

9220 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ reported: We fought at Ḥunayn with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When we confronted the enemy, I advanced and climbed a mountain pass. A man from the enemy confronted me, so I shot an arrow at him. He disappeared from my sight, and I did not know what had happened to him.
I looked toward the enemy and saw that they had emerged from another mountain pass. They met the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ, whereupon the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ turned back. I also returned in retreat. I was wearing two cloaks, one wrapped around my waist and the other over my shoulders. My lower garment came loose, so I gathered the two cloaks together.
As I was retreating, I passed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who was riding his grayish-white mule. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Ibn al-Akwaʿ has certainly witnessed something terrifying.”
When the enemy closed in upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he dismounted from the mule, took a handful of dust from the ground, threw it toward their faces, and said: “May the faces be disfigured!” There was not a single person among them whose eyes Allah did not fill with dust from that handful. They turned and fled, Allah Almighty defeated them, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ distributed their spoils among the Muslims.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1777:81).
The words “I passed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while retreating” describe Ibn al-Akwaʿ, for he had already stated explicitly that he himself retreated. They do not mean that the Prophet ﷺ retreated. None of the Companions ever reported that the Prophet ﷺ fled from any battlefield. They transmitted the Muslims’ consensus that it is impermissible to believe that he fled, and that such a thing was not possible for him.

9221 — Ibn Shihāb reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook the campaign of the Conquest—the Conquest of Makkah. He then set out with the Muslims who accompanied him, and they fought at Ḥunayn. Allah granted victory to His religion and to the Muslims.
On that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah one hundred camels, then another hundred, and then another hundred.
Ibn Shihāb said: Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib narrated to me that Ṣafwān said: “By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave me what he gave me while he was the person I hated most. He continued giving to me until he became the person I loved most.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2313:59).

9222 — Anas reported: On the Day of Ḥunayn, the people retreated from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ except for al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered al-ʿAbbās to call: “O Companions of Sūrat al-Baqarah! O company of the Anṣār!” He then intensified the call specifically to Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj.
When they heard the call, they came forward. By Allah, I could only compare them to camels yearning for their young. When they met the enemy, the battle became fierce, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Now the furnace of battle has grown fierce.”
He ﷺ took a handful of white pebbles, threw them, and said: “They are defeated, by the Lord of the Kaʿbah!” On that day, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib fought more fiercely than anyone else before him.
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (3606).

9223 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said on the Day of Ḥunayn: “Cut them down completely,” and he gestured with his hand toward the throat.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1830).

9224 — Abū Qatādah ibn Ribʿī reported: We set out with the Prophet ﷺ in the year of Ḥunayn. When we met the enemy, the Muslims suffered a temporary setback. I saw a polytheist overpowering a Muslim, so I struck him from behind with my sword upon the junction of his neck and shoulder, cutting through his armor.
He turned toward me and embraced me so tightly that I sensed the approach of death. Death then overtook him, and he released me. I caught up with ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and asked, “What has happened to the people?” He replied, “It is the decree of Allah Almighty.”
The Muslims then returned, and the Prophet ﷺ sat down and declared: “Whoever kills an enemy and possesses evidence of it shall receive his personal spoils.” I asked, “Who will testify for me?” Then I sat down. The Prophet ﷺ repeated the same declaration, so I stood up. He asked: “What is the matter, O Abū Qatādah?” I told him what had happened.
A man said, “He has spoken the truth. The slain man’s spoils are with me, so compensate him on my behalf.” Abū Bakr said, “No, by Allah! He will not deprive a lion from among the lions of Allah, who fights for Allah and His Messenger ﷺ, and give you his spoils!”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “He has spoken the truth, so give them to him.” The man gave them to me, and I used them to purchase an orchard among Banū Salimah. That was the first property I accumulated in Islam.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (18), al-Bukhārī (4321), and Muslim (1751:41).

9225 — Abū Muḥammad, the freedman of Abū Qatādah, reported that Abū Qatādah said: On the Day of Ḥunayn, I saw a Muslim fighting a polytheist while another polytheist was stealthily approaching the Muslim from behind to kill him. I hurried toward the man who was trying to take him by surprise.
He raised his hand to strike me, but I struck his hand and severed it. He then seized me and embraced me so tightly that I became afraid. He eventually weakened and released his hold, so I pushed him away and killed him.
The Muslims retreated, and I retreated with them. I then found ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb among the people and asked him, “What has happened to the people?” He replied, “It is the decree of Allah.” The people then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ declared: “Whoever presents evidence that he killed an enemy shall receive his personal spoils.” I stood up to seek someone who could testify concerning the man I had killed, but I found no one to testify for me, so I sat down. I then reconsidered and mentioned the matter to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
A man sitting with him said, “The weapons of the slain man whom he mentioned are with me. Compensate him on my behalf.” Abū Bakr said, “No! He will not give them to a petty man from Quraysh while leaving a lion from among the lions of Allah who fights for Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then stood and returned the spoils to me. I purchased an orchard with them, and it was the first property I accumulated in Islam.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4322) and Muslim (1751).

9226 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that Hawāzin came on the Day of Ḥunayn with their children, women, camels, and livestock. They arranged them in rows to make their numbers appear greater before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
When the forces met, the Muslims turned and retreated, as Allah Almighty stated. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ called: “O servants of Allah, I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger! O company of the Anṣār, I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger!” Allah then defeated the polytheists.
ʿAffān said: “We did not strike with a sword or thrust with a spear.”
The Prophet ﷺ declared that day: “Whoever kills a disbeliever shall receive his personal spoils.” Abū Ṭalḥah killed twenty men that day and took their spoils.
Abū Qatādah said, “O Messenger of Allah, I struck a man at the junction of his neck and shoulder while he was wearing armor, but I was driven away from him. Find out who took it.” A man stood and said, “I took it. Compensate him for it and give it to me.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was never asked for something without either giving it or remaining silent. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent. ʿUmar said, “No, by Allah! Allah would not grant it as spoils to one of His lions and then have it given to you!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and said: “ʿUmar has spoken the truth.”
Umm Sulaym had a dagger with her. Abū Ṭalḥah asked, “What is this you have with you?” She replied, “I took it so that if any polytheist comes near me, I can rip open his stomach with it.” Abū Ṭalḥah said, “O Messenger of Allah, do you not hear what Umm Sulaym is saying?”
She said, “O Messenger of Allah, kill those freed people who remained behind us; they fled and abandoned you.” He replied: “Allah has sufficed us and granted us an excellent outcome, O Umm Sulaym.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (13975, 12977) and Ibn Ḥibbān (4838).
The words “We did not strike with a sword or thrust with a spear” appear in some narrations in the passive form: “No sword was used to strike, and no spear was used to thrust.”
“The junction of the shoulder” refers to the place near the neck upon which the upper garment rests. It is also said to be a vein or tendon located there.
“I was driven away from him” means that he was removed or made to slip away from him.
Umm Sulaym said, “Kill those freed people who remained behind us; they fled and abandoned you,” because she believed that the freed people of Makkah had caused the Muslims’ initial retreat. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ answered her by saying, “Allah has sufficed us and granted us an excellent outcome,” referring to the victory the Muslims ultimately achieved.

9227 — Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Khālid reported: I saw the mark of a blow upon the hand of Ibn Abī Awfā. He said, “I sustained it while accompanying the Prophet ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn.” I asked, “Were you present at Ḥunayn?” He replied, “I was present even before that.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4314).

9228 — Anas reported that on the Day of Ḥunayn, Umm Sulaym carried a dagger. Abū Ṭalḥah saw her and said, “O Messenger of Allah, here is Umm Sulaym carrying a dagger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked her: “What is this dagger?” She replied, “I took it so that if any polytheist comes near me, I can rip open his stomach with it.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began to laugh.
She said, “O Messenger of Allah, kill those freed people who remained behind us; they fled and abandoned you.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied: “O Umm Sulaym, Allah has sufficed us and granted us an excellent outcome.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1809:134).

9229 — Abū Mūsā reported: When the Prophet ﷺ had finished the Battle of Ḥunayn, he placed Abū ʿĀmir in command of an army and sent it to Awṭās. He encountered Durayd ibn al-Ṣimmah, who was killed, and Allah defeated his companions.
Abū Mūsā said: The Prophet ﷺ sent me with Abū ʿĀmir. Abū ʿĀmir was struck in the knee by an arrow shot by a man from Jusham, and the arrow lodged in his knee. I reached him and asked, “O uncle, who shot you?” Abū ʿĀmir pointed and said, “That is the man who killed me—the one who shot me.”
I headed toward him and caught up with him. When he saw me, he turned and fled. I pursued him, saying, “Are you not ashamed? Will you not stand your ground?” He stopped, and we exchanged two blows with our swords, and I killed him.
I then returned to Abū ʿĀmir and said, “Allah has killed your assailant.” He said, “Remove this arrow.” I pulled it out, and fluid gushed from the wound.
He said, “O son of my brother, convey my greetings of peace to the Prophet ﷺ and ask him to seek forgiveness for me.” Abū ʿĀmir appointed me to command the people after him. He remained alive for only a short while and then died.
I returned and entered upon the Prophet ﷺ in his house. He was lying upon a bed woven with cords, with a mattress upon it, and the bed’s cords had left marks upon his back and sides. I informed him of what had happened to us and to Abū ʿĀmir, and told him that Abū ʿĀmir had asked him to seek forgiveness for him.
He called for water, performed ablution, raised his hands, and prayed: “O Allah, forgive ʿUbayd Abū ʿĀmir.” I could see the whiteness of his armpits. He then prayed: “O Allah, on the Day of Resurrection, raise him above many of Your human creation.”
I said, “Seek forgiveness for me as well.” He prayed: “O Allah, forgive ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays his sin, and admit him to an honorable entrance on the Day of Resurrection.”
Abū Burdah said: One of the two supplications was for Abū ʿĀmir, and the other was for Abū Mūsā.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4323) and Muslim (2498:165).
Abū ʿĀmir’s name was ʿUbayd ibn Sulaym ibn Ḥuḍār al-Ashʿarī. He was the paternal uncle of Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī and was one of the senior Companions.

9230 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported that on the Day of Ḥunayn, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent an army to Awṭās. They encountered an enemy, fought them, overcame them, and took female captives.
Some of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ felt uneasy about having relations with them because they had husbands among the polytheists. Allah Almighty therefore revealed concerning this:
“And also forbidden to you are married women, except those whom your right hands possess.” [Al-Nisāʾ 4:24]
That is, they became lawful for them after their prescribed waiting period had ended.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1456:33).
Awṭās is a valley between al-Ṭāʾif and Ḥunayn. After their defeat, Hawāzin fled to Awṭās. The Prophet ﷺ sent Abū ʿĀmir al-Ashʿarī against them, and he fought them. He was martyred after appointing Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī to succeed him, and Allah granted Abū Mūsā victory.

9231 — Ḥanash al-Ṣanʿānī reported: We accompanied Ruwayfiʿ ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī in a campaign against a town in the western lands called Jarbah. He stood to address us and said: “O people, I will tell you only what I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say. He stood among us on the Day of Ḥunayn and said:
“It is not lawful for a man who believes in Allah and the Last Day to water the crop of another with his own water”—meaning having intercourse with pregnant female captives.
“Nor may he have intercourse with a previously married captive woman until he has ascertained that her womb is free of pregnancy”—meaning after acquiring her.
“Nor may he sell any spoils before they have been divided; nor ride an animal belonging to the Muslims’ war booty until he exhausts it and then return it; nor wear a garment belonging to the Muslims’ war booty until he wears it out and then return it.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2708, 2158, 2159) and Aḥmad (16997). This wording is Aḥmad’s.

9232 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ besieged the people of al-Ṭāʾif but did not achieve anything against them. He said: “We shall return, if Allah wills.”
His Companions said, “Shall we return without conquering it?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ told them: “Go out to fight tomorrow morning.” They went out to fight and suffered wounds. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then told them: “We shall return tomorrow.”
They were pleased with this, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4325) and Muslim (1778:82).

9233 — Umm Salamah reported that an effeminate man was in her home while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was present. He said to Umm Salamah’s brother, “O ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah, if Allah grants you victory over al-Ṭāʾif tomorrow, I shall direct you to the daughter of Ghaylān. She approaches with four folds and turns away with eight.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard him and said: “Such people must not enter upon you.”
Al-Bukhārī added: At that time, he ﷺ was besieging al-Ṭāʾif.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4324) and Muslim (2180:32).

9234 — Abū Najīḥ al-Sulamī reported: We besieged the fortress of al-Ṭāʾif with the Prophet of Allah ﷺ. I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: “Whoever causes an arrow to reach the enemy shall receive one rank in Paradise.”
He said: “That day, I caused sixteen arrows to reach their targets.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17022), Abū Dāwūd (3965), al-Tirmidhī (1638), al-Nasāʾī (6/26), Ibn Ḥibbān (4615), who authenticated it, and al-Ḥākim (2/95), all through Hishām ibn Abī ʿAbdullāh, from Qatādah, from Sālim ibn Abī Najīḥ, from Maʿdān ibn Abī Ṭalḥah, from Abū Najīḥ al-Sulamī. The wording is Aḥmad’s, where the report appears at greater length in its appropriate place.

9235 — Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī reported: I heard Saʿd—who was the first person to shoot an arrow in the cause of Allah—and Abū Bakrah, who had climbed down from the fortress of al-Ṭāʾif with a group of men and come to the Prophet ﷺ, say: We heard the Prophet ﷺ declare: “Whoever knowingly claims descent from someone other than his father, Paradise is forbidden to him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4326) and Muslim (115:62). This wording is al-Bukhārī’s.
Abū Bakrah came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was at al-Ṭāʾif together with a group of enslaved men; they were twenty-three in number and all embraced Islam. Al-Bukhārī mentioned this immediately afterward, saying that Hishām reported from Maʿmar, from ʿĀṣim, from Abū al-ʿĀliyah—or Abū ʿUthmān al-Nahdī—who said: I heard Saʿd and Abū Bakrah narrate from the Prophet ﷺ.
ʿĀṣim said: I said, “Two men have testified before you, and they are sufficient as witnesses.” He replied, “Yes. One of them was the first person to shoot an arrow in the cause of Allah, and the other came down to the Prophet ﷺ as one of twenty-three men from al-Ṭāʾif.” Hishām here is Ibn Yūsuf al-Ṣanʿānī.

9236 — A man from Thaqīf reported: We asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for three concessions, but he did not grant them to us.
We said, “Our land is cold,” and asked him to grant us a concession concerning purification, but he did not grant it. We asked him to permit us to use gourd vessels for preparing intoxicating drinks, but he did not permit them to us even temporarily. We also asked him to return Abū Bakrah to us, but he refused and said: “He is one whom Allah has set free and whom His Messenger has set free.”
Abū Bakrah had come out to the Prophet ﷺ and embraced Islam while he was besieging al-Ṭāʾif.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17530).

9237 — Jābir reported: The people said, “O Messenger of Allah, the arrows of Thaqīf have pierced us, so supplicate to Allah against them.” He instead prayed: “O Allah, guide Thaqīf.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3942) and Aḥmad (14702). Al-Tirmidhī said: “good, authentic, strange hadith.”
The siege of al-Ṭāʾif continued for approximately half a month. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that some vineyards and date-palm groves on the outskirts of al-Ṭāʾif be burned to place pressure upon Thaqīf. Twelve Muslim men were martyred during the siege. Allah ultimately opened the way for their guidance, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed that Thaqīf be guided, after which what was destined to occur took place.
The Prophet ﷺ then returned to al-Jiʿrānah, distributed the spoils, and went back to Makkah to perform ʿumrah.

9238 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀṣim reported: When Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ the spoils on the Day of Ḥunayn, he distributed them among the people whose hearts were being reconciled to Islam, but gave nothing to the Anṣār. They appeared to feel some distress because they had not received what others had received.
He addressed them, saying: “O company of the Anṣār, did I not find you astray and Allah guided you through me? Were you not divided and Allah united you through me? Were you not poor and Allah enriched you through me?”
Whenever he said something, they replied, “Allah and His Messenger have bestowed the greater favor.”
He asked: “What prevents you from answering the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?” Whenever he said something, they replied, “Allah and His Messenger have bestowed the greater favor.”
He said: “Had you wished, you could have said: ‘You came to us under such-and-such circumstances.’ Are you not pleased that the people should depart with sheep and camels while you depart with the Prophet ﷺ to your homes? Were it not for the migration, I would have been a man from the Anṣār. If the people took one valley and mountain pass while the Anṣār took another valley and pass, I would take the valley and pass of the Anṣār. The Anṣār are the inner garment, while the people are the outer garment. After me, you will encounter others being favored over you, so remain patient until you meet me at the Pool.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4330) and Muslim (1061:139).

9239 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ the wealth of Hawāzin, the Prophet ﷺ began giving certain men one hundred camels each. Some of the Anṣār said, “May Allah forgive the Messenger of Allah ﷺ! He gives to Quraysh and leaves us, even though our swords are still dripping with their blood!”
Anas said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was informed of their statement. He sent for the Anṣār and gathered them inside a leather tent, admitting no one else with them.
When they had assembled, the Prophet ﷺ stood and asked: “What is this statement that has reached me concerning you?”
The learned men of the Anṣār replied, “As for our leaders, O Messenger of Allah, they said nothing. But some of our younger men said, ‘May Allah forgive the Messenger of Allah ﷺ! He gives to Quraysh and leaves us, even though our swords are still dripping with their blood!’”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “I give to men who only recently left disbelief, seeking to reconcile their hearts. Are you not pleased that the people should depart with wealth while you depart with the Prophet ﷺ to your homes? By Allah, what you take back with you is better than what they take back with them.”
They replied, “O Messenger of Allah, we are pleased.”
The Prophet ﷺ told them: “You will encounter severe favoritism after me. Remain patient until you meet Allah and His Messenger ﷺ, for I shall be waiting at the Pool.”
Anas said: “But they did not remain patient.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4331) and Muslim (1059:132).

9240 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: On the Day of Ḥunayn, Hawāzin, Ghaṭafān, and others advanced with their livestock and children. The Prophet ﷺ had ten thousand men with him, as well as some of the freed people of Makkah. They retreated from him until he was left alone.
That day he made two distinct calls without mixing one with the other. He turned to his right and called: “O company of the Anṣār!” They replied, “Here we are at your service, O Messenger of Allah! Rejoice, for we are with you.”
He then turned to his left and called: “O company of the Anṣār!” They replied, “Here we are at your service, O Messenger of Allah! Rejoice, for we are with you.”
He was riding a white mule. He dismounted and declared: “I am the servant of Allah and His Messenger.” The polytheists were defeated, and abundant spoils were acquired that day. He distributed them among the Emigrants and the freed people of Makkah but gave nothing to the Anṣār.
The Anṣār said, “When hardship comes, it is we who are summoned, but the spoils are given to others.”
This reached him, so he gathered them inside a tent and asked: “O company of the Anṣār, what is this statement that has reached me concerning you?” They remained silent.
He said: “O company of the Anṣār, are you not pleased that the people should depart with worldly possessions while you depart with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, taking him back to your homes?” They replied, “Indeed, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ then said: “If the people took one valley and the Anṣār another mountain pass, I would take the pass of the Anṣār.”
Hishām said: I asked, “O Abū Ḥamzah, were you present for that?” He replied, “Where else would I have been?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4337) and Muslim (1059:135).

9241 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “O company of the Anṣār, did I not come to you while you were astray, and Allah Almighty guided you through me? Did I not come to you while you were divided, and Allah united you through me? Did I not come to you while you were enemies, and Allah reconciled your hearts through me?”
They replied, “Indeed, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said: “Why do you not say: ‘You came to us afraid and we gave you security; expelled and we gave you shelter; abandoned and we supported you’?”
They replied, “Rather, all favor in this belongs to Allah, Blessed and Most High, and to His Messenger.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12021).

9242 — Anas reported: Around the time of the Conquest of Makkah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ distributed spoils among Quraysh, and the Anṣār became upset.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Are you not pleased that the people should depart with worldly possessions while you depart with the Messenger of Allah?” They replied, “Indeed.”
He said: “If the people took one valley or mountain pass, I would take the valley or pass of the Anṣār.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4332) and Muslim (1059:134). This wording is al-Bukhārī’s.
The phrase “on the Day of the Conquest of Makkah” means during the period of the Conquest of Makkah. These were actually the spoils of Ḥunayn, because no spoils were taken at the Conquest of Makkah.

9243 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported: On the Day of Ḥunayn, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ favored certain people in the distribution. He gave al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis one hundred camels and gave ʿUyaynah the same. He also gave to other Arab nobles and favored them in the distribution that day.
A man said, “By Allah, this is a distribution in which justice was not observed and by which Allah’s pleasure was not intended!”
I said, “By Allah, I shall inform the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” I went to him and told him what the man had said. His face changed until it became as red as ṣirf, and he said: “Who, then, will act justly if Allah and His Messenger do not act justly?”
He then said: “May Allah have mercy upon Moses. He was harmed by more than this, yet he remained patient.”
I said to myself, “Certainly, I shall never convey another such statement to him after this.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4336) and Muslim (1062:140).
“His face became as red as ṣirf”: Ṣirf is a red dye used for coloring leather. Ibn Durayd said that blood may also be called ṣirf.

9244 — ʿAmr ibn Taghlib reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was brought some wealth—or captives—and distributed it. He gave to some men and left others without anything.
He was informed that those whom he had left out were displeased. He praised Allah and extolled Him, then said: “To proceed: By Allah, I give to one man and leave another, although the one whom I leave is dearer to me than the one to whom I give. I give to certain people because of the anxiety and fear that I see within their hearts, while I entrust other people to the contentment and goodness that Allah has placed within their hearts. ʿAmr ibn Taghlib is among them.”
By Allah, I would not exchange this statement of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for the finest red camels.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (923).

9245 — Ibn Shihāb reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook the campaign of the Conquest—the Conquest of Makkah. He then set out with the Muslims who accompanied him, and they fought at Ḥunayn. Allah granted victory to His religion and to the Muslims.
On that day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah one hundred camels, then another hundred, and then another hundred.
Ibn Shihāb said: Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib narrated to me that Ṣafwān said: “By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave me what he gave me while he was the person I hated most. He continued giving to me until he became the person I loved most.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Virtues (2313), through Abū al-Ṭāhir Aḥmad ibn ʿAmr ibn Sarḥ, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Wahb, from Yūnus, from Ibn Shihāb.

9246 — Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah, ʿUyaynah ibn Ḥiṣn, and al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis one hundred camels each. He gave ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās less than that.
ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās said:
Do you place my share and the share of al-ʿUbayd
 Between ʿUyaynah and al-Aqraʿ?
Neither Badr nor Ḥābis ever surpassed
 Mirdās when the people assembled.
Nor was I inferior to either of them;
 Whomever you lower today will not be raised.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then completed his share to one hundred camels.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1060:137).
“My share” means my portion of the spoils. “Al-ʿUbayd” was the name of his horse.

9247 — A Bedouin man reported: I crowded against the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn. I was wearing a thick sandal and stepped upon the foot of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He lightly struck me with a whip in his hand and said: “In the name of Allah! You have hurt me.”
I spent the night reproaching myself and saying, “I hurt the Messenger of Allah ﷺ!” I passed such a night as only Allah knows.
When morning came, a man called, “Where is so-and-so?” I said to myself, “By Allah, this must be because of what I did yesterday.”
I went forward feeling afraid. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me: “Yesterday, you stepped upon my foot with your sandal and hurt me, so I struck you lightly with the whip. Here are eighty sheep; take them in compensation for it.”
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī in Al-Sunan (73).

9248 — Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam and al-Miswar ibn Makhramah reported that when the delegation of Hawāzin came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ as Muslims, he stood to address them. They asked him to return their wealth and captives to them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Those whom you see are with me, and the speech dearest to me is that which is most truthful. Choose one of the two groups: either the captives or the wealth. I had already delayed the distribution while waiting for you.”
After returning from al-Ṭāʾif, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had waited for them for more than ten nights. When it became clear to them that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would return only one of the two groups, they said, “We choose our captives.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood among the Muslims, praised Allah as He deserves to be praised, and then said:
“To proceed: Your brothers have come to us in repentance, and I have decided to return their captives to them. Whoever among you is pleased to surrender his share freely may do so. Whoever wishes to retain his entitlement until we compensate him from the first spoils Allah grants us may do so.”
The people replied, “We freely surrender them, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “We cannot distinguish those among you who have granted permission from those who have not. Return, therefore, until your representatives convey your decision to us.”
The people returned, and their representatives spoke to them. They then came back to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him that they had willingly granted permission.
The narrator said: This is what reached me concerning the captives of Hawāzin.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4318, 4319).

9249 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb reported from his father, from his grandfather, who said: I was present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn. Delegations from Hawāzin came to him and said, “O Muḥammad, we are a people of shared ancestry and kinship. Show us favor as Allah has shown favor to you, for a calamity has befallen us whose severity is not hidden from you.”
He said: “Choose between your women and children on the one hand, and your wealth on the other.”
They replied, “You have given us a choice between our families and our wealth; we choose our children.”
He said: “Whatever belongs to me and Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib is yours. After I perform the noon prayer, say: ‘We seek the intercession of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with the believers, and the intercession of the believers with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, concerning our women and children.’”
They did so.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said: “Whatever belongs to me and Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib is yours.”
The Emigrants said, “Whatever belongs to us belongs to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” The Anṣār said the same.
ʿUyaynah ibn Badr said, “As for what belongs to me and Banū Fazārah, no.” Al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis said, “As for me and Banū Tamīm, no.” ʿAbbās ibn Mirdās said, “As for me and Banū Sulaym, no.”
But the two tribes replied to their leaders, “You have spoken falsely; rather, our shares belong to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “O people, return their women and children to them. Whoever holds on to any portion of the spoils shall receive six camels of the prescribed zakāt-age classes from the first spoils Allah grants us.”
He then mounted his riding animal. The people crowded around him, saying, “Distribute our spoils among us!” They pressed him against a samurah tree, and his cloak was pulled away.
He said: “O people, return my cloak to me. By Allah, if you possessed livestock equal in number to the trees of Tihāmah, I would distribute it among you. You would then find me neither miserly, cowardly, nor dishonest.”
He approached his camel, took a single hair from its hump, placed it between his index and middle fingers, raised it, and said:
“O people, I have no share in these spoils—not even the equivalent of this hair—except the one-fifth, and even the one-fifth is returned for your benefit. Therefore, return even the thread and the needle, for stealing from the spoils will bring its perpetrator shame, fire, and disgrace on the Day of Resurrection.”
A man stood holding a small ball of hair and said, “I took this to repair the saddle pad of my camel, whose back had become sore.”
He said: “Whatever portion of it belongs to me and Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib is yours.”
The man replied, “O Messenger of Allah, now that the matter has reached the seriousness I see, I have no need of it.” He threw it away.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3688), Abū Dāwūd (2694), Aḥmad (7037), and al-Bayhaqī (6/336).

9250 — Abū Mūsā reported: I was with the Prophet ﷺ while he was encamped at al-Jiʿrānah, between Makkah and Madinah, and Bilāl was with him.
A Bedouin came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked, “Will you not fulfill what you promised me?”
He replied: “Receive glad tidings.”
The Bedouin said, “You have told me ‘Receive glad tidings’ too many times!”
The Prophet ﷺ turned toward Abū Mūsā and Bilāl, appearing displeased, and said: “He has rejected the glad tidings, so you two accept them.”
They replied, “We accept them.”
He then called for a vessel containing water, washed his hands and face in it, and rinsed his mouth into it. He said: “Drink from it, pour some over your faces and chests, and receive glad tidings.”
They took the vessel and did so. Umm Salamah called from behind the curtain, “Leave some for your mother.” So they left a portion of it for her.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4328) and Muslim (2497:164).

9251 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mālik ibn Juʿshum al-Mudlijī reported from his father that Surāqah ibn Mālik told him: When the Prophet ﷺ set out as an emigrant toward Madinah, Quraysh offered one hundred camels to anyone who brought him back.
While I was sitting in my tribe’s gathering place, a man came and said, “By Allah, I have just seen three riders pass by me, and by Allah, I believe one of them is Muḥammad, peace be upon him.”
I signaled to him with my eyes to remain silent and said, “They are merely members of such-and-such a clan searching for a lost animal.” He replied, “Perhaps so,” and fell silent.
I remained there for a short while, then stood and entered my home. I ordered that my horse be led to the bottom of the valley. I took my weapons out from behind my dwelling, took the arrows I used for divination, and put on my armor.
I drew my divining arrows, and the result I disliked appeared: that I should not harm him. I had been hoping to bring him back and obtain the hundred camels.
I mounted and followed his trail. While my horse was galloping with me, it stumbled and I fell from it. I again drew my divining arrows, and once more the result I disliked appeared: that I should not harm him. Nevertheless, I insisted upon pursuing him.
I remounted. When the group came into view and I could see them, my horse stumbled beneath me. Its forelegs sank into the ground, and I fell from it. The horse pulled its legs free, and a cloud resembling smoke or dust rose behind them.
I then knew that he had been protected from me and that he would prevail. I called out to them, “Wait for me! By Allah, I shall not cause you alarm, nor will anything you dislike come from me.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Ask him what he wants.”
I said, “Write a document of protection for me.” He wrote it and threw it to me. I then remained silent and did not mention anything that had occurred.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Makkah and completed the campaign of Ḥunayn, I went out to him carrying the document he had written for me.
As I headed toward him, I entered among one of the Anṣār’s battalions. They began prodding me with their spears and saying, “Keep away! Keep away!” until I drew near the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He was riding a she-camel, and I could see his leg in the stirrup, appearing like the heart of a palm tree.
I raised my hand holding the document. The Prophet ﷺ said: “Today is a day of faithfulness and righteousness. Bring him near.”
I then embraced Islam. Afterward, I returned home and later brought my obligatory charity to the Prophet ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim (1029, 1030) and Ibn Abī ʿUmar in his Musnad, as cited in Al-Maṭālib al-ʿĀliyah (9/467).

9252 — Anas reported: The Prophet ﷺ performed four ʿumrahs, all of them during Dhū al-Qaʿdah except the one performed together with his ḥajj:
The ʿumrah from al-Ḥudaybiyah—or during the period of al-Ḥudaybiyah—in Dhū al-Qaʿdah; the ʿumrah in the following year during Dhū al-Qaʿdah; the ʿumrah from al-Jiʿrānah, where he distributed the spoils of Ḥunayn, during Dhū al-Qaʿdah; and the ʿumrah performed with his ḥajj.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4148) and Muslim (1253).
“The ʿumrah in the following year” was the ʿumrah known as ʿUmrat al-Qaḍiyyah, the Compensatory ʿUmrah.
See the remaining hadiths in The Book of Ḥajj and ʿUmrah.

9253 — Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān reported that when ʿUthmān was besieged, he looked out over those surrounding him and said: “I adjure you—and I adjure only the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ—do you not know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘Whoever digs the well of Rūmah shall have Paradise,’ and I dug it? Do you not know that he said: ‘Whoever equips the Army of Hardship shall have Paradise,’ and I equipped it?”
They confirmed that what he said was true.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2778).

9254 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to undertake a campaign, he would usually conceal it by giving the impression that he intended another destination. The Campaign of Tabūk was the exception.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook it during intense heat, facing a long journey through desert terrain and an expedition against a numerous enemy. He therefore made the matter clear to the Muslims so they could prepare properly to face their enemy, and he informed them of the destination he intended.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2948) and Muslim (2769:54).

9255 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samurah reported: When the Prophet ﷺ was equipping the Army of Hardship, ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān came to him carrying one thousand dinars in his garment.
He poured them into the Prophet’s ﷺ lap. The Prophet ﷺ began turning the coins over with his hand and repeatedly saying: “Nothing Ibn ʿAffān does after this day will harm him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3701), Aḥmad (20630), Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in Al-Jihād (82), and al-Ḥākim (3/102).

9256 — Abū Mūsā reported: My companions sent me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to ask him for riding animals, as they wished to accompany him in the Army of Hardship—the Campaign of Tabūk.
I said, “O Prophet of Allah, my companions have sent me to ask you to provide them with mounts.”
He replied: “By Allah, I shall not provide you with any mounts.”
I had unknowingly approached him while he was upset. I returned saddened by the Prophet’s ﷺ refusal and afraid that the Prophet ﷺ might have become displeased with me.
I returned to my companions and informed them of what the Prophet ﷺ had said. Only a short while passed before I heard Bilāl calling, “O ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays!”
I responded, and he said, “Answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ; he is calling you.”
When I came to him, he said: “Take these two paired camels and these two paired camels”—six camels that he had just purchased from Saʿd—“and take them to your companions. Tell them: ‘Allah—or the Messenger of Allah ﷺ—has provided you with these mounts, so ride them.’”
Abū Mūsā said: I took the camels to my companions and said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has provided you with these mounts. But by Allah, I shall not leave you until some of you accompany me to those who heard what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said when I first asked him on your behalf and he refused, and then saw him give them to me afterward. I do not want you to think that I have attributed to him something he did not say.”
They replied, “By Allah, we regard you as truthful, but we shall do as you wish.”
Abū Mūsā went with some of them to those who had heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ initially refuse to provide the mounts and then give them afterward. They related to them exactly what Abū Mūsā had reported.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4415) and Muslim (1649:8).

9257 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out for Tabūk, he appointed ʿAlī as his deputy. ʿAlī asked, “Are you leaving me behind with the women and children?”
He replied: “Are you not pleased that your position in relation to me should be like the position of Aaron in relation to Moses, except that there is no prophet after me?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4416) and Muslim in The Virtues of the Companions (2404:31), both through Shuʿbah, from al-Ḥakam ibn ʿUtaybah, from Muṣʿab ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, from his father Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ.

9258 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet ﷺ set out for the Campaign of Tabūk on a Thursday, and he liked to set out on Thursdays.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2950).

9259 — Wāthilah ibn al-Asqaʿ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ called the people to join the Campaign of Tabūk. I went to my family, but when I returned, the first group of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had already departed.
I began calling throughout Madinah, “Is there anyone who will provide a man with a mount in exchange for his share of the spoils?”
An elderly man from the Anṣār called to me and asked, “Will his share belong to us if we take turns carrying him and provide his food?” I replied, “Yes.” He said, “Then travel with Allah’s blessing.”
I set out with the finest of companions. When Allah granted us spoils, I received some young she-camels and drove them to him.
He came out and sat upon one of his camels’ packs. He said, “Drive them away from me.” Then he said, “Drive them toward me.”
He said, “I see that your young she-camels are fine animals.”
I replied, “They are the spoils that belong to you according to our agreement.”
He said, “Take your young she-camels, son of my brother. It was something other than your share that we sought.”
In another narration, Wāthilah said: I set out to emigrate to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When the people assembled—some coming forward and others standing—the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would approach every seated person he saw and ask: “Do you have any need?” He began with the first row, then the second, then the third, until he came near me.
He asked: “Do you have any need?”
I replied, “Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He asked: “What do you need?”
I replied, “Islam.”
He said: “It is better for you.”
He then asked: “Will you also migrate?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He asked: “The migration of the desert or permanent migration?”
I asked, “Which is better?”
He replied: “Permanent migration means that you remain with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The migration of the desert means that you return to your desert home while remaining obligated to hear and obey during hardship and ease, in what you dislike and what pleases you, and even when others are favored over you.”
I extended my hand and pledged allegiance to him.
He added a qualification for me that I had not added for myself: “To the extent of your ability.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called the people to the Campaign of Tabūk. I went to my family and found my father sitting with his back toward the sun. I greeted him with the greeting of Islam.
He asked, “Have you abandoned your religion?”
I replied, “I have embraced Islam.”
He said, “Perhaps Allah will place good in it for us and for you.”
I was satisfied with this response from him.
While I was with him, my sister came and greeted me. I said, “My sister, provide me with provisions as a woman provides her brother who is departing for battle.”
She brought me some dough in a bucket, with the bucket placed inside a provision bag.
I returned after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had already departed and began calling, “Is there anyone who will provide a man with a mount in exchange for his share of the spoils?”
An elderly man from the Anṣār called to me and asked, “Will his share belong to us if we take turns carrying him and provide his food?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He said, “Then travel with Allah’s blessing.”
I set out with the finest of companions. Beyond what we had agreed, he provided me with additional mounts and gave me food specifically for myself besides the food I shared with him.
When Allah granted us spoils, I received some young she-camels and drove them to him while he was in his tent. I called him, and he came out and sat upon one of his camels’ packs.
He said, “Drive them away from me,” so I drove them away. He then said, “Drive them toward me,” so I drove them toward him.
He said, “I see that your young she-camels are fine animals.”
I replied, “They are the spoils that belong to you according to our agreement.”
He said, “Take your young she-camels, son of my brother. It was something other than your share that we sought.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2676) and al-Bayhaqī (9/28).
The words “It was something other than your share that we sought” appear to mean: “I did not seek your share of the spoils; I sought to share with you in the spiritual reward.” Al-Khaṭṭābī stated this in Al-Maʿālim.

9260 — Yaʿlā ibn Umayyah reported: I accompanied the Prophet ﷺ during the Campaign of the Army of Hardship.
Yaʿlā used to say, “That campaign is the deed of mine in which I place the greatest confidence.”
ʿAṭāʾ said: Ṣafwān reported that Yaʿlā said: I had a hired worker who fought with another man, and one of them bit the other’s hand.
ʿAṭāʾ said: Ṣafwān told me which of the two bit the other, but I forgot.
The man who had been bitten pulled his hand out of the biter’s mouth, causing one of the biter’s front teeth to be pulled out. They went to the Prophet ﷺ, who declared that no compensation was due for the tooth.
ʿAṭāʾ said: I believe he also reported that the Prophet ﷺ said: “Was he supposed to leave his hand inside your mouth so that you could gnaw upon it as a male camel gnaws?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4417) and Muslim (1674:23).

9261 — Abū Hurayrah—or Abū Saʿīd, as al-Aʿmash was uncertain—reported: During the Campaign of Tabūk, the people suffered severe hunger.
They said, “O Messenger of Allah, if you permit us, we could slaughter our camels used for carrying water, eat their meat, and use their fat.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Do so.”
ʿUmar then came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, if they do that, the number of riding animals will become too small. Instead, ask them to bring whatever remains of their provisions, then supplicate to Allah to place blessing in them. Perhaps Allah will provide through that.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied: “Yes.”
He called for a leather mat and spread it out, then asked the people to bring the remnants of their provisions.
One man brought a handful of millet, another brought a handful of dates, and another brought a piece of bread, until a small amount had accumulated upon the mat.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated for blessing in it and then said: “Fill your containers.”
They filled their containers until no container in the entire army remained without being filled. They ate until they were satisfied, and some food still remained.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said: “I testify that there is no deity except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah. No servant will meet Allah with these two testimonies, without doubting them, and then be barred from Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (27:45). It was also narrated by Imām Aḥmad (11080), with the wording: “During the Campaign of Tabūk, the people suffered severe hunger.”

9262 — Abū Hurayrah reported: We were traveling with the Prophet ﷺ when the people’s provisions ran out, to the extent that they considered slaughtering some of the camels carrying their supplies.
ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, perhaps you could gather what remains of the people’s provisions and supplicate to Allah over them.”
He did so.
Those possessing wheat brought their wheat, and those possessing dates brought their dates.
Mujāhid added: “Those possessing date pits brought their date pits.”
The narrator asked, “What did they do with date pits?”
He replied, “They would suck them and then drink water.”
The Prophet ﷺ supplicated over the food until the people filled their provision bags.
He then said: “I testify that there is no deity except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah. No servant will meet Allah with these two testimonies, without doubting them, except that he will enter Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (27:44).

9263 — Faḍālah ibn ʿUbayd al-Anṣārī reported: We accompanied the Prophet ﷺ on the Campaign of Tabūk, and the riding animals became severely exhausted.
The people complained to the Prophet ﷺ about their animals’ exhaustion. He waited until they reached a narrow passage and then traveled through it, saying: “Pass through in the name of Allah.”
The people passed before him with their riding animals, and he began lightly blowing upon their backs while supplicating:
“O Allah, make them carry in Your cause. You provide conveyance upon the strong and the weak, and upon the moist and the dry, on land and at sea.”
Before we reached Madinah, the animals had become so energetic that they pulled against us through their reins.
Faḍālah said: “I understood the Prophet’s ﷺ supplication concerning the strong and the weak, but what did ‘the moist and the dry’ mean?”
Later, when we reached Syria, we took part in the naval campaign of Cyprus. When I saw the ships upon the sea and what they carried, I understood the Prophet’s ﷺ supplication.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23955), Ibn Ḥibbān (4681), and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in Al-Āḥād wa-al-Mathānī (2110).

9264 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was asked, “Tell us about the circumstances of the Army of Hardship.”
He replied: We set out for Tabūk during intense heat. We stopped at a place where we became so thirsty that we thought our necks would break.
A man would go out searching for water and remain away so long that we thought his neck had broken from thirst.
A man would even slaughter his camel, squeeze the liquid from its stomach contents and drink it, then place the remainder over his abdomen.
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said, “O Messenger of Allah, Allah has accustomed you to receiving good when you supplicate, so pray for us.”
He asked: “Would you like me to do that?”
Abū Bakr replied, “Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ raised his hands and had not yet lowered them when a cloud shaded us and poured down rain. The people filled all the containers they possessed.
We then went to investigate and found that the rain had not extended beyond the army’s encampment.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (1383), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1841), and al-Ḥākim (1/159).

9265 — Abū al-Ṭufayl reported: A disagreement of the kind that sometimes occurs between people arose between one of the men associated with the mountain pass and Ḥudhayfah. The man asked him, “I adjure you by Allah, how many were the men of the mountain pass?”
The people said to Ḥudhayfah, “Answer him, since he has asked you.”
He replied, “We used to be informed that they were fourteen. If you were among them, then they numbered fifteen.
“I testify by Allah that twelve of them were at war with Allah and His Messenger in this world and on the Day when the witnesses arise.
“Three were excused because they said, ‘We did not hear the caller of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and we did not know what the others intended.’
“The Prophet ﷺ was traveling through rocky terrain and said: ‘The water is scarce, so no one must reach it before me.’
“He found that some men had preceded him to it, so he cursed them that day.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2997:11).
Al-Nawawī said: This mountain pass was not the famous al-ʿAqabah where the Anṣār, may Allah be pleased with them, pledged allegiance. It was a mountain pass on the road from Tabūk where the hypocrites gathered to betray the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the Campaign of Tabūk, but Allah protected him from them.

9266 — Abū al-Ṭufayl reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was returning from the Campaign of Tabūk, he ordered a caller to announce: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ will take the mountain pass, so no one else is to take it.”
While Ḥudhayfah was leading the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ mount and ʿAmmār was driving it from behind, a group of masked men approached upon their riding animals. They closed in upon ʿAmmār while he was driving the Prophet’s ﷺ mount.
ʿAmmār began striking the faces of their animals. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Ḥudhayfah: “Continue, continue,” until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ descended from the pass.
When he reached the bottom, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted and ʿAmmār returned.
He asked: “O ʿAmmār, did you recognize the men?”
He replied, “I recognized most of their riding animals, but the men had covered their faces.”
He asked: “Do you know what they intended?”
ʿAmmār replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said: “They intended to startle the Messenger of Allah’s mount and throw him from it.”
ʿAmmār asked one of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: “I adjure you by Allah, how many do you know the men of the mountain pass to have been?”
He replied, “Fourteen.”
ʿAmmār said, “If you were among them, then they numbered fifteen.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ excused three of them because they said, “By Allah, we did not hear the caller of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and we did not know what the others intended.”
ʿAmmār said: “I testify that the remaining twelve are at war with Allah and His Messenger in this world and on the Day when the witnesses arise.”
Abū al-Ṭufayl also mentioned that during that campaign, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was informed that the available water was scarce.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered a caller to announce: “No one is to reach the water before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached it, he found that a group of men had already arrived before him, so he cursed them that day.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23792).

9267 — Ḥudhayfah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out during the Campaign of Tabūk and was informed that the water was scarce.
He ordered a caller to announce among the people: “No one is to reach the water before me.”
When he reached the water, he found that some men had preceded him, so he cursed them.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23395).

9268 — Maḥmūd ibn Labīd reported from some men of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal. The narrator said: I asked Maḥmūd, “Did the people recognize the hypocrites among them?”
He replied, “Yes, by Allah. A man would recognize hypocrisy in his brother, father, paternal uncle, or other member of his clan, yet they continued to maintain normal relations with one another despite that.”
Maḥmūd then said: Some men from my tribe informed me about a hypocrite whose hypocrisy was well known. He traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wherever he went.
After what occurred to the people at al-Ḥijr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated, and Allah sent a cloud that rained until the people had all quenched their thirst. We approached the hypocrite and said, “Woe to you! Can there be any doubt after this?”
He replied, “It was merely a passing cloud.”
Ibn Isḥāq said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued traveling until, at one point along the road, his she-camel went missing. His Companions went out to search for it.
A Companion named ʿUmārah ibn Ḥazm was with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He had participated in the pledge at al-ʿAqabah and the Battle of Badr and was the paternal uncle of Banū ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm.
Traveling with him was Zayd ibn al-Luṣayt of Banū Qaynuqāʿ, who was a hypocrite.
Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn Qatādah narrated to me from Maḥmūd ibn Labīd, from some men of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, who said:
While Zayd ibn al-Luṣayt was staying at ʿUmārah’s encampment and ʿUmārah was with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Zayd said, “Does Muḥammad not claim to be a prophet and inform you about news from heaven, while he does not even know where his she-camel is?”
While ʿUmārah was with him, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A man has said: ‘This Muḥammad informs you that he is a prophet and claims to tell you about matters from heaven, yet he does not know where his she-camel is.’ By Allah, I know only what Allah teaches me. Allah has now shown me where it is. It is in this valley, in such-and-such a mountain path, where a tree has caught its reins. Go and bring it to me.”
They went and brought it back.
ʿUmārah ibn Ḥazm returned to his encampment and said, “By Allah, something astonishing has just happened. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed us of a statement made by a man, which Allah had disclosed to him. The man said such-and-such”—mentioning the words of Zayd ibn al-Luṣayt.
A man who had been at ʿUmārah’s encampment but had not been present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “By Allah, Zayd made that very statement before you arrived.”
ʿUmārah turned upon Zayd, striking him on the neck and saying, “Come here, servants of Allah! A calamity has been staying in my encampment without my knowledge! Get out of my camp, enemy of Allah, and do not accompany me!”
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq. Sīrat Ibn Hishām (2/522–523).

9269 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: When the Prophet ﷺ passed through al-Ḥijr, he said:
“Do not enter the dwellings of those who wronged themselves, lest what afflicted them should afflict you, unless you enter weeping.”
He then covered his head and hastened onward until he had passed beyond the valley.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4419) and Muslim (2980).

9270 — Sabrah ibn Maʿbad al-Juhanī reported that when the Prophet ﷺ stopped at al-Ḥijr, he told his Companions:
“Whoever has kneaded anything using water from this—their well—must throw it away.”
Those who had prepared dough threw away their dough, and those who had prepared ḥays threw away their ḥays.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (7/136) and al-Ḥākim (2/566).
Al-Ḥijr was the dwelling place of the people of Thamūd. The Messenger ﷺ and his Companions passed through it during the Campaign of Tabūk.

9271 — Muḥammad ibn Abī Kabshah al-Anmārī reported from his father—whose name was ʿAmr ibn Saʿd, though it is also said to have been ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd—who said:
During the Campaign of Tabūk, the people hastened to enter the dwellings of the inhabitants of al-Ḥijr. When this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he called among the people: “Gather for prayer.”
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was holding his camel, and he was saying:
“Why are you entering upon a people with whom Allah was angry?”
One of the men called to him, “We are amazed by them, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said: “Shall I not tell you of something more astonishing than that? A man from among yourselves informs you of what occurred before you and what will occur after you. Remain upright and follow the proper course, for your punishment would be of no consequence to Allah; and there will come a people who will be utterly unable to protect themselves.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (18029), al-Ṭabarānī (22/340–341), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Mushkil al-Āthār (3741). This wording is Aḥmad’s.

9272 — Abū Ḥumayd reported: We set out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Campaign of Tabūk. When we reached Wādī al-Qurā, we passed an orchard belonging to a woman.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Estimate its produce.”
We estimated it, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ estimated it at ten wasqs. He told the woman: “Keep an accurate account of it until we return to you, if Allah wills.”
We continued until we reached Tabūk.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “A violent wind will blow upon you tonight. None of you should stand during it, and whoever has a camel must securely fasten its tether.”
A violent wind blew. One man stood up, and the wind carried him away until it threw him at the two mountains of Ṭayyiʾ.
The messenger of Ibn al-ʿUlamāʾ, the ruler of Aylah, came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with a letter and presented him with a white mule.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote a reply to him and presented him with a mantle.
We then continued on our return until we reached Wādī al-Qurā. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked the woman about her orchard: “How much produce did it yield?”
She replied, “Ten wasqs.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I intend to travel quickly. Whoever wishes may hasten with me, and whoever wishes may remain behind.”
We continued until we came within sight of Madinah. He said:
“This is Ṭābah, and this is Uḥud—a mountain that loves us and that we love.”
He then said:
“The best of the Anṣār’s neighborhoods is the neighborhood of Banū al-Najjār, followed by that of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, then that of Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj, and then that of Banū Sāʿidah. There is goodness in every neighborhood of the Anṣār.”
Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah caught up with us, and Abū Usayd said to him, “Do you not see that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ranked the neighborhoods of the Anṣār and placed us last?”
Saʿd caught up with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, you ranked the neighborhoods of the Anṣār and placed us last.”
He replied: “Is it not sufficient for you to be among the best?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1481) and Muslim (1392:11).

9273 — Shihāb al-ʿAnbarī reported: A companion and I came to Ibn ʿAbbās and encountered Abū Hurayrah at Ibn ʿAbbās’s door.
He asked, “Who are you?” We told him.
He said, “Go to some people whose knowledge consists of dates and water; every valley flows according to its capacity.”
We replied, “May your goodness increase. Seek permission for us to enter upon Ibn ʿAbbās.”
He then sought permission for us.
We heard Ibn ʿAbbās narrating from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ delivered a sermon on the Day of Tabūk and said:
“Among the people, none is like a man who holds the reins of his horse, striving in the cause of Allah and avoiding the evils of the people; or like a man living in the desert among his sheep, who honors his guest and fulfills the rights due from him.”
I asked, “Did he truly say that?”
He replied, “He said it.”
I asked again, “Did he truly say that?”
He replied, “He said it.”
I asked a third time, “Did he truly say that?”
He replied, “He said it.”
I then proclaimed Allah’s greatness, praised Allah, and gave thanks.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1987, 2837), Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in Al-Jihād (154), and al-Ḥākim (2/67).

9274 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported: I never remained behind when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went on a military campaign except during the Campaign of Tabūk. I had also remained behind during the Campaign of Badr, but no one who remained behind on that occasion was reproached.
