The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had set out only to intercept the caravan of Quraysh, until Allah brought the Muslims and their enemy together without a prior appointment.
I was present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the night of al-ʿAqabah, when we pledged ourselves firmly to Islam. I would not exchange my attendance at al-ʿAqabah for attendance at Badr, even though Badr is more widely remembered among the people.
As for my circumstances, I had never been stronger or more prosperous than I was when I remained behind during that campaign. By Allah, I had never possessed two riding animals at the same time before that campaign, but during it I possessed two.
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended a campaign, he would conceal its true destination by giving the impression that he intended somewhere else. This continued until that campaign. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook it during intense heat, facing a long journey through desert terrain and a numerous enemy. He therefore made the matter clear to the Muslims so they could prepare properly for the campaign, and he informed them of the destination he intended.
The Muslims accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were numerous, and no comprehensive register contained all their names—meaning a military record.
Kaʿb said: Any man who wished to absent himself assumed that his absence would go unnoticed unless revelation from Allah came down concerning him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook that campaign at a time when the fruits had ripened and the shade had become pleasant.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims accompanying him began preparing. I would go out in the morning intending to prepare with them, but I would return without accomplishing anything, saying to myself, “I am capable of preparing whenever I wish.”
I continued delaying until the people became fully serious about their departure. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims accompanying him set out, while I had still made none of my preparations.
I said to myself, “I shall prepare a day or two after his departure and then catch up with them.”
After they had departed, I went out in the morning to prepare, but returned without accomplishing anything. The following morning I went out again, yet once more returned without accomplishing anything.
I continued in this condition until they had traveled swiftly ahead and the campaign had passed me by. I considered setting out to catch up with them—and I wish I had done so—but that was not decreed for me.
Whenever I went out among the people after the departure of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and walked among those who remained, it saddened me that I saw no one except a man suspected of hypocrisy or someone among the weak whom Allah had excused.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not mention me until he reached Tabūk. While sitting among the people there, he asked: “What has happened to Kaʿb?”
A man from Banū Salimah replied, “O Messenger of Allah, his fine garments and his admiration of his appearance detained him.”
Muʿādh ibn Jabal said, “What an evil thing you have said! By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, we have known nothing but good about him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained silent.
Kaʿb ibn Mālik continued: When I learned that the Prophet ﷺ had begun his return journey, anxiety came upon me. I began thinking of false excuses and asking myself, “How will I escape his displeasure tomorrow?” I sought the assistance of every wise member of my family in devising an excuse.
But when it was announced that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was about to arrive, every false idea vanished from me. I realized that I could never escape the consequences by using any lie, so I resolved to tell him the truth.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in the morning. Whenever he returned from a journey, he would begin by going to the mosque, where he would perform two rakʿahs and then sit to receive the people.
When he had done so, those who had remained behind came to him. They began presenting excuses and swearing oaths to him. They numbered a little over eighty men.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted their outward declarations, renewed their pledges of allegiance, sought forgiveness for them, and entrusted their hidden intentions to Allah.
I came to him, and when I greeted him, he smiled the smile of one who was angry. He then said: “Come forward.”
I walked toward him and sat before him. He asked: “What caused you to remain behind? Had you not purchased your riding animal?”
I replied, “Indeed, I had. By Allah, if I were sitting before anyone else in this world, I am confident that I could escape his displeasure by presenting an excuse, for I have certainly been granted skill in argument.”
“But, by Allah, I know that if I tell you a lie today that makes you pleased with me, Allah may soon make you displeased with me. If I tell you the truth, however, and you become displeased with me because of it, I can still hope for Allah’s pardon.
“No, by Allah, I had no excuse. By Allah, I had never been stronger or more prosperous than I was when I remained behind you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “As for this man, he has spoken the truth. Rise and wait until Allah passes judgment concerning you.”
I rose, and some men from Banū Salimah sprang up and followed me. They said, “By Allah, we never knew you to have committed any sin before this. Yet you were incapable of presenting an excuse to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ like those who remained behind! The Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ prayer for forgiveness would have been enough to erase your sin.”
By Allah, they continued reproaching me until I nearly returned to the Prophet ﷺ and declared my own earlier statement false.
Then I asked them, “Has anyone else experienced the same thing as I have?”
They replied, “Yes. Two men said the same thing you said, and they were given the same response you received.”
I asked, “Who are they?”
They replied, “Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ al-ʿAmrī and Hilāl ibn Umayyah al-Wāqifī.”
They named two righteous men who had participated in Badr and whose example could strengthen me.
When they mentioned them, I continued on my way.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ forbade the Muslims from speaking to the three of us, distinguishing us from all the others who had remained behind.
The people avoided us and changed their behavior toward us, until the very earth seemed unfamiliar to me and no longer appeared to be the land I knew.
We remained in that condition for fifty nights.
My two companions submitted quietly, remained in their homes, and wept.
As for me, I was the youngest and strongest of the three. I would go out, attend the prayers with the Muslims, and walk through the markets, but no one would speak to me.
I would approach the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and greet him while he sat among the people after prayer. I would ask myself, “Did he move his lips to return my greeting, or did he not?”
I would then pray close to him and steal glances at him. Whenever I turned my attention toward my prayer, he would look at me; but whenever I turned toward him, he would turn away from me.
When the people’s estrangement became too difficult for me, I walked to the orchard of Abū Qatādah, my cousin and the person dearest to me, and climbed over its wall.
I greeted him, but by Allah, he did not return my greeting.
I said, “Abū Qatādah, I adjure you by Allah: Do you know that I love Allah and His Messenger?”
He remained silent.
I repeated the question and adjured him again, but he remained silent. I repeated it a third time and adjured him.
He replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
My eyes overflowed with tears. I turned away and climbed back over the wall.
While I was walking through the marketplace of Madinah, I saw a Nabatean farmer from Syria who had come to sell food in Madinah. He was asking, “Who will direct me to Kaʿb ibn Mālik?”
The people began pointing toward me. When he reached me, he handed me a letter from the Ghassānid king.
It read: “To proceed: We have been informed that your companion has treated you harshly. Allah has not placed you in a land of humiliation and neglect. Join us, and we shall console and support you.”
When I read it, I said, “This too is part of the trial.”
I took the letter to the oven and burned it.
When forty of the fifty nights had passed, a messenger from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to me and said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commands you to separate yourself from your wife.”
I asked, “Should I divorce her, or what should I do?”
He replied, “No. Separate yourself from her and do not approach her.”
The Prophet ﷺ sent the same command to my two companions.
I told my wife, “Go to your family and remain with them until Allah judges this matter.”
Kaʿb continued: The wife of Hilāl ibn Umayyah came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, Hilāl ibn Umayyah is an elderly, helpless man who has no servant. Would you object if I continued serving him?”
He replied: “No, but he must not approach you.”
She said, “By Allah, he has no desire for anything. By Allah, he has continued weeping from the time this matter began until this very day.”
Some members of my family said to me, “Why do you not seek permission from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning your wife, just as he permitted the wife of Hilāl ibn Umayyah to serve him?”
I replied, “By Allah, I shall not seek the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ permission concerning her. How do I know what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say if I sought his permission, when I am a young man?”
I remained in that condition for another ten nights, completing fifty nights from the time the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had forbidden the people from speaking to us.
After I performed the dawn prayer on the morning of the fiftieth night, I was upon the roof of one of our houses. As I sat in the condition Allah described—my soul had become constrained for me and the earth, despite all its vastness, had become constrained—I heard someone who had climbed Mount Salʿ calling at the top of his voice: “Kaʿb ibn Mālik, rejoice!”
I immediately fell in prostration and knew that relief had arrived.
After performing the dawn prayer, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ announced that Allah had accepted our repentance.
The people set out to bring us the glad tidings, and other bearers of good news went toward my two companions.
One man galloped toward me on horseback, while another man from Aslam ran and climbed the mountain. The voice reached me faster than the horse.
When the man whose voice I had heard came to congratulate me, I removed my two garments and gave them to him as a reward for his glad tidings.
By Allah, I possessed no other garments that day.
I borrowed two garments, put them on, and set out toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
People met me group after group, congratulating me upon the acceptance of my repentance and saying, “May Allah’s acceptance of your repentance bring you happiness.”
Kaʿb said: I entered the mosque and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ seated with the people around him.
Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbaydillāh rose and hurried toward me, shook my hand, and congratulated me.
By Allah, no other man from the Emigrants stood to greet me, and I shall never forget this kindness of Ṭalḥah.
Kaʿb continued: When I greeted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, his face was radiant with joy, and he said:
“Rejoice in the best day you have experienced since your mother gave birth to you.”
I asked, “Is this from you, O Messenger of Allah, or from Allah?”
He replied: “No, it is from Allah.”
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was pleased, his face would shine as though it were a piece of the moon, and we recognized this quality in him.
When I sat before him, I said, “O Messenger of Allah, as part of my repentance, I wish to relinquish all my wealth as charity for Allah and His Messenger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Retain some of your wealth, for that is better for you.”
I replied, “Then I shall retain my share at Khaybar.”
I then said, “O Messenger of Allah, Allah saved me only because I spoke the truth. Part of my repentance is that I shall speak nothing but the truth for as long as I live.”
By Allah, I do not know of any Muslim whom Allah has tested and blessed in truthfulness, from the time I made that pledge to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, more favorably than He has tested and blessed me.
From the day I made that pledge to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until this very day, I have never deliberately told a lie. I hope Allah will preserve me in truthfulness throughout the remainder of my life.
Allah revealed to His Messenger ﷺ:
“Allah has certainly accepted the repentance of the Prophet and the Emigrants…” [Al-Tawbah 9:117]
through His words:
“And be with the truthful.” [Al-Tawbah 9:119]
By Allah, after guiding me to Islam, Allah has never bestowed upon me a blessing greater in my estimation than my truthfulness to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. I did not lie to him and consequently perish as those who lied perished.
When revelation came down, Allah spoke about those who lied in the harshest terms He used concerning anyone. He, Blessed and Most High, said:
“They will swear by Allah to you when you return to them…” through His words:
“Indeed, Allah is not pleased with the defiantly disobedient people.” [Al-Tawbah 9:95–96]
Kaʿb said: The matter of the three of us was deferred, unlike the matter of those whose excuses the Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted when they swore their oaths to him, renewed their pledges, and received his prayer for forgiveness.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ postponed judgment concerning us until Allah decided our case.
It was concerning this that Allah said:
“And also the three whose case had been deferred.” [Al-Tawbah 9:118]
The words Allah used concerning our being “deferred” do not refer merely to our remaining behind from the campaign. Rather, they refer to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ postponing our case and separating it from the cases of those who swore oaths and offered excuses that he accepted.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4418) and Muslim (2769:53).
The statement, “The Muslims accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were numerous, and no comprehensive register contained them,” is explained in another narration recorded by Muslim (2769:55): “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went on the campaign with a large number of people exceeding ten thousand, and no comprehensive military register contained their names.”

9275 — Jābir reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained at Tabūk for twenty days, shortening the prayers.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1235), Aḥmad (14139), Ibn Ḥibbān (2749), and al-Bayhaqī (3/152).

9276 — Abū Ḥumayd al-Sāʿidī reported: We returned with the Prophet ﷺ from the Campaign of Tabūk. When we came within sight of Madinah, he said:
“This is Ṭābah, and this is Uḥud—a mountain that loves us and that we love.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4422) and Muslim (1392:503).

9277 — Anas reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from the Campaign of Tabūk and drew near Madinah, he said:
“There are people in Madinah who accompanied you in reward whenever you traveled any distance or crossed any valley.”
They asked, “O Messenger of Allah, even though they remained in Madinah?”
He replied: “Even though they remained in Madinah; a valid excuse prevented them from joining you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4423).

9278 — Al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd reported: I remember going out with the children to Thaniyyat al-Wadāʿ to welcome the Prophet ﷺ when he returned from the Campaign of Tabūk.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4427), through ʿAbdullāh ibn Muḥammad, from Sufyān, from al-Zuhrī, from al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd.

9279 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported: A silk garment was presented to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. His Companions began touching it and expressing amazement at its softness.
He said: “Are you amazed by its softness? The handkerchiefs of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh in Paradise are better and softer than this.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3802) and Muslim (2468:126).

9280 — Anas reported: A robe of fine silk was presented to the Prophet ﷺ, although he prohibited men from wearing silk. The people were amazed by it.
He said: “By the One in whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, the handkerchiefs of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh in Paradise are finer than this.”
Another narration states that Ukaydir of Dūmat al-Jandal presented a garment to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2615) and Muslim (2469:127). Muslim also narrated the additional version in the same location.

9281 — Qays ibn al-Nuʿmān reported: A neighbor of mine completed his recitation of the Qur’an before ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
He related: The cavalry of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out, and Ukaydir of Dūmat al-Jandal heard about it. He came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have learned that your cavalry has departed, and I fear for my land and wealth. Write me a document ensuring that nothing belonging to me will be harmed, for I acknowledge the obligation I owe.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote a document for him.
Ukaydir then brought out a cloak woven with gold, of the kind that the Persian emperor used to bestow upon them.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Take your cloak back, for no one wears something like this in the world except that he will be deprived of it in the Hereafter.”
The man took it back. When he reached his residence, he felt distressed that his gift had been rejected. He returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, we are a family who find it difficult to bear the rejection of our gift, so please accept my gift.”
He told him: “Go and give it to ʿUmar.”
ʿUmar had heard what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had said, so he asked, “Has something new been revealed concerning me? You said about this cloak what I heard, and then you sent it to me?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed until he placed his hand over his mouth. He then said: “I did not send it to you so that you could wear it. I sent it so that you could sell it and benefit from its price.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā, as cited in Al-Maṭālib al-ʿĀliyah (2237), and in an abbreviated form by Ibn Qāniʿ in Muʿjam al-Ṣaḥābah (2/351).

9282 — Abū Hurayrah reported that during the pilgrimage over which the Prophet ﷺ appointed Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq before the Farewell Pilgrimage, Abū Bakr sent him on the Day of Sacrifice with a group to announce among the people:
“After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4363) and Muslim (1347).
Another narration in al-Bukhārī (369) states that Abū Hurayrah said: During that pilgrimage, Abū Bakr sent me with the announcers on the Day of Sacrifice to proclaim at Minā:
“After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”
Ḥumayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then sent ʿAlī after them and instructed him to proclaim Sūrat Barāʾah.
Abū Hurayrah said: On the Day of Sacrifice, ʿAlī joined us in announcing among the people of Minā: “After this year, no polytheist may perform pilgrimage, and no naked person may circumambulate the House.”

9283 — ʿAlī reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent me to Yemen. I said, “O Messenger of Allah, you are sending me to people who are older than I am so that I may judge between them.”
He replied: “Go, for Allah Most High will make your tongue firm and guide your heart.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (636, 661) and Ibn Mājah (2309).

9284 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent ʿAlī to Khālid to take possession of the one-fifth share of the spoils. I disliked ʿAlī, and he had performed a ritual bath.
I said to Khālid, “Do you not see what this man has done?”
When we returned to the Prophet ﷺ, I mentioned the matter to him. He asked: “O Buraydah, do you dislike ʿAlī?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He said: “Do not dislike him, for he is entitled to more than that from the one-fifth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4350).
“To take possession of the one-fifth” means one-fifth of the spoils. Another narration reads: “To distribute the one-fifth.”
Buraydah’s statement, “I disliked ʿAlī,” arose because he saw that ʿAlī had taken a captive woman from the spoils and assumed that he had acted dishonestly. When the Prophet ﷺ informed him that ʿAlī had taken less than his rightful share, Buraydah came to love him, as stated in the following hadith:

9285 — Buraydah reported: I disliked ʿAlī with an intensity with which I had never disliked anyone else.
I loved a man from Quraysh only because he also disliked ʿAlī.
That man was appointed to command a cavalry force, and I accompanied him solely because of his dislike for ʿAlī.
We acquired captives, so he wrote to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asking him to send someone to divide the spoils and set aside the one-fifth.
The Prophet ﷺ sent ʿAlī to us.
Among the captives was a young woman who was one of the finest captives. ʿAlī set aside the one-fifth and distributed the remainder.
He later came out with water dripping from his head. We asked, “Abū al-Ḥasan, what is this?”
He replied, “Did you not see the young woman who was among the captives? I divided the spoils and set aside the one-fifth. She became part of the one-fifth, then part of the share belonging to the Prophet’s ﷺ household, and thereafter part of ʿAlī’s family share, so I had relations with her.”
The commander wrote to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ concerning the matter.
I said, “Send me with the letter,” so he sent me to confirm its contents.
I began reading the letter and saying after every point, “He has spoken the truth.”
The Prophet ﷺ took hold of my hand and the letter and asked: “Do you dislike ʿAlī?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He said: “Then do not dislike him. If you already love him, increase your love for him. By the One in whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, the share belonging to ʿAlī’s family from the one-fifth is greater than a single young woman.”
Buraydah said: After this statement of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, no one among the people was dearer to me than ʿAlī.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22967).

9286 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported: While ʿAlī was in Yemen, he sent the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a piece of unrefined gold still mixed with soil.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ divided it among four men: al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis al-Ḥanẓalī, ʿUyaynah ibn Badr al-Fazārī, ʿAlqamah ibn ʿUlāthah al-ʿĀmirī of Banū Kilāb, and Zayd al-Khayr al-Ṭāʾī of Banū Nabhān.
Quraysh became angry and said, “Do you give to the chiefs of Najd while leaving us?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied: “I have done so only to reconcile their hearts.”
A man with a thick beard, prominent cheekbones, sunken eyes, a protruding forehead, and a shaved head came forward and said, “Fear Allah, O Muḥammad!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied: “Who would obey Allah if I disobeyed Him? He entrusts me with the people of the earth, yet you do not trust me?”
The man then turned away. Another man from the gathering—whom the narrators believed to be Khālid ibn al-Walīd—asked permission to kill him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“From the progeny or kind of this man will emerge people who recite the Qur’an, but it will not pass beyond their throats. They will kill the people of Islam and leave the idolaters alone. They will pass through Islam just as an arrow passes through its target. If I live to encounter them, I shall destroy them as the people of ʿĀd were destroyed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7432) and Muslim (1064:143).

9287 — Al-Barāʾ reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us to Yemen with Khālid ibn al-Walīd. He later sent ʿAlī to replace him and said:
“Tell Khālid’s companions: Whoever wishes to remain and continue the campaign with you may do so, and whoever wishes to return may return.”
I was among those who remained with ʿAlī, and I acquired several ounces of silver as spoils.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4349).
This occurred after the Prophet ﷺ returned from al-Ṭāʾif and distributed the spoils at al-Jiʿrānah. Al-Bukhārī stated that it occurred before the Farewell Pilgrimage.
“To remain and continue with you” means to return to Yemen. Al-Taʿqīb means that part of an army returns after initially withdrawing in order to participate in another campaign. Al-Khaṭṭābī explained it this way, as cited in Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/66).

9288 — Saʿīd ibn Abī Burdah reported from his father that the Prophet ﷺ sent his grandfather Abū Mūsā and Muʿādh to Yemen and instructed them:
“Make matters easy and do not make them difficult. Give glad tidings and do not drive people away. Cooperate with one another.”
Abū Mūsā said, “O Prophet of Allah, in our land there is a drink made from barley called mizr and a drink made from honey called bitʿ.”
He replied: “Every intoxicant is forbidden.”
They departed, and Muʿādh asked Abū Mūsā, “How do you recite the Qur’an?”
He replied, “Standing, sitting, and while riding my mount. I recite it in portions at different times.”
Muʿādh said, “As for me, I sleep and I stand in prayer. I seek reward for my sleep just as I seek reward for standing in prayer.”
Each set up a tent, and they would visit one another.
Muʿādh visited Abū Mūsā and found a man bound there.
He asked, “What is this?”
Abū Mūsā replied, “He is a Jewish man who embraced Islam and then renounced it.”
Muʿādh said, “I shall certainly strike off his head.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4344) and Muslim (1733:15).

9289 — Abū Burdah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Abū Mūsā and Muʿādh ibn Jabal to Yemen, appointing each over a separate province, for Yemen was divided into two provinces.
He instructed them: “Make matters easy and do not make them difficult. Give glad tidings and do not drive people away.”
Each of them then proceeded to his assigned territory.
Whenever either of them traveled through his own territory and came near his companion, he would renew his contact with him and greet him.
Muʿādh was traveling through his territory near his companion Abū Mūsā. He rode his mule until he reached him.
Abū Mūsā was sitting with the people gathered around him, and a man was present whose hands had been bound to his neck.
Muʿādh asked him, “ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays, what is the matter with this man?”
He replied, “This is a man who disbelieved after embracing Islam.”
Muʿādh said, “I shall not dismount until he is executed.”
Abū Mūsā replied, “He was brought here for that very purpose, so dismount.”
Muʿādh said, “I shall not dismount until he is executed.”
Abū Mūsā ordered that the man be executed, and Muʿādh then dismounted.
He asked, “ʿAbdullāh, how do you recite the Qur’an?”
Abū Mūsā replied, “I recite it in portions at different times.”
He asked, “How do you recite it, Muʿādh?”
Muʿādh replied, “I sleep during the first part of the night and then rise after receiving my allotted share of sleep. I recite whatever Allah has decreed for me, and I seek reward for my sleep just as I seek reward for standing in prayer.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4341, 4342).
“Province” means a district. Muʿādh was appointed over the upper region toward Aden, while Abū Mūsā was appointed over the lower region.
“I recite it in portions” means: “I maintain its recitation throughout the night and day, one portion after another.”
“He renewed his contact with him” means that he renewed their relationship by visiting him.

9290 — Abū Mūsā reported that the Prophet ﷺ sent him and Muʿādh to Yemen and instructed them:
“Make matters easy and do not make them difficult. Give glad tidings and do not drive people away. Cooperate with one another and do not disagree.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3038) and Muslim (1733:7).

9291 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī reported: The Prophet ﷺ sent Muʿādh ibn Jabal and me to Yemen.
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, a drink called mizr is made from barley in our land, and another drink called bitʿ is made from honey.”
He replied: “Every intoxicant is forbidden.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4343) and Muslim (1733:70).

9292 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Muʿādh ibn Jabal to Yemen, he told him:
“You will come to a people who possess Scripture. When you reach them, invite them to testify that there is no deity except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah.
“If they obey you in that, inform them that Allah has obligated them to perform five prayers during every day and night.
“If they obey you in that, inform them that Allah has obligated them to give charity that is taken from their wealthy and returned to their poor.
“If they obey you in that, beware of taking the most valuable portions of their wealth. Fear the supplication of the oppressed, for there is no barrier between it and Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4347) and Muslim (19).

9293 — Muʿādh reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent him to Yemen, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out with him to advise him. Muʿādh was mounted, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ walked beside and beneath his riding animal.
When he had finished advising him, he said:
“Muʿādh, perhaps you will not meet me again after this year. Perhaps you will pass by this mosque of mine and my grave.”
Muʿādh wept intensely at the thought of separation from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ then turned his face toward Madinah and said:
“The people closest to me are those who fear Allah, whoever they may be and wherever they may be.”
In another narration, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not weep, Muʿādh, for this kind of weeping is from Satan.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22052, 22054), al-Ṭabarānī (20/121), and Ibn Ḥibbān (647).
Ibn Ḥibbān added:
“O Allah, I do not permit them to corrupt what I have set right. By Allah, members of my nation will surely be overturned from their religion just as a vessel is overturned upon the open ground.”

9294 — Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Bajalī reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“Will you not relieve me of Dhū al-Khalaṣah?”
I replied, “Certainly.”
I set out with one hundred and fifty horsemen from Aḥmas, who were skilled cavalrymen. I, however, could not remain firmly seated upon a horse.
I mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ. He struck my chest with his hand until I could see the mark of his hand upon my chest, and he prayed:
“O Allah, make him firm and make him one who guides others and is himself rightly guided.”
After that, I never fell from a horse.
Dhū al-Khalaṣah was a temple in Yemen belonging to Khathʿam and Bajīlah. It contained idols that were worshipped and was called “the Kaʿbah.”
Jarīr reached it, burned it with fire, and destroyed it.
When Jarīr reached Yemen, there was a man there who sought decisions through divining arrows.
The man was told, “The messenger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is here. If he captures you, he will strike off your head.”
While the man was using the arrows, Jarīr came upon him and said, “You must break them and testify that there is no deity except Allah, or I shall strike off your head.”
The man broke them and pronounced the testimony of faith.
Jarīr then sent a man from Aḥmas, known as Abū Arṭāh, to bring the Prophet ﷺ the glad tidings.
When he reached the Prophet ﷺ, he said, “O Messenger of Allah, by the One who sent you with the truth, I did not come until I had left it looking like a mangy camel.”
The Prophet ﷺ invoked blessings five times upon the cavalry and men of Aḥmas.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4357) and Muslim (2476:137).

9295 — Jarīr reported: During the pre-Islamic period, there was a temple called Dhū al-Khalaṣah. It was also known as the Yemeni Kaʿbah and the Syrian Kaʿbah.
The Prophet ﷺ said to me: “Will you not relieve me of Dhū al-Khalaṣah?”
I set out with one hundred and fifty riders. We destroyed it and killed those whom we found stationed there.
I returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him, and he supplicated for us and for Aḥmas.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4355) and Muslim (2476:136).

9296 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in Madinah for nine years without performing ḥajj. Then, in the tenth year, an announcement was made among the people that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would perform ḥajj.
A great multitude came to Madinah, all seeking to follow the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and perform the rites as he performed them.
The narrator then related the hadith in full.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).
It was called the Farewell Pilgrimage because the Prophet ﷺ bade farewell to the people during it and told them:
“Learn your pilgrimage rites from me, for perhaps I will not meet you after this year.”
During the Farewell Pilgrimage, Allah perfected the religion He had chosen for humanity. Allah Most High said:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My favor upon you, and chosen Islam as your religion.” [Al-Māʾidah 5:3]

9297 — Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb reported that some Jewish people said, “Had this verse been revealed to us, we would have taken the day of its revelation as a festival.”
ʿUmar asked, “Which verse?”
They replied:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you and completed My favor upon you.” [Al-Māʾidah 5:3]
ʿUmar said, “I know precisely where it was revealed. It was revealed while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was standing at ʿArafah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4407) and Muslim (3017). This wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9298 — Zayd ibn Arqam reported: The Prophet ﷺ participated in nineteen military campaigns. After migrating, he performed only one ḥajj and performed no other pilgrimage afterward: the Farewell Pilgrimage.
Abū Isḥāq added: “He also performed another pilgrimage while in Makkah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4404), and Muslim in The Book of Ḥajj (1254:218) and The Book of Jihād and Expeditions (1254:144).
Regarding the words “He also performed another pilgrimage while in Makkah,” al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said: This may give the impression that he performed only one ḥajj before the Hijrah, but that is not the case. Rather, he performed ḥajj several times before migrating.

9299 — Abū Hurayrah reported that after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed ḥajj with his wives, he said:
“This pilgrimage is sufficient; afterward, remain upon the backs of the mats”—meaning: remain in your homes.
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (9765), Abū Yaʿlā (7158), al-Ṭayālisī (1752), and al-Bayhaqī (5/228).
Al-Bayhaqī explained the meaning of the hadith: “The fact that ʿĀʾishah and other Mothers of the Believers performed ḥajj after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ indicates that this report means that ḥajj was obligatory upon them only once, just as it was made obligatory upon men once. It does not prohibit them from performing additional pilgrimages.”
The word al-ḥuṣur, with a ḍammah on the ḥāʾ and a silent ṣād in its shortened pronunciation, is the plural of ḥaṣīr, meaning a mat spread inside a home.
The hadith indicates that they should generally remain at home and leave voluntary pilgrimage after Allah had enabled them to perform ḥajj with the Prophet ﷺ. It does not completely prohibit voluntary ḥajj after the obligatory pilgrimage. It is authentically established that the Prophet’s ﷺ wives performed ḥajj after his death.

9300 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported in the account of the Farewell Pilgrimage: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued until he reached ʿArafah, where he found that a tent had been erected for him at Namirah, and he stopped there.
When the sun passed its zenith, he ordered that al-Qaṣwāʾ be saddled. He rode to the bottom of the valley and addressed the people:
“Your blood and wealth are sacred to one another, just as this day of yours is sacred, in this month of yours, in this city of yours.
“Behold! Everything belonging to the pre-Islamic period is abolished beneath my feet. The blood claims of the pre-Islamic period are abolished.
“The first claim of blood from among our claims that I abolish is that of the son of Rabīʿah ibn al-Ḥārith. He was being nursed among Banū Saʿd when Hudhayl killed him.
“The usury of the pre-Islamic period is abolished. The first of our usury that I abolish is the usury of al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. All of it is abolished.
“Fear Allah concerning women. You have taken them under Allah’s protection and made marital relations with them lawful by the word of Allah.
“Your right over them is that they must not allow anyone you dislike upon your bedding. If they do so, discipline them in a manner that causes no injury.
“Their right over you is that you provide their food and clothing according to what is reasonable.
“I have left among you that which, if you hold firmly to it, will prevent you from ever going astray: the Book of Allah.
“You will be questioned concerning me, so what will you say?”
They replied, “We testify that you conveyed the message, fulfilled your trust, and gave sincere counsel.”
He raised his index finger toward the sky and then pointed it toward the people, saying three times:
“O Allah, bear witness! O Allah, bear witness! O Allah, bear witness!”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

9301 — Ibn Abī Bakrah reported from his father that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Time has returned to its original order as it was on the day Allah created the heavens and the earth. The year consists of twelve months, four of which are sacred. Three are consecutive: Dhū al-Qaʿdah, Dhū al-Ḥijjah, and Muḥarram; and the fourth is Rajab of Muḍar, which falls between Jumādā and Shaʿbān.
“Which month is this?”
We replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He remained silent until we thought he might give it a different name.
He asked: “Is it not Dhū al-Ḥijjah?”
We replied, “Indeed.”
He asked: “Which city is this?”
We replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He remained silent until we thought he might give it a different name.
He asked: “Is it not the Sacred City?”
We replied, “Indeed.”
He asked: “Which day is this?”
We replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He remained silent until we thought he might give it a different name.
He asked: “Is it not the Day of Sacrifice?”
We replied, “Indeed.”
Then he said:
“Your blood and wealth”—Muḥammad said: I believe he also said, “and your honor”—“are sacred to one another, just as this day of yours is sacred, in this city of yours, in this month of yours.
“You will meet your Lord, and He will question you concerning your deeds.
“Beware! Do not return after me to misguidance, striking one another’s necks.
“Let those who are present convey this to those who are absent. Perhaps someone to whom it is conveyed will understand it better than someone who heard it directly.”
Whenever Muḥammad, the sub-narrator, related this hadith, he would say, “Muḥammad ﷺ spoke the truth.”
The Prophet ﷺ then asked twice: “Have I conveyed the message?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4406) and Muslim (1679:29).

9302 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Prophet ﷺ arrived in Makkah, circumambulated the Kaʿbah, and performed saʿy between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah. After that circumambulation, he did not approach the Kaʿbah again until he returned from ʿArafah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1625).

9303 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from Madinah to Makkah, and he continued performing each four-rakʿah prayer as two rakʿahs until he returned.
The narrator asked, “How long did he remain in Makkah?”
Anas replied, “Ten days.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4297) and Muslim in The Book of the Prayer of Travelers (693:15).
This hadith of Anas refers to the Farewell Pilgrimage, because the Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah on the fourth and departed on the fourteenth. Ibn Ḥajar stated this in Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/21).

9304 — Yazīd ibn Ḥayyān reported: Ḥuṣayn ibn Sabrah, ʿUmar ibn Muslim, and I went to Zayd ibn Arqam.
When we sat with him, Ḥuṣayn said, “Zayd, you have encountered abundant goodness. You saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, heard his words, participated in campaigns with him, and prayed behind him. You have indeed encountered abundant goodness. Zayd, tell us what you heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He replied, “Son of my brother, by Allah, I have grown old, much time has passed, and I have forgotten some of what I once retained from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Accept whatever I relate to you, and do not burden me regarding what I do not relate.”
He then said: One day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood to address us at a watering place called Khumm, between Makkah and Madinah. He praised and extolled Allah, admonished us, and reminded us.
He then said:
“To proceed: O people, I am only a human being. The messenger of my Lord—the Angel of Death—may soon come to me, and I shall answer his summons.
“I am leaving among you two weighty trusts. The first is the Book of Allah, containing guidance and light. Hold firmly to the Book of Allah and adhere to it.”
He strongly encouraged and urged adherence to the Book of Allah.
Then he said:
“And the members of my household. I remind you of Allah concerning the members of my household. I remind you of Allah concerning the members of my household. I remind you of Allah concerning the members of my household.”
Ḥuṣayn asked him, “Who are the members of his household, Zayd? Are his wives not members of his household?”
He replied, “His wives are members of his household. However, the members of his household intended here are those for whom obligatory charity remained forbidden after him.”
He asked, “Who are they?”
Zayd replied, “The family of ʿAlī, the family of ʿAqīl, the family of Jaʿfar, and the family of al-ʿAbbās.”
He asked, “Was obligatory charity forbidden to all of them?”
He replied, “Yes.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2408).

9305 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed Usāmah as commander over a group of people, but they criticized his appointment. He said:
“If you criticize his command, you previously criticized his father’s command. By Allah, his father was certainly worthy of command and was among the people dearest to me. This son of his is also among the people dearest to me after him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4250) and Muslim (2426).

9306 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: I saw the Prophet ﷺ casting the pebbles from atop his riding animal on the Day of Sacrifice, while saying:
“Learn your pilgrimage rites from me, for I do not know; perhaps I will not perform ḥajj after this pilgrimage of mine.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1297).

9307 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported in his description of the Prophet’s ﷺ pilgrimage that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have left among you that which, if you hold firmly to it, will prevent you from going astray: the Book of Allah.
“You will be questioned concerning me, so what will you say?”
They replied, “We testify that you conveyed the message, fulfilled your trust, and gave sincere counsel.”
He raised his index finger toward the sky and then pointed it toward the people, saying three times:
“O Allah, bear witness! O Allah, bear witness! O Allah, bear witness!”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1218).

9308 — Saʿīd ibn Jubayr reported that Ibn ʿAbbās said: “Thursday—and what a Thursday it was!” He then wept until his tears moistened the pebbles.
I asked, “Ibn ʿAbbās, what happened on Thursday?”
He replied: The Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ illness became severe, and he said:
“Bring me writing materials so that I may write for you a document after which you will not go astray.”
The people disagreed, although disagreement is not appropriate in the presence of a prophet.
They said, “What is the matter with him? Is he speaking incoherently? Ask him to clarify.”
He said: “Leave me, for the state in which I am is better. I instruct you regarding three matters: Expel the polytheists from the Arabian Peninsula, and honor visiting delegations as I used to honor them.”
Ibn ʿAbbās remained silent concerning the third instruction, or he said, “He mentioned it, but I forgot it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4431) and Muslim (1637:20).

9309 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was near death, several men were present in the house, including ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Come, let me write for you a document after which you will not go astray.”
ʿUmar said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has been overcome by pain. You possess the Qur’an; the Book of Allah is sufficient for us.”
The people in the house disagreed and disputed. Some said, “Bring the writing materials so that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ may write for you a document after which you will not go astray,” while others supported what ʿUmar had said.
When their argument and disagreement became excessive in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he said:
“Leave.”
ʿUbaydullāh said: Ibn ʿAbbās used to say, “The complete calamity was that their disagreement and clamor prevented the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from writing that document for them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4432) and Muslim in The Book of Bequests (1637:22).

9310 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: As the Prophet ﷺ approached death, he called for a sheet upon which he could write a document after which the people would not go astray.
Jābir said: ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb opposed the request, and it was consequently left aside.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (14726).

9311 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: ʿUmar used to admit me into the company of the elders who had participated in Badr. Some of them asked, “Why do you admit this young man among us when we have sons of his age?”
ʿUmar replied, “He is one whose knowledge and status you already know.”
One day, ʿUmar summoned them and also summoned me. I believed he called me that day only to demonstrate my knowledge to them.
He asked, “What do you say concerning Allah’s words:
‘When Allah’s help and the victory come, and you see the people entering Allah’s religion in multitudes…’
until the end of the sūrah?”
Some replied, “We were commanded to praise Allah and seek His forgiveness when He grants us help and victory.”
Others said, “We do not know,” while some said nothing.
ʿUmar asked me, “Ibn ʿAbbās, do you say the same?”
I replied, “No.”
He asked, “What do you say?”
I replied, “It refers to the appointed time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, which Allah made known to him:
‘When Allah’s help and the victory come’—meaning the Conquest of Makkah—that is the sign that your appointed time is near.
‘Then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever accepting of repentance.’”
ʿUmar said, “I understand nothing from it beyond what you have said.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4294).

9312 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb used to bring Ibn ʿAbbās close to him.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf said, “We have sons of the same age as he.”
ʿUmar replied, “He possesses the knowledge and status you know.”
ʿUmar then asked Ibn ʿAbbās about the verse:
“When Allah’s help and the victory come.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied, “It referred to the appointed time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, which Allah made known to him.”
ʿUmar said, “I understand nothing from it beyond what you understand.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4430).

9313 — Muʿādh ibn Jabal reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent him to Yemen, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out with him to advise him. Muʿādh was mounted, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ walked beside his riding animal.
When he had finished advising him, he said:
“Muʿādh, perhaps you will not meet me again after this year. Perhaps you will pass by this mosque of mine or my grave.”
Muʿādh wept intensely at the thought of separation from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ then turned his face toward Madinah and said:
“The people closest to me are those who fear Allah, whoever they may be and wherever they may be.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22052, 22054) through Abū al-Mughīrah—ʿAbd al-Quddūs ibn al-Ḥajjāj al-Khawlānī—from Ṣafwān ibn ʿAmr, from Rāshid ibn Saʿd, from ʿĀṣim ibn Ḥumayd al-Sakūnī, from Muʿādh ibn Jabal.
“Intensely” means that he was overcome by distress at separation from one he loved.

9314 — ʿĀʾishah reported: During the illness in which the Prophet ﷺ died, he called Fāṭimah, may Allah be pleased with her, and whispered something to her, causing her to weep.
He then called her again and whispered something else to her, causing her to laugh.
We later asked her about this. She said, “The Prophet ﷺ privately informed me that he would die during the illness from which he was suffering, so I wept. He then privately informed me that I would be the first member of his family to follow him in death, so I laughed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Military Campaigns (4433, 4434) and Muslim in The Book of the Virtues of the Companions (2450:97).

9315 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: When the following verse was revealed:
“When Allah’s help and the victory come,”
the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called Fāṭimah and said:
“My impending death has been announced to me.”
She wept, so he said:
“Do not weep, for you will be the first member of my family to join me.”
She then laughed.
Some of the Prophet’s ﷺ wives saw her and asked, “Fāṭimah, we saw you weep and then laugh.”
She replied, “He informed me that his impending death had been announced to him, so I wept. He then told me:
‘Do not weep, for you will be the first member of my family to join me.’
So I laughed.”
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī (80), al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/329), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (7/167).

9316 — ʿĀʾishah reported: I used to hear that no prophet dies until he is given a choice between this world and the Hereafter.
During the illness in which the Prophet ﷺ died, I heard him say in a hoarse voice:
“With those upon whom Allah has bestowed His favor—the prophets, the steadfast affirmers of truth, the martyrs, and the righteous. What excellent companions they are!” [Al-Nisāʾ 4:69]
I understood that he had been given the choice at that moment.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4435) and Muslim (2443:86).

9317 — ʿĀʾishah reported: While the Prophet ﷺ was healthy, he used to say:
“No prophet is taken in death until he is shown his place in Paradise and then given a choice.”
When death approached him, his head was resting upon my thigh, and he lost consciousness.
He then regained consciousness, fixed his gaze upon the ceiling of the house, and said:
“O Allah, the Highest Companion.”
I said to myself, “Then he will not choose to remain with us.”
I recognized that this was the fulfillment of the hadith he had related to us while healthy.
She said: His final words were:
“O Allah, the Highest Companion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4463) and Muslim (2444:87).

9318 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat upon the pulpit and said:
“Allah gave one of His servants a choice between receiving whatever splendor of this world he desired and receiving what is with Allah. The servant chose what is with Allah.”
Abū Bakr wept and said, “May our fathers and mothers be sacrificed for you.”
We were surprised by him, and the people said, “Look at this elderly man! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ is speaking about a servant whom Allah gave a choice between the splendor of this world and what is with Him, yet he says, ‘May our fathers and mothers be sacrificed for you!’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ himself was the servant who had been given the choice, and Abū Bakr understood him better than any of us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also said:
“Among all people, Abū Bakr has shown me the greatest generosity through his companionship and wealth. If I were to take an intimate friend from my nation, I would take Abū Bakr, but the brotherhood and affection of Islam are sufficient. Every small doorway opening into the mosque must be closed except the doorway of Abū Bakr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3904) and Muslim (2382).

9319 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported: During the illness in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, he came out to us while we were in the mosque. His head was wrapped with a cloth. He proceeded toward the pulpit, ascended it, and we followed him.
He said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, I can see the Pool from this very place where I stand.”
He then said:
“One of Allah’s servants was offered this world and its adornments, but he chose the Hereafter.”
No one understood his meaning except Abū Bakr. His eyes overflowed and he wept, saying, “Rather, may our fathers, mothers, lives, and wealth be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ then descended from the pulpit and never stood upon it again until the present time.
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī (78) and Ibn Ḥibbān (6593).

9320 — ʿĀʾishah reported: I never saw anyone suffer more severe pain than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5646) and Muslim (2570:44).

9321 — ʿĀʾishah reported that during the illness in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, he repeatedly asked:
“Where will I be tomorrow? Where will I be tomorrow?”
He was awaiting ʿĀʾishah’s allotted day.
His wives gave him permission to remain wherever he wished, so he stayed in ʿĀʾishah’s house until he died there.
ʿĀʾishah said: He died on the day that was allotted to me, inside my house. Allah took his soul while his head rested between my upper chest and neck, and his saliva mixed with mine.
She continued: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr entered carrying a tooth-stick with which he was cleaning his teeth. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at it.
I said, “ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, give me that tooth-stick.”
He gave it to me. I cut off its end, softened it by chewing it, and then gave it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He cleaned his teeth with it while leaning against my chest.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4450) and Muslim (2443:84).

9322 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became gravely ill and his pain intensified, he sought permission from his wives to be nursed in my house, and they permitted him.
He came out supported between two men, with his feet dragging along the ground. One was al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, and the other was another man.
ʿUbaydullāh said: I related what ʿĀʾishah had said to ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās. He asked me, “Do you know who the other man was whom ʿĀʾishah did not name?”
I replied, “No.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said, “It was ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.”
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, also reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered her house and his pain intensified, he said:
“Pour water over me from seven water skins whose ties have not been opened, so that I may go out and give final instructions to the people.”
We seated him in a washing vessel belonging to Ḥafṣah, the Prophet’s ﷺ wife, and began pouring water over him from those skins until he gestured with his hand that we had poured enough.
She said: He then went out to the people, led them in prayer, and addressed them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4442) and Muslim (418:92).

9323 — ʿĀʾishah reported: During the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ illness, we poured medicine into the side of his mouth. He gestured to us not to do so.
We thought this was merely a sick person’s dislike of medicine.
When he regained consciousness, he said:
“Everyone present must have the medicine administered to them in the same manner, except al-ʿAbbās, because he was not present with you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4458) and Muslim (2213:85).
“We administered medicine through the side of his mouth” means that they placed medicine into the side of his mouth without his consent.

9324 — Asmāʾ bint ʿUmays reported: The Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ illness first began while he was in Maymūnah’s house. His illness became so severe that he lost consciousness.
His wives consulted one another about administering medicine through the side of his mouth, and they did so.
When he regained consciousness, he said:
“This is the practice of women who came from that land,” pointing toward Abyssinia. Asmāʾ bint ʿUmays had been among those who lived there.
They said, “O Messenger of Allah, we feared that you were suffering from pleurisy.”
He replied:
“That is a disease with which Allah would not afflict me. No one in the house must remain without having the medicine administered in the same way, except the uncle of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ”—meaning al-ʿAbbās.
Maymūnah had the medicine administered to her that day even though she was fasting, in fulfillment of the firm command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (9754; 5/428–429). Al-Ḥāfiẓ also authenticated it in Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/148).

9325 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib came out from the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the illness in which he died.
The people asked, “Abū al-Ḥasan, how is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ this morning?”
He replied, “By Allah’s praise, he is recovering.”
Al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib took ʿAlī by the hand and said, “By Allah, within three days you will become subject to another’s authority. By Allah, I believe that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ will die from this illness. I recognize the appearance of the descendants of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib when death approaches.”
“Come with me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that we may ask him to whom this authority will belong.
“If it belongs to us, we shall know that. If it belongs to someone else, we shall know that as well, and he can instruct his successor to treat us well.”
ʿAlī replied, “By Allah, if we ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for it and he refuses us, the people will never give it to us afterward.
“By Allah, I shall not ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4447).
Regarding the words, “Within three days you will become subject to another’s authority,” al-Ḥāfiẓ said: This describes someone who becomes subject to another. The meaning is that the Prophet ﷺ would die after three days and ʿAlī would then be placed under another person’s authority. Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/143).

9326 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported: I came to the Prophet ﷺ during his illness while he was suffering from an extremely severe fever.
I said, “You are suffering from an extremely severe fever.”
I asked, “Is that because you receive twice the reward?”
He replied: “Yes. No Muslim is afflicted by harm without Allah causing his sins to fall away from him just as leaves fall from a tree.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5647) and Muslim (2571). This wording is al-Bukhārī’s.
Another wording narrated by both states: “Yes, I suffer fever as severely as two men among you.”

9327 — One of the Prophet’s ﷺ wives reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill, and his suffering or pain grew extremely severe.
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, you are showing intense distress from the pain. Had one of us women behaved like this, you would have been surprised by her!”
He replied:
“Do you not know that the believer’s suffering is intensified so that it may serve as expiation for his sins?”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd (2/207).

9328 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill, he would recite the protective sūrahs, lightly blow over himself, and wipe his body with his hand.
During the illness in which he died, I began reciting over him the protective sūrahs that he used to recite and lightly blow, while wiping his body with the Prophet’s ﷺ own hand.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (10), al-Bukhārī (4439), and Muslim (2192:51).

9329 — Umm al-Faḍl bint al-Ḥārith reported: I heard the Prophet ﷺ recite Sūrat al-Mursalāt during the sunset prayer. He did not lead us in another prayer after that before Allah took his soul.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (26), al-Bukhārī (4429), and Muslim (462:173).
Umm al-Faḍl was the mother of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās. Her name was Lubābah bint al-Ḥārith.
The following chapter contains ʿĀʾishah’s report that the Prophet ﷺ instructed Abū Bakr to lead the people in the night prayer. Abū Bakr led them in prayer during those days. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ later felt some relief and came out for the noon prayer while Abū Bakr was leading the people.

9330 — ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah reported: I entered upon ʿĀʾishah and asked, “Will you not tell me about the illness of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
She replied: Yes. The Prophet ﷺ became gravely ill and asked:
“Have the people prayed?”
We replied, “No. They are waiting for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said: “Place water for me in the washing vessel.”
We did so, and he bathed. He then attempted to rise but lost consciousness.
When he regained consciousness, he asked: “Have the people prayed?”
We replied, “No. They are waiting for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said: “Place water for me in the washing vessel.”
We did so, and he bathed. He then attempted to rise but lost consciousness.
When he regained consciousness, he asked: “Have the people prayed?”
We replied, “No. They are waiting for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said: “Place water for me in the washing vessel.”
We did so, and he bathed. He then attempted to rise but lost consciousness.
When he regained consciousness, he asked: “Have the people prayed?”
We replied, “No. They are waiting for you, O Messenger of Allah.”
She said: The people remained gathered in the mosque, waiting for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to lead the final night prayer.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent word to Abū Bakr instructing him to lead the people in prayer.
The messenger came to Abū Bakr and said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commands you to lead the people in prayer.”
Abū Bakr, who was tenderhearted, said, “ʿUmar, lead the people in prayer.”
ʿUmar replied, “You have a greater right to do so.”
ʿĀʾishah said: Abū Bakr therefore led them in prayer during those days.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ later felt some relief and came out supported between two men, one of whom was al-ʿAbbās, for the noon prayer while Abū Bakr was leading the people.
When Abū Bakr saw him, he began to step backward, but the Prophet ﷺ gestured to him not to retreat.
He said to the two men: “Seat me beside him.”
They seated him beside Abū Bakr.
Abū Bakr remained standing and followed the Prophet’s ﷺ prayer, while the people followed Abū Bakr’s movements, and the Prophet ﷺ prayed seated.
ʿUbaydullāh said: I entered upon ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās and asked, “Shall I relate to you what ʿĀʾishah told me about the illness of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He replied, “Proceed.”
I related her report to him, and he objected to none of it except that he asked, “Did she name the man who was with al-ʿAbbās?”
I replied, “No.”
He said, “It was ʿAlī.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (687) and Muslim (418:90).

9331 — ʿĀʾishah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”
ʿĀʾishah said, “O Messenger of Allah, when Abū Bakr stands in your place, the people will be unable to hear him because of his weeping. Instruct ʿUmar to lead the people.”
He repeated: “Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”
ʿĀʾishah said: I told Ḥafṣah, “When Abū Bakr stands in his place, the people will be unable to hear him because of his weeping. Ask the Prophet ﷺ to instruct ʿUmar to lead them.”
Ḥafṣah did so.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You women are indeed like the women associated with Joseph! Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”
Ḥafṣah said to ʿĀʾishah, “I have never received any good from following your suggestion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (89), al-Bukhārī (7303), and Muslim (418:94–95).

9332 — ʿĀʾishah reported: I repeatedly appealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning that matter. The only reason I persisted was that I could not imagine the people ever loving a man who stood in his place after him.
I believed that anyone who stood in his place would be regarded by the people as an ill omen, so I wanted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to divert that responsibility away from Abū Bakr.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4445) and Muslim (418:93).

9333 — Abū Mūsā reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill, and his illness intensified. He said:
“Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer.”
ʿĀʾishah said, “O Messenger of Allah, Abū Bakr is a tenderhearted man. If he stands in your place, he will be unable to lead the people in prayer.”
He replied:
“Tell Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer. You women are indeed like the women associated with Joseph.”
Abū Bakr therefore led the people in prayer during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (678) and Muslim (420).

9334 — Anas ibn Mālik al-Anṣārī—who followed, served, and accompanied the Prophet ﷺ—reported that Abū Bakr had been leading the people in prayer during the illness from which the Prophet ﷺ died.
On Monday, while the people were standing in rows for prayer, the Prophet ﷺ lifted the curtain of his chamber and stood looking at us. His face appeared like a page of the Qur’an.
He then smiled broadly.
We nearly became distracted from our prayer because of our joy at seeing the Prophet ﷺ.
Abū Bakr stepped backward upon his heels to join the row, thinking that the Prophet ﷺ was coming out to lead the prayer.
The Prophet ﷺ gestured to us to complete our prayer and lowered the curtain. He died later that same day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (680) and Muslim (419:98).
“His face appeared like a page of the Qur’an” describes his extraordinary beauty, fine complexion, purity of face, and radiance.

9335 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: “Thursday—and what a Thursday it was!” He then wept until his tears stained the pebbles.
He said: The Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ pain became severe on Thursday, and he said:
“Bring me writing materials so that I may write for you a document after which you will never go astray.”
The people disagreed, although disagreement is not appropriate in the presence of a prophet.
They said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ is speaking incoherently.”
He said:
“Leave me, for the state in which I am is better than that to which you are calling me.”
At the time of his death, he gave three instructions:
“Expel the polytheists from the Arabian Peninsula, and honor visiting delegations as I used to honor them.”
The narrator forgot the third instruction.
Yaʿqūb ibn Muḥammad said: I asked al-Mughīrah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān about the Arabian Peninsula. He replied, “Makkah, Madinah, al-Yamāmah, and Yemen.”
Yaʿqūb said: Al-ʿArj is the beginning of Tihāmah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3053) and Muslim (1637).
The third instruction was a warning against taking the graves of prophets as places of worship, as mentioned in the following hadiths:

9336 — Abū ʿUbaydah reported: Among the final words spoken by the Prophet ﷺ were:
“Expel the Jews of the Ḥijāz and the people of Najrān from the Arabian Peninsula. Know that the worst of people are those who took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (1691), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (439), Abū Yaʿlā (872), al-Dārimī (2501), al-Ḥumaydī (58), al-Bukhārī in al-Tārīkh al-Kabīr (4/57), and al-Bayhaqī (9/208), through routes from Ibrāhīm ibn Maymūn, from Saʿd ibn Samurah ibn Jundab, from his father, from Abū ʿUbaydah.
Scholars differed over the precise boundaries of the Arabian Peninsula. The correct view is that it is the land situated between the Indian Ocean, the Red Sea, the Arabian Gulf, and the Abyssinian Sea.
It is attributed to the Arabs because it was under their control and contained their homelands and settlements.
According to the majority of scholars, however, the specific territory in which polytheists may not reside is the Ḥijāz, particularly Makkah and Madinah.
According to al-Shāfiʿī, they may enter with the ruler’s permission when doing so serves the Muslims’ interests.
For further discussion, see Fatḥ al-Bārī (6/171).

9337 — The Mother of the Believers ʿĀʾishah reported that during the illness from which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not recover, he said:
“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians; they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”
She said: Were it not for this concern, his grave would have been made visible and exposed. However, it was feared that it would be taken as a place of worship.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4441) and Muslim (529:19).

9338 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: Abū Bakr and al-ʿAbbās passed by a gathering of the Anṣār who were weeping.
One of them asked, “What is making you weep?”
They replied, “We remembered the gatherings in which the Prophet ﷺ used to sit among us.”
He entered upon the Prophet ﷺ and informed him of this.
The Prophet ﷺ came out with a strip of cloth wrapped around his head.
He ascended the pulpit and never ascended it again after that day.
He praised and extolled Allah, then said:
“I instruct you to care for the Anṣār, for they are my close community and trusted confidants. They have fulfilled the obligations upon them, while the rights owed to them remain. Accept the good deeds of those among them who do well, and pardon those among them who do wrong.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3799) and Muslim (2510).
“The Anṣār are my karsh and my ʿaybah” means: “They are my close community and special companions.”
Al-Khaṭṭābī said in Aʿlām al-Ḥadīth (3/1144): “My karsh and my ʿaybah” means: “They are my intimate associates and special confidants.” He used the stomach as a metaphor because it is where an animal’s nourishment, upon which its survival depends, is stored. Karsh may also refer to a man’s dependents and family. The Arabs say, “So-and-so has a scattered karsh,” meaning that he has many dependents.
An ʿaybah is a container in which a person stores his finest and most carefully protected garments. He used it as a metaphor to indicate that the Anṣār were the repositories of his secrets and trust.

9339 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: During the illness in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, he lifted the curtain while his head was wrapped and said three times:
“O Allah, have I conveyed the message?”
He then said:
“Nothing remains of the glad tidings of prophethood except a righteous dream that a righteous servant sees or that is seen concerning him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (479:208).
Muslim also narrated it with the following addition:
“Beware! I have been forbidden from reciting the Qur’an while bowing or prostrating. While bowing, therefore, glorify the Lord; and while prostrating, strive earnestly in supplication, for it is most likely that your supplication will be answered.”
The word qamin means likely, deserving, and appropriate.

9340 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib reported: Among the final words of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were:
“Prayer, prayer! Fear Allah concerning those whom your right hands possess.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5156), Ibn Mājah (2698), Aḥmad (585), al-Bukhārī in Al-Adab al-Mufrad (50), and al-Bayhaqī (8/11).

9341 — Al-Aswad ibn Yazīd reported: It was mentioned in ʿĀʾishah’s presence that ʿAlī had been specially designated as the Prophet’s ﷺ executor and successor.
She asked, “When did he make such a bequest to him? I was supporting the Prophet ﷺ against my chest—or she said, in my lap—and he called for a basin. He then collapsed in my lap, and I did not initially realize that he had died. When, then, could he have made such a bequest to ʿAlī?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4459) and Muslim (1636:19).

9342 — Ṭalḥah ibn Muṣarrif reported: I asked ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā, “Did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ leave a bequest?”
He replied, “No.”
I asked, “Why, then, was making a bequest prescribed for the Muslims, or why were they commanded to make bequests?”
He replied, “He bequeathed adherence to the Book of Allah Almighty.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4460) and Muslim (1634:16).

9343 — ʿĀʾishah reported: The Prophet ﷺ died while resting between my chest and chin. After witnessing the Prophet’s ﷺ severe experience of death, I will never dislike the severity of death for anyone.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4446).

9344 — ʿĀʾishah used to say: Among Allah’s blessings upon me is that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died inside my house, on my allotted day, between my upper chest and neck, and that Allah joined my saliva with his at the time of his death.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān entered carrying a tooth-stick while I was supporting the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
I saw him looking at it and understood that he wanted the tooth-stick.
I asked, “Shall I take it for you?”
He nodded yes.
I took it, but it was too hard for him.
I asked, “Shall I soften it for you?”
He nodded yes.
I softened it for him.
A small leather vessel—or a wooden container, as ʿUmar, the narrator, was uncertain—containing water was before him.
He began placing his hands into the water, wiping his face with them, and saying:
“There is no deity except Allah. Death truly has agonies.”
He then raised his hand and continued saying:
“With the Highest Companion.”
This continued until his soul was taken and his hand fell.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4449).

9345 — ʿĀʾishah reported that shortly before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, while he was leaning against her chest, she listened closely and heard him saying:
“O Allah, forgive me, have mercy upon me, and join me with the Highest Companion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (46), al-Bukhārī (4440), and Muslim (2444:85).

9346 — ʿAlī reported: The final words of the Prophet ﷺ were:
“Prayer, prayer! Fear Allah concerning those whom your right hands possess.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5156), Ibn Mājah (2698), and Aḥmad (585).
The statements of ʿAlī and ʿĀʾishah may be reconciled by understanding ʿAlī’s report as referring to the Prophet’s ﷺ final words concerning legal rulings, while ʿĀʾishah’s report refers to his final words generally, as she was present at his death.
Their two reports also refute the Rāfiḍah who claim that the Prophet ﷺ designated ʿAlī as caliph before his death.

9347 — Jarīr reported: I was in Yemen when I met two Yemeni men, Dhū Kalāʿ and Dhū ʿAmr. I began telling them about the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Dhū ʿAmr said, “If what you are relating concerning your companion is true, then his appointed time must have passed three days ago.”
They accompanied me. While we were traveling, a caravan appeared before us coming from the direction of Madinah, so we questioned them.
They replied, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died, Abū Bakr has been appointed as his successor, and the people remain righteous.”
They said, “Inform your leader that we came, and perhaps we will return, if Allah wills.”
They then returned to Yemen.
I informed Abū Bakr about them, and he asked, “Why did you not bring them here?”
Later, Dhū ʿAmr said to me, “Jarīr, you hold an honored place with me, so I shall tell you something:
“You Arabs will continue to remain upon goodness so long as, whenever one ruler dies, you consult among yourselves and appoint another.
“But when authority is seized by the sword, the rulers will become kings who become angry as kings become angry and pleased as kings become pleased.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4359).
“If what you relate concerning your companion is true” means: If it is indeed true.
“You appoint a ruler” means that you consult one another and appoint a leader whom you choose from among yourselves.
“When it is determined by the sword” means when political authority is acquired through force and domination.

9348 — Abū al-Khayr reported that al-Ṣunābiḥī was asked, “When did you migrate?”
He replied: We left Yemen as emigrants and reached al-Juḥfah, where a rider approached us.
I asked him, “What is the news?”
He replied, “We buried the Prophet ﷺ five days ago.”
I asked, “Have you heard anything concerning the Night of Decree?”
He replied, “Yes. Bilāl, the Prophet’s ﷺ caller to prayer, informed me that it is sought during the seven remaining nights of the final ten nights.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4470).

9349 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: During the Prophet’s ﷺ lifetime, we were cautious about speaking freely and behaving casually with our wives, fearing that revelation might come down concerning us.
After the Prophet ﷺ died, we spoke and behaved more freely with them.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5187).

9350 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: On the day the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered Madinah, everything within it became illuminated.
On the day he died, everything within it became dark.
We had scarcely withdrawn our hands from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while burying him before we felt that our hearts had changed and were no longer as they had been.
Authentic: Narrated and authenticated by al-Tirmidhī (3618), and narrated by Ibn Mājah (1631) and Aḥmad (13830). Ibn Ḥibbān (662) also authenticated it.

9351 — Anas reported: When the Prophet ﷺ became gravely ill, the agonies of death began repeatedly overwhelming him.
Fāṭimah said, “Oh, the suffering of my father!”
He told her:
“Your father will suffer no distress after today.”
When he died, she said:
“O my father, he answered the Lord who summoned him!
 O my father, the Garden of Firdaws is his dwelling!
 O my father, to Gabriel we announce his death!”
After he was buried, Fāṭimah said, “Anas, how could your hearts allow you to cast earth over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4462).
A narration in Musnad Aḥmad (13117) reads: “Anas, how could your hearts allow you to bury the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the earth and then return?”

9352 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ experienced the agonies of death, Fāṭimah said, “Oh, the suffering of my father!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ told her:
“Your father will suffer no distress after today. That which spares no one has now come to your father; the final meeting will be on the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (1629), whose wording this is, al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (380), and Abū Yaʿlā (3441).

9353 — ʿĀʾishah used to say: Among Allah’s blessings upon me is that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died inside my house, on my allotted day, and between my upper chest and neck.
The remainder of the hadith follows.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4449).

9354 — ʿĀʾishah and Ibn ʿAbbās reported that Abū Bakr kissed the Prophet ﷺ after his death.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4455–4457).

9355 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Abū Bakr, may Allah be pleased with him, came riding a horse from his residence at al-Sunḥ. He dismounted and entered the mosque.
He did not speak to the people before entering ʿĀʾishah’s room. He went directly to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, whose body was covered with a striped Yemeni cloth.
He uncovered his face, bent over him, kissed him, and wept.
He then said, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! By Allah, Allah will not cause you to experience death twice. You have now experienced the death that was decreed for you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4452–4453).

9356 — Anas reported: On Monday, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ lifted the curtain of his chamber and saw Abū Bakr leading the people in prayer.
I looked at his face, which appeared like a page of the Qur’an, and he was smiling.
We nearly became distracted from our prayer because of our joy at seeing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Abū Bakr intended to step backward, but the Prophet ﷺ gestured to him, “Remain where you are.”
He then lowered the curtain and died later that same day.
ʿUmar stood and said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has not died. Rather, his Lord has summoned him just as He summoned Moses, who remained away from his people for forty nights.
“By Allah, I hope that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ will remain alive until he cuts off the hands and tongues of hypocritical men who claim—or he said, who say—that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (13028), ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1163), and ʿAbd al-Razzāq in his Muṣannaf (9754; 5/433).

9357 — Yazīd ibn Bābnūs reported: A companion and I went to ʿĀʾishah and sought permission to enter. She placed a cushion for us and drew the curtain around herself.
