My companion asked, “Mother of the Believers, what do you say concerning al-ʿirāk?”
She asked, “What is al-ʿirāk?” My companion struck me on the shoulder.
She said, “Stop! You have hurt your brother.”
She then asked, “What is al-ʿirāk? Do you mean menstruation? Use the expression Allah used: menstruation.”
She continued: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would embrace me and attend to my hair while a garment separated me from him and I was menstruating.
She then said: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed my door, he would sometimes say a brief word through which Allah Almighty would bring benefit.
One day, he passed without saying anything. He then passed again without saying anything. This occurred two or three times.
I said, “Servant girl, place a cushion for me beside the door.”
I wrapped my head.
When he passed me, he asked:
“ʿĀʾishah, what is the matter with you?”
I replied, “My head is aching.”
He said: “Rather, it is my head that aches!”
He left, but only a short time passed before he was brought back, carried in a cloak.
He entered my room and sent word to his other wives, saying:
“I have become ill and can no longer make the rounds among you. Give me permission to remain with ʿĀʾishah.”
I would help him perform ablution, though I had never done so for anyone before him.
One day, while his head rested upon my shoulder, it inclined toward my head.
I thought he wanted something from me.
A small drop of cold saliva came from his mouth and fell into the hollow at the base of my throat, causing my skin to shiver.
I thought he had lost consciousness, so I covered him with a garment.
ʿUmar and al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah arrived and sought permission to enter. I admitted them and drew the curtain around myself.
ʿUmar looked at him and said, “Oh, his unconsciousness! How severe is the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ loss of consciousness!”
They then rose to leave.
When they approached the door, al-Mughīrah said, “ʿUmar, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died.”
ʿUmar replied, “You have lied! You are a man overcome by tribulation. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ will not die until Allah Almighty destroys the hypocrites.”
Abū Bakr then arrived, and I raised the curtain.
He looked at the Prophet ﷺ and said, “Indeed, we belong to Allah, and to Him we shall return. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died.”
He approached him from the direction of his head, lowered his mouth, kissed his forehead, and said, “Oh, my Prophet!”
He raised his head, then lowered his mouth again, kissed his forehead, and said, “Oh, Allah’s chosen one!”
He raised his head once more, lowered his mouth and kissed him, saying, “Oh, my intimate friend! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died.”
He went out to the mosque, where ʿUmar was addressing the people and saying, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ will not die until Allah Almighty destroys the hypocrites.”
Abū Bakr began to speak. He praised and extolled Allah, then said:
“Allah Almighty says:
‘Indeed, you will die, and they too will die.’” [Al-Zumar 39:30]
He completed the verse.
He then recited:
“Muḥammad is only a messenger. Messengers have passed away before him. If he dies or is killed, will you turn back upon your heels?” [Āl ʿImrān 3:144]
He completed the verse and then said:
“Whoever worshipped Allah Almighty should know that Allah is living and never dies. Whoever worshipped Muḥammad should know that Muḥammad has died.”
ʿUmar said, “Is that truly in the Book of Allah? I had not realized that it was in the Book of Allah!”
ʿUmar then said, “O people, this is Abū Bakr, the venerable elder of the Muslims, so pledge allegiance to him.”
The people then pledged allegiance to him.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (25841), Abū Yaʿlā (4962), and al-Bayhaqī in Al-Dalāʾil (7/213–214).

9358 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported: Abū Bakr came out while ʿUmar was speaking to the people and said, “Sit down, ʿUmar.”
ʿUmar refused to sit, so the people turned toward Abū Bakr and left ʿUmar.
Abū Bakr said:
“To proceed: Whoever among you worshipped Muḥammad ﷺ should know that Muḥammad has died. Whoever among you worships Allah should know that Allah is living and never dies.
“Allah says:
‘Muḥammad is only a messenger. Messengers have passed away before him…’ through the end of the verse, ‘and Allah will reward the grateful.’” [Āl ʿImrān 3:144]
Ibn ʿAbbās said: By Allah, it was as though the people had not known that Allah had revealed this verse until Abū Bakr recited it.
All the people learned it from him, and I heard every person reciting it.
Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib informed me that ʿUmar said: “By Allah, as soon as I heard Abū Bakr recite it, my legs failed me and could no longer support me. I fell to the ground when I heard him recite it and realized that the Prophet ﷺ had indeed died.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4454).

9359 — ʿĀʾishah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three.
Ibn Shihāb said: Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib informed me of the same.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4466) and Muslim (2349:115).

9360 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three. Abū Bakr also died at the age of sixty-three, and ʿUmar died at the age of sixty-three.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2348:114).

9361 — Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died at the age of sixty-three, as did Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, and I am now sixty-three years old.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2351:120).

9362 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in Makkah for thirteen years and died at the age of sixty-three.
Another wording states: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in Makkah for thirteen years receiving revelation, and in Madinah for ten years. He died at the age of sixty-three.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3903) and Muslim (2351:117). The second wording was narrated by Muslim (2351:118).
Muslim also narrated (2353:121) from ʿAmmār, the freedman of Banū Hāshim, who said: I asked Ibn ʿAbbās, “How old was the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day he died?”
He replied, “I did not think that someone like you, from among his people, would be unaware of that.”
I said, “I have asked the people, but they gave me different answers, so I wanted to know your view.”
He asked, “Can you calculate?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He said, “Begin with forty, the age at which he was commissioned. Add fifteen years in Makkah during which he experienced safety and fear, and ten years after his migration to Madinah.”
It is likewise reported from ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in Makkah for fifteen years. For seven years he heard sounds and saw light without seeing any being, and for eight years revelation came to him. He then remained in Madinah for ten years.
The statement that “he remained in Makkah for fifteen years” is anomalous.
The correct report is: “He remained in Makkah for thirteen years and in Madinah for ten, and died at the age of sixty-three.” This agrees with the majority view.
The error may have originated with ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār, the freedman of Banū Hāshim.
After citing ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār’s report from Ibn ʿAbbās that the Prophet ﷺ died at the age of sixty-five, al-Bukhārī said in Al-Tārīkh al-Ṣaghīr (1/29): “He is not corroborated in this, and Shuʿbah used to criticize ʿAmmār.”
Ibn Ḥibbān included him in Al-Thiqāt (5/267) but said: “He used to make mistakes.”

9363 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died while Abū Bakr was at al-Sunḥ. Ismāʿīl explained that this was in the upper region of Madinah.
ʿUmar stood and said, “By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has not died.”
ʿĀʾishah said that ʿUmar later explained, “By Allah, nothing else occurred to my mind. I believed Allah would surely raise him so that he might cut off the hands and feet of certain men.”
Abū Bakr arrived, uncovered the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ face, kissed him, and said, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! You were pure in life and in death. By the One in whose hand is my soul, Allah will never make you experience death twice.”
He then went out and said, “O man who is swearing, remain calm.”
When Abū Bakr began to speak, ʿUmar sat down.
Abū Bakr praised and extolled Allah and said:
“Know that whoever worshipped Muḥammad should know that Muḥammad has died. Whoever worships Allah should know that Allah is living and never dies.”
He recited:
“Indeed, you will die, and they too will die.” [Al-Zumar 39:30]
He also recited:
“Muḥammad is only a messenger. Messengers have passed away before him. If he dies or is killed, will you turn back upon your heels? Whoever turns back upon his heels will never harm Allah in the least, and Allah will reward the grateful.” [Āl ʿImrān 3:144]
The people then began sobbing.
The Anṣār assembled around Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah in the hall of Banū Sāʿidah and said, “There should be one ruler from us and one ruler from you.”
Abū Bakr, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, and Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ went to them.
ʿUmar intended to speak, but Abū Bakr silenced him.
ʿUmar later said, “By Allah, I intended to speak only because I had prepared words that pleased me, and I feared that Abū Bakr might not express them as effectively.”
Abū Bakr then spoke, and he was the most eloquent of the people. Among his words were: “We shall be the rulers, and you shall be the ministers.”
Al-Ḥubāb ibn al-Mundhir said, “No, by Allah, we will not accept that. There must be one ruler from us and one ruler from you.”
Abū Bakr replied, “No. We shall be the rulers, and you shall be the ministers. Quraysh occupy the central position among the Arabs in homeland and possess the clearest lineage. Pledge allegiance to either ʿUmar or Abū ʿUbaydah.”
ʿUmar said, “Rather, we shall pledge allegiance to you. You are our leader, the best among us, and the one of us most beloved to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
ʿUmar took Abū Bakr’s hand and pledged allegiance to him, and the people also pledged allegiance.
Someone said, “You have killed Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.”
ʿUmar replied, “May Allah deal with Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Virtues (3667–3668), through Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAbdullāh, from Sulaymān ibn Bilāl, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, from ʿĀʾishah.

9364 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that he heard ʿUmar’s later address when he sat upon the pulpit on the day following the Prophet’s ﷺ death.
ʿUmar pronounced the testimony of faith while Abū Bakr remained silent.
He said: “I had hoped that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would live until he was the last of us to die.
“But if Muḥammad has died, Allah Most High has left among you a light by which you may be guided—the light through which Allah guided Muḥammad ﷺ.”
“Abū Bakr was the companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the second of the two in the cave. He therefore has the greatest right among the Muslims to manage your affairs. Rise and pledge allegiance to him.”
A group of them had already pledged allegiance to him in the hall of Banū Sāʿidah. The general public pledge then took place at the pulpit.
Al-Zuhrī reported from Anas ibn Mālik: I heard ʿUmar say to Abū Bakr that day, “Ascend the pulpit.”
He continued urging him until Abū Bakr ascended the pulpit, whereupon the people generally pledged allegiance to him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Judgments (7219), through Ibrāhīm ibn Mūsā, from Hishām, from Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9365 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: I used to teach the Qur’an to men from the Emigrants, including ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf.
While I was staying at his residence in Minā during the final pilgrimage performed by ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān returned to me and said:
“You should have seen the man who came to the Commander of the Believers today and said, ‘Commander of the Believers, what do you think of so-and-so? He says: “If ʿUmar dies, I shall pledge allegiance to so-and-so. By Allah, the pledge to Abū Bakr was merely an unexpected event, yet it was completed.”’”
ʿUmar became angry and said, “If Allah wills, I shall stand before the people this evening and warn them against those who wish to seize control of their affairs.”
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said: I told him, “Commander of the Believers, do not do that. The pilgrimage season gathers the common and disorderly people. They will be the majority surrounding you when you stand before the people.
“I fear that you will make a statement that everyone will carry away from you, without understanding it or placing it in its proper context.”
“Wait until you reach Madinah, for it is the home of migration and the Sunnah. There you can address the people of understanding and the respected leaders. You can deliver your statement with confidence, and the people of knowledge will understand it and place it in its proper context.”
ʿUmar replied, “By Allah, if Allah wills, I shall certainly address this matter in the first gathering at which I speak in Madinah.”
Ibn ʿAbbās continued: We reached Madinah near the end of Dhū al-Ḥijjah.
When Friday came, I went early after the sun had passed its zenith and found Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl sitting beside a corner of the pulpit.
I sat close to him until my knee touched his.
It was not long before ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came out.
When I saw him approaching, I told Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl, “This afternoon, he will make a statement he has not made since becoming caliph.”
Saʿīd rejected my suggestion and asked, “What could he possibly say that he has not said before?”
ʿUmar sat upon the pulpit.
When the callers to prayer had finished, he stood and praised Allah as He deserves to be praised.
He then said:
“To proceed: I am going to tell you something that it has been decreed for me to say. I do not know; perhaps I am saying it shortly before my death.
“Whoever understands and retains it may relate it wherever his mount carries him. Whoever fears that he has not understood it is not permitted to attribute falsehood to me.”
“Allah sent Muḥammad ﷺ with the truth and revealed the Book to him.
“Among what Allah revealed was the verse concerning stoning. We recited it, understood it, and retained it.
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ carried out stoning, and we carried it out after him.
“I fear that if a long time passes, someone will say, ‘By Allah, we do not find the verse of stoning in the Book of Allah,’ and people will go astray by abandoning an obligation Allah revealed.”
“Stoning is a true ruling in Allah’s Book for married men and women who commit adultery when evidence is established, pregnancy occurs, or a confession is made.
“Among what we used to recite from the Book of Allah was: ‘Do not deny your fathers, for denying your fathers is disbelief on your part’—or: ‘It is disbelief on your part to deny your fathers.’”
“Behold! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also said:
‘Do not praise me excessively as Jesus son of Mary was praised excessively. Rather, say: Allah’s servant and His Messenger.’”
“It has reached me that one of you says, ‘By Allah, when ʿUmar dies, I shall pledge allegiance to so-and-so.’
“No one should deceive himself by saying, ‘The pledge to Abū Bakr was merely an unexpected event, yet it was completed.’
“It was indeed sudden, but Allah protected the Muslims from its potential harm. None among you possesses the stature toward which people’s necks turn as they did toward Abū Bakr.”
“Whoever pledges allegiance to a man without consultation with the Muslims, neither he nor the person to whom he pledged should be followed, lest both expose themselves to being killed.”
“What occurred when Allah took the soul of His Prophet ﷺ was that the Anṣār separated themselves from us and assembled in their entirety at the hall of Banū Sāʿidah.
“ʿAlī, al-Zubayr, and those with them also remained apart from us.
“The Emigrants assembled around Abū Bakr.
“I said to Abū Bakr, ‘Abū Bakr, let us go to these brothers of ours from the Anṣār.’”
“We set out toward them.
“When we drew near, we met two righteous men from the Anṣār, who informed us of what the people had agreed upon.
“They asked, ‘Where are you going, Emigrants?’
“We replied, ‘We are going to these brothers of ours from the Anṣār.’
“They said, ‘There is no need for you to approach them. Settle your own matter.’
“I said, ‘By Allah, we shall certainly go to them.’”
“We continued until we reached them in the hall of Banū Sāʿidah.
“A man wrapped in garments was lying among them.
“I asked, ‘Who is this?’
“They replied, ‘This is Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.’
“I asked, ‘What is wrong with him?’
“They replied, ‘He has a fever.’”
“After we had been seated for a short while, their spokesman pronounced the testimony of faith and praised Allah as He deserves to be praised. He then said:
“‘To proceed: We are the supporters of Allah and the army of Islam. You Emigrants are only a smaller group.
“‘Yet a group from among your people has suddenly arrived, intending to separate us from our position and exclude us entirely from authority.’”
“When he fell silent, I intended to speak.
“I had prepared a speech that pleased me and wanted to deliver it before Abū Bakr spoke. I would sometimes try to moderate his gentleness with my firmness.
“When I was about to speak, Abū Bakr said, ‘Remain calm.’
“I disliked making him angry.”
“Abū Bakr then spoke. He was more forbearing and dignified than I.
“By Allah, he did not omit a single statement from the speech I had prepared and admired without expressing spontaneously something equal or superior to it. He then said:
“‘Whatever goodness you have mentioned concerning yourselves, you are indeed worthy of it.
“‘But the Arabs will recognize this authority only for this clan of Quraysh. They occupy the central position among the Arabs in lineage and homeland.
“‘I approve either of these two men for you, so pledge allegiance to whichever of them you wish.’”
“He took my hand and the hand of Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ, who was sitting between us.
“Nothing he said displeased me except that proposal.
“By Allah, I would rather be brought forward and have my neck struck—provided it did not bring me closer to sin—than become ruler over a people among whom was Abū Bakr.
“Unless, perhaps, my soul suggests something to me at the time of death that I do not feel now.”
“One of the Anṣār said, ‘I am its seasoned counselor and its honored, supported leader. Let there be one ruler from us and one ruler from you, people of Quraysh.’
“Clamor increased and voices rose until I feared that disagreement would occur.
“I said, ‘Extend your hand, Abū Bakr.’
“He extended his hand, and I pledged allegiance to him. The Emigrants then pledged allegiance to him, followed by the Anṣār.”
“We crowded around Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.
“One of them said, ‘You have killed Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.’
“I replied, ‘May Allah deal with Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah.’”
ʿUmar continued: “By Allah, under the circumstances confronting us, we found no course of action stronger than pledging allegiance to Abū Bakr.
“We feared that if we left the people without securing a pledge, they would pledge allegiance to one of their own after our departure.
“We would then either have to accept something with which we were not pleased or oppose them, resulting in disorder.”
“Therefore, whoever pledges allegiance to a man without consultation with the Muslims must not be followed, nor should the man to whom he pledged be followed, lest both expose themselves to being killed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Legal Punishments (6830) and Muslim in The Book of Legal Punishments (1691:15), from the report of al-Zuhrī through ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah from Ibn ʿAbbās. This wording is al-Bukhārī’s; Muslim narrated only the portion concerning stoning.
